\begin{document}
\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
This manuscript is an evidence-tiered research atlas built around the
symplectic polar space $W(3,3)$: the incidence geometry of totally isotropic
subspaces of $\PG(3,\FF_3)$ for a non-degenerate alternating form.  Its
collinearity graph is the symplectically constructed member of the $28$
non-isomorphic strongly regular graphs with parameters $\SRG(40,12,2,4)$;
the parameter tuple alone does not identify the graph.

The exact finite core includes the $40$-point/$240$-edge geometry, its
$\PSp(4,3)$ and $\PGSp(4,3)\cong W(E_6)$ symmetry actions, chain and code
constructions, exceptional-lattice comparison objects, and executable GAP
certificates.  The current frontier also records sharp negative results: the
natural minimal-logical-pair action has four sheets rather than a Weyl torsor;
the phase-compatible normalizer is $D_8$; and its marked comparison with
$N_{W(E_6)}(K)/K$ has exactly four choices, not a preferred state map.

The repository additionally contains conditional selection arguments and
phenomenological proposals involving gauge theory, particle data, and
cosmology.  They are retained as hypotheses or bridges, not as consequences
of finite geometry alone.  No result in this manuscript presently derives the
Standard Model, measured masses or couplings, spacetime dynamics, or
cosmology from $W(3,3)$ without additional physical assumptions.  The current
claims ledger and the explicit errata below govern the status of every such
statement; earlier chronological sections are preserved as a research record,
not silently promoted to theorem.
\end{abstract}

\readerguide{The current-claims ledger and the retraction record are the
       first places to look.}

\begin{plainbox}[What this document is, in one paragraph]
This is a research atlas, not a finished theory. It collects everything this project has
established about one finite geometric object --- the symplectic polar space $W(3,3)$, a
shape with forty points --- and about the exceptional structures $E_6$ and $E_8$ that keep
appearing when it is examined. Results are tiered by how strongly they are established:
some are proved, some are exhaustive machine checks over a stated range, some are
observations we have not explained. Each says which it is. Where a claim has been withdrawn
it stays on the page beside the measurement that overturned it, because a record that
quietly deletes its mistakes cannot be audited.
\end{plainbox}


\begin{center}\fbox{\parbox{0.92\textwidth}{%

sections were appended as work proceeded and were \emph{not} pruned when later
work overturned them. Several sections below therefore state results that have
since been \textbf{withdrawn}. Before relying on any section, consult the
\textbf{Errata} in ``Honest Synthesis and Errata'' at the end, and the full
ledger in \texttt{analysis/W33\_HONEST\_SYNTHESIS.md}

\begin{plainbox}
The corrections. Everything here was believed, written down, and then withdrawn,
with the measurement that overturned it. The attribution note at the top is the most
important: two results this project once claimed as its own were already published by other
people, and the entry names them. A record that quietly drops its mistakes cannot be
audited, which is why this section exists and why it is long.
\end{plainbox}

\smallskip
\begin{warnbox}[The two claims this project had to give back]
The note below is the single most consequential correction in this manuscript: two results
once presented here as ours were already published by other people, and in one case already
proved inside this repository before the passes that ``derived'' it. Both are withdrawn,
both name their true source, and the entry is kept rather than deleted --- a corpus that
silently drops its wrong attributions cannot be audited by anyone.
\end{warnbox}

\textbf{ATTRIBUTION --- READ FIRST (Passes 322--326).} An earlier version of this
box claimed the incidence rank law and the CSS family as results of this
programme. \textbf{Both claims were wrong, and are withdrawn.} The rank law is
\emph{published}, and was \emph{already proved in this repository} before the
passes that ``derived'' it:
\begin{itemize}\itemsep0pt
\item \textbf{Even $q$:} $\operatorname{rank}_2=\operatorname{Tr}(B^t)+1$ with
$B=\begin{psmallmatrix}4&2\\2&5\end{psmallmatrix}$ \textbf{is} Sastry--Sin,
\emph{The code of a regular generalized quadrangle of even order}: their
$r_n=1+\big(\tfrac{1+\sqrt{17}}2\big)^{2n}+\big(\tfrac{1-\sqrt{17}}2\big)^{2n}$
is the same formula, since $\big(\tfrac{1\pm\sqrt{17}}2\big)^2=\tfrac{9\pm\sqrt{17}}2$
are the eigenvalues of $B$.
\item \textbf{Odd $q$, cross characteristic:} $\tfrac12(q^2+1)(q+2)$ is the boxed,
\emph{algebraically proved} $\tfrac12\!\left(q(q+1)^2+2\right)$ of
\texttt{analysis/2026-07-10\_levi\_next5.md} (identical: both are
$\tfrac12(q^3{+}2q^2{+}q{+}2)$), which also boxes $\operatorname{rank}_2A_L=q^2+1$
--- the CSS family's $k$.
\item \textbf{Odd $q$, defining characteristic:} Chandler--Sin--Xiang,
\emph{J.\ Algebra} \textbf{323} (2010) 3157--3181 (arXiv:math/0603100), Thm.~1.1:
$\operatorname{rank}_p=1+\alpha_1^t+\alpha_2^t$,
$\alpha_{1,2}=\tfrac{p(p+1)^2}4\pm\tfrac{p(p+1)(p-1)}{12}\sqrt{17}$. This
\textbf{closes} what this manuscript called the last open gap: $\det(B_p)=
-\tfrac1{36}p^2(p+1)^2(2p^2{-}13p{+}2)$, giving $16,76,325$ at $p=2,3,5$, and
$\operatorname{Tr}=\tfrac12p(p+1)^2=\mathrm{char}_0(p)-1$. At $p=2$ the $\alpha_i$
\emph{are} the eigenvalues of $B$ --- which answers ``why $B$?''. It also confirms
the conjecture $\operatorname{rank}_3W(3,27)=8353$.
\item \textbf{The CSS code:} $[[40,10,4]]$ and its sentinel $[40,15,8]$ were in
\texttt{docs/index.html} before the pass that claimed them, from Passes 187/189,
which prove \emph{more}: $\mathbf F_2^{40}$ is uniserial with layers
$1|14|1|8|1|14|1$, $[40,15,8]$ is the \emph{unique} invariant $15$-space, and
$C^\perp\!/C$ is \emph{forced}. Moreover $k\cdot d=n$ is a \textbf{tautology}
($n$ is \emph{defined} as $(q+1)(q^2+1)$), and $d=q+1$ is established only at
$q=3$; for $q\ge5$ only $d\le q+1$ is proved. The honest family is
$[[(q+1)(q^2+1),\,q^2+1,\,\le q+1]]$.
\end{itemize}

\smallskip
\textbf{What is actually this programme's:} the \emph{selection layer} --- the
argument that $q=3$ is forced rather than assumed (the corpus elsewhere assumes
it). Both selections are \textbf{conditional}, each on one identification that is
assumed and not derived: Pass 225 assumes the shadow half-spinor \emph{is} a
Standard-Model generation ($2^{(q^2-1)/2}=16$ then has unique odd solution
$q=3$); Pass 227 assumes the magic resource must be an exceptional-group cubic
($\operatorname{rank}\le8$ then holds only at $q=3$) --- the QEC literature
requires no such thing, restoring universality with \emph{any} magic state. The
two assumptions differ, so the independence is real. The honest thesis is: \emph{if}
these identifications hold, $q=3$ is doubly forced. Also repo-specific are the
bound $d\le q+1$ (Pass 229) and Pass 332's explicit three-lattice lift of the
incidence $H_{10}$, with the standard ATLAS and exterior-algebra ingredients
cited. Attribution correction: $\mathbf Q(\sqrt6)$ predates Pass 298 in the May
18 and July 10 repo tracks; Pass 298 is a later forcing route, not ownership.

\smallskip
\textbf{THE THESIS, FINAL (Passes 346--347).} The obstruction below is
unconditional inside the finite model.  Its Standard-Model reading retains
the explicit Pass~225 identification above:
\begin{center}\emph{Under that identification, this substrate hosts a finite
carrier for the Standard Model's chirality and provably cannot select it.}\end{center}
Pass 333 certifies an integral involution $T$ with $\det T=-1$ generating the
substrate's own controller $\langle U_4(2),T\rangle=W(E_6)=\mathrm{PGSp}(4,3)$.
Three independent routes show $T$ \emph{exchanges} the half-spins $S^\pm$: the
outer swaps $5a\leftrightarrow5b$ (BT866's degree-$10$ $W(E_6)$ fusion), so
$\alpha(1{+}10a{+}5b)=1{+}10b{+}5a$; coefficient conjugation swaps the maximal
isotropics of $5a\oplus5a^*$, and half-spins are indexed by those families; and
$\det=-1$ is improper (Pin/Spin). Hence \textbf{no $\mathrm{PGSp}(4,3)$-invariant
can separate the two chiralities} --- and every datum built from the substrate is
invariant by construction. Under the Pass~225 identification, a generation is
\emph{one} chirality while the substrate supplies a symmetric \emph{pair}.
Selecting one would require breaking
$\mathrm{PGSp}(4,3)$: an input from \emph{outside}.

\smallskip
Equivalently, with the group theory removed: $W(E_6)$ is a \emph{reflection}
group, $\ker(\det)=U_4(2)$ has index two, and choosing a chirality is choosing
which coset is positive --- i.e.\ \emph{orienting} the $E_6$ root system.
\textbf{A reflection group contains its own orientation reversals, so it cannot
orient itself.} Pass 347 further shows the broken Eisenstein scalar (332), the
$\mathbf F_2$ type flip $\mathrm{minus}\to\mathrm{plus}$, and this chirality
choice are \emph{one binary act}: $L/2L$ is $\omega$-stable, hence $\mathbf F_4^5$
with traced-Hermitian type $(-1)^5=$ minus ($496$ isotropic), while
$H_{10}=L_i/2L_i$ sits on a leaf $\omega$ stabilises \emph{none} of --- which is
exactly what frees it to be plus ($528$, as certified). And $243=3^5=|d_{\mathbf
Q(\omega)}|^{5}$ is forced by restriction of scalars, while the reported
``odd vs alternating'' parity is an artefact of \emph{halving}
($\mathrm{Tr}\mapsto A_2$, even; $\tfrac12\mathrm{Tr}\mapsto$ norm, odd).

\smallskip
\textbf{LATEST INTEGRITY AUDIT (Passes 358--359).}  A fetched parallel packet
reintroduced several claims already contradicted by this manuscript.  The exact
GAP repair is
\[
 \PSp(4,3)\cong U_4(2)=W(E_6)^+,
 \quad \Sp(4,3)\cong2.U_4(2),
 \quad \PGSp(4,3)\cong U_4(2).2=W(E_6),
\]
so the two groups of order $51840$ are not isomorphic: the first is perfect
with centre $C_2$, while the reflection group has trivial centre and
abelianization $C_2$.  The ordinary Weil carrier is $9=5+4$, not $6+3$;
the unique signed-outer fusion gives the real envelope
$18=10+8\downarrow=(5_a+5_b)+(4_a+4_b)$.  At $p=5$ the CSX polynomial is
$x^2-90x+325$, with roots $45\pm10\sqrt{17}$, and
$\operatorname{ord}_{3089}(2)=772$, closing the purported $q=7$ exception.
Finally, GAP identifies the two degree-$68$ factors of $x^{137}-1$ as the
quadratic-residue/nonresidue pair, constructs orthogonal rank-$68$ CSS checks,
and gives $k=1$.  Applying the published exact QR distance
$[137,69,21]$ (Tjhai--Tomlinson--Ambroze--Ahmed, arXiv:0801.3926) yields
\[
 \boxed{[[137,1,21]]},
\]
not the unproved $[[137,1,3]]$ in the parallel packet.  This is a code theorem;
it does not derive the physical fine-structure constant.

\smallskip
\textbf{EXACT LOGICAL ACTION (Pass 360).}  Index the $137$ qubits by
$\mathbf F_{137}$.  GAP verifies on the actual QR/NQR row spaces that translation
and the residue multiplier $x\mapsto9x$ preserve both CSS check spaces, whereas
the primitive nonresidue multiplier $x\mapsto3x$ exchanges them.  Consequently
\[
 \widetilde P_{a,b}=\begin{cases}
 P_{a,b},&a\text{ quadratic-residue},\\
 P_{a,b}H^{\otimes137},&a\text{ quadratic-nonresidue}
 \end{cases}
\]
is a faithful Clifford action of
\[
 \widetilde{\operatorname{AGL}}(1,137)\cong C_{137}:C_{136},
 \qquad |\widetilde{\operatorname{AGL}}(1,137)|=18{,}632.
\]
Its pure-permutation kernel is $C_{137}:C_{68}$ of order $9{,}316$.  The
all-ones word lies in both normalizer codes but neither check space, has odd
self-overlap, and supplies $\bar X=X(\mathbf1)$, $\bar Z=Z(\mathbf1)$; hence
$P_{3,0}H^{\otimes137}$ acts as the exact logical Hadamard
$\bar X\leftrightarrow\bar Z$.

Adding the parity coordinate at infinity, the explicit Mobius maps
$x\mapsto x+1$, $x\mapsto9x$, and $x\mapsto-1/x$ preserve the chosen
$[138,69,22]$ extended QR code and generate doubly-transitive
$PSL(2,137)$ of order $1{,}285{,}608$, whose infinity stabilizer is the same
$C_{137}:C_{68}$.  Adjoining $x\mapsto3x$ gives the index-two
$PGL(2,137)$ envelope of order $2{,}571{,}216$; its outer coset exchanges the
two extended QR codes rather than preserving either separately.  The general
$PSL_2(p)$ containment is classical (Tjhai et al., arXiv:0801.3926), and the
permutation-plus-transversal-duality mechanism is fold-transversal
(Breuckmann--Burton, arXiv:2202.06647).  The contribution here is the explicit
$p=137$ stabilizer transport, logical representatives, and 37-check GAP
certificate---not a coupling-constant or hardware claim.

\smallskip
\textbf{CLIFFORD MAXIMALITY BOUNDARY (Pass 361).}  Put
$A=Q^\perp$ and $B=N^\perp$ for the two rank-$68$ check spaces.  A second GAP
certificate proves the nondegenerate orthogonal decomposition
\[
 \mathbf F_2^{137}=A\perp B\perp\langle\mathbf1\rangle,
 \qquad \dim(A,B,\langle\mathbf1\rangle)=(68,68,1),
\]
and the dual identities
$Q=B\oplus\langle\mathbf1\rangle$,
$N=A\oplus\langle\mathbf1\rangle$.  This makes the simple logical-gate
boundary exact.  Modulo Pauli phases a uniform one-qubit Clifford has one of
the six label maps in $GL(2,2)$.  Four send $X$ or $Z$ to $Y$ and would force a
nonzero permuted support into $A\cap B=0$; only identity and $X/Z$ swap survive.
Thus the uniform-local-Clifford-plus-permutation logical image is exactly
$\langle\bar H\rangle\cong C_2$.

Nor can a subset phase mask supply the missing map.  For
$D_m=\operatorname{diag}(m)$, the condition $D_mA\subseteq B$ gives
$68\cdot69=4{,}692$ binary constraints on $137$ mask bits; GAP finds rank
$137$ and nullity zero.  The reverse system $D_mB\subseteq A$ has the same
rank.  Hence no nonzero subset of odd powers of $S$ works, even when combined
with a pair-preserving or pair-swapping coordinate symmetry.  This no-go is
limited to the named simple lift class: general Clifford circuits, entangling
gates, ancillas, measurements, and code deformation remain outside its scope.

\smallskip
\textbf{TWO-BLOCK REAL-CLIFFORD DOUBLE SHADOW (Pass 362).}  The entangling
escape can now be decided exactly.  Two independent QR blocks give a
$[[274,2,21]]$ code with stabilizer rank $272$.  GAP acts on the actual
$548$-coordinate Pauli-label stabilizer and proves that the separately encoded
Hadamards and transversal CNOT preserve it.  On
$(x_1,x_2,z_1,z_2)$ their image is the full quadratic isometry group
\[
 O^+(4,2)\cong(S_3\times S_3):C_2,
 \qquad |O^+(4,2)|=72,
 \qquad [Sp(4,2):O^+(4,2)]=10.
\]
Thus the inter-block gate really escapes the one-block $C_2$ boundary without
supplying the still-absent complex phase gate.

Keeping matrix phases, the logical generators produce the real two-qubit
Clifford group $C_{\mathbb R}(2)$ of order $2{,}304$, with real Pauli normal
subgroup $2^{1+4}_+$ of order $32$ and quotient $O^+(4,2)$.  This one parent
has two nonisomorphic order-$1{,}152$ shadows:
\[
 1\to C_2\to C_{\mathbb R}(2)
   \to\operatorname{Aut}(K_{4,4})\to1,
 \qquad
 1\to W(F_4)\to C_{\mathbb R}(2)
   \xrightarrow{\text{total Hadamard parity}}C_2\to1.
\]
The projective quotient is
$2^4:O^+(4,2)\cong S_4\wr C_2=\operatorname{Aut}(K_{4,4})$ and has trivial
centre and $20$ conjugacy classes.  The parity kernel retains the global sign,
has centre $C_2$ and $25$ classes, and GAP identifies it with the independently
constructed Coxeter group $W(F_4)$.  Hence the familiar equality
$1{,}152=72\cdot16=|W(F_4)|=|\operatorname{Aut}(K_{4,4})|$ conceals a
quotient/kernel bifurcation, not an isomorphism.  Moreover $W(F_4)/Z$ is the
twisted index-two subgroup of $(S_4\times S_4):C_2$ generated by the side swap
and pairs $(\sigma,\rho)$ with
$\operatorname{sgn}\sigma=\operatorname{sgn}\rho$.

This also corrects BT159's order-only identification: its certified
order distribution
$(1,27,80,84,432,144,384)$ at orders
$(1,2,3,4,6,8,12)$ differs from the Coxeter $W(F_4)$ distribution
$(1,139,80,228,464,144,96)$.  BT159 constructed an $F_4$-sized
polarization normalizer, not a proved copy of $W(F_4)$.  The ambient real
Clifford definition and $O^+(2n,2)$ quotient are standard
(Hashagen et al., arXiv:1801.06121); the QR-137 stabilizer lift and the two
order-$1{,}152$ exact sequences are the result here.

\smallskip
\textbf{THE COMPLETE CHARACTER DIAMOND (Pass 363).}  The preceding two
shadows are part of a rigid three-object diagram.  GAP proves
\[
 C_{\mathbb R}(2)^{\rm ab}\cong C_2^2
\]
and constructs all three nonzero characters.  Their kernels have order
$1{,}152$, centre $C_2$, and common derived order $288$, but respectively
$25$, $34$, and $19$ conjugacy classes:
\[
 W(F_4),\qquad
 (2O\times2O)/\langle(-1,-1)\rangle,\qquad
 2_+^{1+4}:((C_3\times C_3):C_4).
\]
They are pairwise nonisomorphic; every pair intersects in
$C_{\mathbb R}(2)'$ and generates the parent.  Thus the formal
$S_3=\operatorname{Aut}(C_2^2)$ does not lift to a triality permuting the
kernels.  Projectively the three images are the two twisted index-two groups
$T_0,T_1$ and the base $S_4\times S_4$ inside
$\operatorname{Aut}(K_{4,4})$.

There is nevertheless a second exact action on the $48$ directions obtained
by radially normalizing the $F_4$ roots.  The determinant kernel is transitive
on this shell, with support pattern $8+24+16$, whereas $W(F_4)$ retains its
$24+24$ short/long orbits and alone preserves the metric root system.  This
also repairs an old notation-level error:
\[
 GL(2,3)=\operatorname{SmallGroup}(48,29)
 \not\cong 2O=\operatorname{SmallGroup}(48,28);
\]
the two groups have $13$ and $1$ involutions, respectively, despite their
common central quotient $S_4$.

\smallskip
\textbf{FOUR-BLOCK $E_8/2E_8$ REALIZATION (Pass 364).}  Four independent
QR blocks form a $[[548,4,21]]$ code with rank-$544$ stabilizer.  GAP acts on
all $1{,}096$ binary Pauli-label coordinates and verifies that four encoded
Hadamards and all twelve directed transversal CNOTs preserve that stabilizer.
On $(x_1,\ldots,x_4,z_1,\ldots,z_4)$ they generate
\[
 O^+(8,2),\qquad |O^+(8,2)|=348{,}364{,}800,\qquad
 [Sp(8,2):O^+(8,2)]=136.
\]
The explicit interleaving
\[
 (x_1,x_2,x_3,x_4,z_1,z_2,z_3,z_4)
 \longmapsto(x_1,z_1,x_2,z_2,x_3,z_3,x_4,z_4)
\]
preserves the quadratic and polar forms on all vectors and ordered pairs.
It therefore realizes, on encoded logical labels, the Pass-124 tower
\[
 \operatorname{SRG}(255,126,61,63)
 \supset
 \operatorname{SRG}(135,70,37,35)
 \sqcup
 \operatorname{SRG}(120,63,30,36).
\]
Pass 124 owns the abstract graph theorem; the new object is its physical
four-QR-block realization and the displayed chosen isometry.

\smallskip
\textbf{THREE-BLOCK MINUS REFINEMENT AND THE W33 SPREADS (Pass 365).}
In interleaved coordinates put
\[
 q_+(v)=x_1z_1+x_2z_2+x_3z_3,\qquad
 a=(0,0,0,0,1,1),\qquad
 q_-(v)=q_+(v)+B(a,v).
\]
The $36$ nonsingular vectors of $q_-$ form
$NO^-(6,2)=\operatorname{SRG}(36,15,6,6)$, and their transvections generate
\[
 O^-(6,2)\cong W(E_6),\qquad |O^-(6,2)|=51{,}840,
\]
with derived group $PSp(4,3)$ of order $25{,}920$.  GAP conjugates this
degree-$36$ derived action to the native action on the $36$ W33 spreads and
checks all pairs under the resulting explicit bijection:
\[
 |S_i\cap S_j|=4\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 B(v_{\phi(i)},v_{\phi(j)})=0.
\]
Relative to $q_+$ the nonsingular directions split as $16+20$: sixteen are
already real-nonsingular and twenty use the phase refinement.  On the
$[[411,3,21]]$ physical code the named rotation
\[
 \widehat R_{z_3}=\exp(-\pi i\bar Z_3/4),
 \qquad \bar Z_3=Z^{\otimes137},
\]
fixes every stabilizer row and sends
$\bar X_3\mapsto\bar X_3\bar Z_3$.  It is an exact encoded Clifford lift, but
is not asserted to be transversal, local, low-weight, or fault-tolerant.

\smallskip
\textbf{THE UNIFORM REFINEMENT TOWER (Pass 366).}  Direct sum gives, for
every positive $m$,
\[
 [[137m,m,21]],\qquad \operatorname{rank}S=136m,
\]
and the block Hadamards and CNOTs generate the standard real-Clifford label
group $O^+(2m,2)$.  GAP checks the physical ranks and full logical groups for
$m=1,2,3,4$, with orders
\[
 2, 72, 40{,}320, 348{,}364{,}800
\]
and symplectic indices $3,10,36,136$.  For
$q_a(v)=q(v)+B(a,v)$ it finds plus/minus refinement counts
\[
 (3,1),\ (10,6),\ (36,28),\ (136,120)
\]
and proves in the checked range the general identity
\[
 q_a\hbox{ is plus}\Longleftrightarrow q(a)=0,\qquad
 [Sp(2m,2):O^+(2m,2)]=2^{m-1}(2^m+1).
\]
At $m=4$ the full $Sp(8,2)$ action is transitive on the $136$ plus
refinements with an order-$348{,}364{,}800$ stabilizer, and separately on the
$120$ minus refinements.  The exceptional boundary matters:
$O^+(6,2)\cong S_8$ is not $W(E_6)$; the latter is the minus group of Pass
365.  At $m=4$, $O^+(8,2)\cong W(E_8)/\{\pm1\}$.

\smallskip
\textbf{UNIVERSAL PARITY PULLBACK AND NONSPLITTING (Pass 367).}  Four
index-two maps recur in the construction: $W(E_6)$ sign, outer-Weil parity,
the QR residue character, and real-Clifford Hadamard parity.  Their
synchronized fiber product $F$ fits into
\[
 1\to PSp(4,3)\times2.U_4(2)\times(C_{137}:C_{68})\times W(F_4)
 \to F\to C_2\to1
\]
and has order $28{,}841{,}108{,}255{,}539{,}200$.  The first, second, and
fourth factor extensions split.  The QR odd affine coset, however, contains
elements only of orders $8$ and $136$.  Hence no odd element of $F$ can be an
involution; GAP constructs one of order $8$, proving the lower bound sharp.
The four exchanges unify exactly as one grading, but not as one common
involutory gate.

\smallskip
\textbf{EXACT BOUNDARY DECODER (Pass 373).}  Returning to the ternary W33
edge complex, GAP proves that the triangle-boundary image itself is the
classical code
\[
 B_1=\operatorname{im}\partial_2=[240,120,3]_3.
\]
The parity matrix has 240 nonzero, projectively distinct columns, excluding
weights one and two, while every oriented triangle supplies a weight-three
word.  Hence all 480 nonzero one-edge errors have different syndromes and the
complete radius-one maximum-likelihood table has exactly 481 entries.  This
does not replace the already-owned logical quotient theorem: the canonical CSS
code remains $[[240,81,3]]_3$ with $(d_X,d_Z)=(3,4)$.

\smallskip
\textbf{NATURAL PHASE-SHEET ACTION (Pass 374).}  BT571/BT637/BT644 already
own the four $\mathbb F_3^\times\!\times\mathbb F_3^\times$ scalar lifts over
each of the 12960 projective minimal-pair incidences, together with their
$25920+25920$ phase split and deck signs.  The missing equivariant question is
now decided: the signed edge-chain action of $PSp(4,3)$ and
$PGSp(4,3)\cong W(E_6)$ has four orbits of size 12960 on the 51840 nonzero
minimal logical $X/Z$ vector pairings.  Their stabilizers are respectively
$C_2$ and $C_2\times C_2$.  Thus the natural full action is four copies of
$W(E_6)/(C_2\times C_2)$, not a regular $W(E_6)$ torsor; a regular lift would
require additional phase transport not supplied by W33 collineations.

\smallskip
\textbf{PHASE-CHARACTER NORMALIZER OBSTRUCTION (Pass 375).}  Let
$D=(\mathbb F_3^\times)^2\cong V_4$ be the scalar deck and retain the
BT644 character $\chi(a,b)=ab$.  GAP gives
\[
 \operatorname{Aut}(D)=S_3,
 \qquad \operatorname{Stab}_{\operatorname{Aut}(D)}(\ker\chi)=C_2.
\]
Thus $D{:}C_3\cong A_4$ and $D{:}S_3\cong S_4$ exist for the bare group,
but the order-three automorphism cycles the three nonzero characters.  On the
four scalar lifts the unrestricted normalizer is $S_4$, whereas the setwise
stabilizer of the fixed $2+2$ phase partition is exactly $D_8$, with no
order-three element.  Independently, if $K\cong V_4$ is the actual Pass-374
stabilizer, then
\[
 |N_{W(E_6)}(K)|=32,
 \qquad N_{W(E_6)}(K)/K\cong D_8.
\]
These are isomorphic normalizer quotients on different objects; no intertwiner
is asserted.  The split enlargement $W(E_6)\times D$ does not repair the
torsor: its explicit coset stabilizer has index $51840$, but a regular
complement would force a subgroup of order $12960$ in $W(E_6)$, and GAP's 350
subgroup classes contain none.

\smallskip
\textbf{MARKED \(D_8\) BRIDGE WITH FOURFOLD AMBIGUITY (Pass 376).}  The
previous boundary can be sharpened at the level of marked group extensions.
The phase pair and the geometric pair are isomorphic as
\[
 \bigl(D_8,\;D=(\mathbb F_3^\times)^2\cong V_4\bigr)
 \cong
 \bigl(N_{W(E_6)}(K)/K,\;C_N(K)/K\cong V_4\bigr).
\]
Both quotients by their marked $V_4$ are $C_2$, both conjugation actions have
orbit profile $1+1+2$, and the fixed phase-character kernel is the central
$C_2$, which maps to $Z(N_{W(E_6)}(K)/K)$.  GAP constructs such a marked
isomorphism, but finds exactly four of them, with residual ambiguity
$C_2\times C_2$.  Thus this is a central-line correspondence, not a preferred
map from scalar sheets to geometric states and not a regular Weyl action.

\smallskip
\textbf{BINARY \(Q_3\) SWITCH-HEADER CLOCK (Pass 377).}  The ``Q3 XOR''
register in BT828 is the low three \emph{binary} address bits, not a ternary
parity gate.  GAP follows all $3\cdot3\cdot40=360$ directed one-axis toggles
through the existing header formula and obtains exactly $48$ of the $192$
tomotope flags, with pairwise-disjoint axis supports
\[
  48=24+12+12.
\]
The fiber profile is $24\cdot5+24\cdot10=360$, and depth increment acts on
this header plane by the free $C_3$ shift $f\mapsto f+64\pmod {192}$, giving
$48=16\cdot3$.  This builds a finite header-address control plane between a
binary switch bank and the Q6 address bus.  It neither identifies a binary
toggle with a Q6 edge traversal nor asserts an analogue oscillator, hardware
switch, or physical interpretation.

\smallskip
\textbf{HEADER/SCHEDULER \(C_3\) FACTORIZATION OBSTRUCTION (Pass 378).}
The Pass-377 header plane and the $48$ BT1406 pulse positions are both bare
free $C_3$-sets with sixteen orbits.  Consequently abstract equivariant
bijections exist, but there are
\[
  16!\,3^{16}=900657498850357248000
\]
of them.  This abundance is precisely the absence of a natural identification.
More sharply, the scheduler preserves its \texttt{tomotope\_flag} throughout
each \texttt{LOAD/FLIP/LATCH} phase triple while the header action moves every
flag.  No $C_3$-equivariant map to the header plane can therefore factor through
the actual scheduler flag label.  On the live stress route the two flag sets
meet only in $\{112,144\}$; fourteen header cycles have no stress flag and two
have one.  The common $16\cdot3$ shape is a noncanonical set-type coincidence,
not an edge, flag, or state intertwiner.

\smallskip
\textbf{HEADER CLOCK / \(Q_6\) GEOMETRY BOUNDARY (Pass 379).}  There remains
one independently checkable temptation: interpreting the arithmetic depth
shift itself as a $Q_6$ edge operation.  GAP transports
$f\mapsto f+64\pmod {192}$ through BT1371's current pinned $192$-row
flag-to-$Q_6$-edge table and tests adjacency in the $Q_6$ line graph.  Of its
$960=64\binom{6}{2}$ adjacent edge pairs, only $146$ remain adjacent; $814$ are
lost, exactly balanced by $814$ false positives.  The first witness takes the
adjacent flag pair $\{0,8\}$ to $\{64,72\}$, whose assigned edges have no
common endpoint.  Thus the finite header $C_3$ is a valid control-address
clock but, through this table, not a $Q_6$ line-graph automorphism, cube-edge
operation, or state traversal.  This is table-specific: an explicitly built
different intertwiner is not ruled out, but none is supplied by the arithmetic
clock alone.

\smallskip
\textbf{MINIMAL SCHEDULER PHASE LIFT (Pass 380).}  The correct scheduler
repair is now exact.  To retain the sixteen live \texttt{tomotope\_flag}
identities while making phase rotation free requires at least
$16\cdot3=48$ labels; the existing pair
$(\texttt{tomotope\_flag},\texttt{phase\_trit})$ attains that minimum.  Its
canonical equivariant full-bus lift is
\[
  \iota(f,p)=f+64p\pmod {192}.
\]
On the live stress body this 48-set meets the Pass-377 header plane in only
six flags, exactly the two complete cycles
\[
  [16,80,144],\qquad [48,112,176].
\]
They anchor scheduler flags $144$ and $112$; fourteen scheduler and fourteen
header cycles remain unbound.  Although two oriented anchors reduce the bare
ambiguity, they still leave
\[
  14!\,3^{14}=416971064282572800
\]
equivariant extensions.  Thus the exact missing compiler object is a typed
sixteen-row header-orbit binding table with phase offsets.  The phase lift is
not a newly discovered Q6 symmetry or a physical pulse map.

\smallskip
\textbf{EXPLICIT HEADER/SCHEDULER ABI AND REVERSIBLE CONTROLLER (Passes
381--382).}  The required compiler object is now supplied as reviewed
configuration, with one row
\[
(\texttt{edge\_step},\texttt{tomotope\_flag},
  \texttt{header\_cycle\_rep},\texttt{phase\_offset})
\]
per scheduler orbit.  Its checked compiler rule is
\[
 c(e,p)=r_e+64(p+o_e)\pmod {192}.
\]
Two rows retain the genuine Pass-380 full-bus anchors; the remaining fourteen
are deliberately named \texttt{reviewed\_external\_binding}.  GAP verifies a
bijection from all $48$ live pulse positions onto the Pass-377 header plane,
the inverse audit trace, and every phase step.  This is executable ABI input,
not a derivation from $Q_6$ geometry.  On the same $16\cdot3$ state type,
Pass~382 builds the isolated reversible
\texttt{LOAD/FLIP/LATCH} controller.  Its successor is one $48$-cycle; the
free phase rotation agrees on the $32$ load/flip states but differs at all
$16$ latches.  Thus a phase relabelling is not silently a controller advance.
(Witnesses:
\path{analysis/w33_pass381_explicit_header_binding_abi.g} and
\path{analysis/w33_pass382_reversible_logic_switch_controller.g}.)

\smallskip
\textbf{BRANCH/PHASE AND COORDINATE-FOLD BOUNDARIES (Passes 383--384).}
On the typed space
\[
 \{0,1\}_{\mathrm{branch}}\times\{0,\ldots,15\}_{\mathrm{row}}
 \times\mathbb Z/3_{\mathrm{phase}},
\]
the supplied orientation-preserving branch switch commutes with phase and
therefore gives $C_2\times C_3\cong C_6$.  An $S_3$ action needs an extra
phase-reflecting involution; the binding table does not manufacture one.
Separately, GAP enumerates all $540$ strict surjective coordinate folds % {540:unrelated}
$\mathbb F_2^6\to\mathbb F_2^3$.  None intertwines the live BT1371 direction
table with the Pass-377 header axes on all $48$ flags.  The six folds that
match the live $[3,2,2,3,4,2]\to[8,4,4]$ usage profile form one candidate
orbit, while the header-depth direction relation generates $S_6$.  Thus the
current coordinate class does not canonize the ABI.  This does not prohibit a
different address table or a separately supplied non-coordinate compiler map.
(Witnesses:
\path{analysis/w33_pass383_branch_phase_control_group.g} and
\path{analysis/w33_pass384_q6_q3_fold_obstruction.g}.)

\smallskip
\textbf{ORBIT-ANCHOR OBSTRUCTION (Pass 385).}  The header quotient has two
intrinsic eight-class orbits, whereas the live sixteen-edge stress path has
trivial setwise stabilizer both in the BT1371 group and in the full $Q_6$ edge
automorphism group.  The two Pass-380 anchors land in the same header orbit
but in opposite BT1371 orbit colors.  Consequently no binding that preserves
both inherited partitions can retain both canonical anchors.  This is exactly
why the Pass-381 crosswalk is an explicit ABI boundary: any complete binding
must relax an inherited partition or abandon an anchor orientation.  It is a
finite control-plane result, not a hardware or oscillator no-go.  (Witness:
\path{analysis/w33_pass385_header_stress_orbit_anchor_obstruction.g}.)

\smallskip
\textbf{What fell:} every \emph{metric} or \emph{basis-dependent} geometric claim
(five retractions, none from the spectral side, Pass 311) --- \emph{and} both
headline theorems, to prior art. Before claiming any result here is new, consult
\texttt{RESULTS\_INDEX.md}, which maps result~$\to$~file; this corpus is indexed
by \emph{date}, not topic, so a topic search cannot find its own proofs.
}}\end{center}

%  PART I: FOUNDATIONS
% ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
\part{Foundations: From a Single Equation to a Unique Geometry}

\section{The Master Equation}
\label{sec:master}

The entire theory rests on a single Diophantine equation.

\begin{theorem}[Uniqueness of $q=3$]
\label{thm:uniqueness}
The equation
\begin{equation}
\boxed{q! = 2q}
\label{eq:master}
\end{equation}
has a unique positive-integer solution: $q = 3$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For $q=1$: $1!=1\neq 2$.
For $q=2$: $2!=2\neq 4$.
For $q=3$: $3!=6 = 2\times 3$. \checkmark

For $q\geq 4$: the ratio $q!/(2q) = (q-1)!/2 \geq 3 > 1$, so $q!>2q$.
Hence $q=3$ is the unique solution. \qedhere
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The SRG Quadratic]
\label{def:quadratic}
From $q=3$, define the \emph{SRG quadratic} whose roots yield the
graph regularity parameters $\lambda$ and $\mu$:
\begin{equation}
x^2 - q!\,x + 2^q = 0 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
x^2 - 6x + 8 = 0 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
(x-2)(x-4) = 0
\label{eq:quadratic}
\end{equation}
with discriminant $(q!)^2 - 4\cdot 2^q = 36 - 32 = 4$ (a perfect square).
The roots are $\lambda = 2$ and $\mu = 4$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Parameter Closure]
\label{prop:params}
From $q$, $\lambda$, $\mu$ alone, every graph constant is determined:
\begin{equation}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{array}{rcl@{\qquad}rcl}
k &=& 2^q + q + 1 = 12 &
v &=& \dfrac{k(k-\lambda-1)}{\mu} + k + 1 = 40 \\[6pt]
r &=& \lambda = 2 &
s &=& -\mu = -4 \\[4pt]
f &=& 24 \;\text{(mult.\ of $r$)} &
g &=& 15 \;\text{(mult.\ of $s$)} \\[4pt]
\Phithree &=& q^2+q+1 = 13 &
\Phisix &=& q^2-q+1 = 7 \\[4pt]
\Phitwelve &=& q^4-q^2+1 = 73 &
\Theta &=& q^2+1 = 10 \\[4pt]
E &=& \tfrac{1}{2}vk = 240 &
T &=& \tfrac{1}{6}vk\lambda = 160 \\[4pt]
\Neff &=& \tbinom{k-1}{2} = 55 &
z &=& (k-1)+\mu\,i = 11+4i
\end{array}
\label{eq:params}
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}


% ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
\section{\texorpdfstring{The Symplectic Polar Space $W(3,3)$}{The Symplectic Polar Space W(3,3)}}
\label{sec:W33}

This section gives the formal mathematical definition of $W(3,3)$
as a \emph{symplectic polar space}---a classical object in
finite geometry, studied by Tits, Payne, Thas, and others since the
1960s.

\begin{definition}[Symplectic Polar Space $W(2n{-}1,q)$]
\label{def:polar}
Let $V = \FF_q^{2n}$ be a $2n$-dimensional vector space over the
finite field $\FF_q$, and let
\begin{equation}
\omega\colon V \times V \longrightarrow \FF_q
\label{eq:form}
\end{equation}
be a \textbf{non-degenerate alternating bilinear form}
(i.e., $\omega(u,u)=0$ for all $u\in V$, and $\omega$ has trivial
radical).  A subspace $S\leq V$ is \textbf{totally isotropic} if
$\omega(u,v)=0$ for all $u,v\in S$.  The \textbf{symplectic polar space}
\begin{equation}
\boxed{W(2n{-}1,\,q)}
\end{equation}
is the incidence geometry whose \emph{points} are the $1$-dimensional
totally isotropic subspaces of $\PG(2n{-}1,\FF_q)$, and whose
\emph{lines} are the $2$-dimensional totally isotropic subspaces---i.e.\
those projective lines that are entirely contained in the polar space.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}
Since $\omega$ is alternating, \emph{every} vector $v$ satisfies
$\omega(v,v)=0$, so every $1$-subspace is isotropic.
The points of $W(2n{-}1,q)$ are therefore \emph{all} points of
$\PG(2n{-}1,q)$.  The constraint falls on lines:
only those projective lines $\ell$ with $\omega(u,v)=0$ for all
$u,v\in\ell$ qualify.
\end{remark}

\begin{construction}[$W(3,3)$ in Coordinates]
\label{con:w33}
Take $n=2$, $q=3$, so $V = \FF_3^4$.  Choose the standard
symplectic basis $\{e_1,e_2,f_1,f_2\}$ with
\begin{equation}
\omega(u,v) = u_1 v_3 - u_3 v_1 + u_2 v_4 - u_4 v_2
\label{eq:sympl-form}
\end{equation}
or equivalently, in matrix form,
\begin{equation}
\Omega = \begin{pmatrix} 0 & I_2 \\ -I_2 & 0 \end{pmatrix}
\quad\text{so that}\quad
\omega(u,v) = u^{\!\top}\Omega\, v.
\label{eq:omega-matrix}
\end{equation}
The \textbf{points} of $W(3,3)$ are the $40$ points of $\PG(3,\FF_3)$:
\begin{equation}
|W(3,3)| = \frac{q^4-1}{q-1} = \frac{80}{2} = 40 = v.
\end{equation}
A projective line $\langle u,v\rangle$ is an \textbf{isotropic line} iff
$\omega(u,v)\equiv 0 \pmod{3}$.  There are exactly $40$ such lines,
each containing $q+1=4=\mu$ points.
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[$W(3,3)$ is a Generalized Quadrangle]
\label{thm:gq}
The symplectic polar space $W(3,3)$ is a generalized quadrangle
$\GQ(3,3)$---a rank-$2$ polar space satisfying the Payne--Thas axioms:
\begin{enumerate}[nosep,label=\upshape(\roman*)]
\item Every line contains exactly $s+1 = q+1 = 4$ points.
\item Every point lies on exactly $t+1 = q+1 = 4$ lines.
\item For any point $p$ not on a line $\ell$, there is a
  \textbf{unique} point $p'\in\ell$ collinear with $p$
  (and a unique line joining $p$ to $p'$).
\end{enumerate}
The equality $s=t=q=3$ gives equal point and line counts and identical
strongly regular parameters on the two concurrence graphs, but it does
\emph{not} imply incidence self-duality.  For odd $q$, the dual of the
symplectic quadrangle $W(3,q)$ is the nonisomorphic parabolic quadrangle
$Q(4,q)$; at $q=3$ the halves are spectrally paired but geometrically
non-swappable (Theorem~\ref{thm:pass173-dark-lattices}).
\end{theorem}


\subsection{\texorpdfstring{The Collinearity Graph: $\SRG(40,12,2,4)$}{The Collinearity Graph: SRG(40,12,2,4)}}
\label{ssec:srg}

\begin{definition}[Collinearity Graph]
\label{def:coll}
The \textbf{collinearity graph} $\Gamma$ of $W(3,3)$ has
vertex set $=$ points of $W(3,3)$
and edge set $=$ pairs of distinct collinear points
(i.e., points sharing an isotropic line).
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Strongly Regular Parameters]
\label{thm:srg}
The collinearity graph of $\GQ(s,t)$ is strongly regular with parameters
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\bigl((s+1)(st+1),\; s(t+1),\; s-1,\; t+1\bigr).}
\label{eq:gq-to-srg}
\end{equation}
For $s=t=q=3$:
\begin{equation}
(v,k,\lambda,\mu) = (40,\,12,\,2,\,4) = \SRG(40,12,2,4).
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Every point has $s(t+1) = 12$ collinear neighbours (on $t+1=4$ lines,
each contributing $s=3$ new points).
Two adjacent vertices lie on a common line; besides themselves, the line
contains $s-1=2$ further points, all adjacent to both---giving
$\lambda = s-1 = 2$.
Two non-adjacent vertices $p,p'$ satisfy the GQ axiom: each of the
$t+1=4$ lines on $p'$ produces a unique point collinear to $p$
(and vice versa), so $\mu = t+1 = 4$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Spence Enumeration, 2000]
There are exactly $\mathbf{28}$ non-isomorphic $\SRG(40,12,2,4)$ graphs,
and $28 = \binom{8}{2} = \dim\,\SO(8)$.
The graph used here is identified by the displayed symplectic polar-space
construction, not by the four SRG parameters or by group order alone.  Its
full automorphism group is
$\operatorname{Aut}(\PSp(4,3))\cong\PGSp(4,3)$ of order $51\,840$; the
normal inner projective subgroup $\PSp(4,3)$ has order $25\,920$.
\end{corollary}

\subsection{Bose--Mesner Algebra}

The adjacency matrix $A$ of $\SRG(v,k,\lambda,\mu)$ satisfies:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{A^2 = (k-\mu)\,I + (\lambda-\mu)\,A + \mu\,J}
\label{eq:bose-mesner}
\end{equation}
where $J$ is the all-ones matrix.  For $W(3,3)$:
\begin{equation}
A^2 = 8I - 2A + 4J.
\label{eq:bm-w33}
\end{equation}

Eigenvalues and multiplicities:
\begin{equation}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{array}{c|ccc}
\text{eigenvalue} & k=12 & r=\lambda=2 & s=-\mu=-4 \\
\hline
\text{multiplicity} & 1 & f=24 & g=15
\end{array}
\end{equation}
with $r+s = \lambda-\mu = -2$ and $r\cdot s = \mu-k = -8 = -2^q$.


\subsection{Spectral Decomposition and Trace Identities}

\begin{equation}
A = k\,P_k + r\,P_r + s\,P_s, \qquad
P_k+P_r+P_s = I_{v\times v}.
\end{equation}

\begin{align}
\Tr(A^0) &= v = 40 \\
\Tr(A^1) &= 0 \\
\Tr(A^2) &= k^2 + f\,r^2 + g\,s^2 = 144+96+240 = 480 = vk \\
\Tr(A^3) &= k^3 + f\,r^3 + g\,s^3 = 1728+192-960 = 960 = 6T
\end{align}

\begin{theorem}[Energy Equipartition --- Specific to $W(3,3)$]
\label{thm:equi}
\begin{equation}
\boxed{f\cdot\Theta = g\cdot\lambda^\mu = E = 240}
\end{equation}
i.e., $24\times 10 = 15\times 16 = 240$.
This identity holds for \emph{no other} strongly regular graph.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{The Exact Shifted Cubic and the Reversible Complement Switch}
\label{sec:mastercubic}

The shifted adjacency operator
\[
   D:=A-I
\]
is canonical for a strongly regular graph with $\lambda=2$: it is the
half-intersection shift $A-\lambda I/2$, the unique scalar shift for which
the edge term disappears after squaring.

\begin{theorem}[Exact shifted cubic]\label{thm:mastercubic}
On the full $40$-point carrier, $D=A-I$ has spectrum
\[
  11^{\,1}\oplus 1^{\,24}\oplus(-5)^{\,15}
\]
and minimal polynomial
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mastercubic}
  \boxed{(t-11)(t-1)(t+5)=0.}
\end{equation}
Consequently every $D$-equivariant subspace is a sum of the
$1$-, $24$-, and $15$-dimensional eigenspaces.  In particular, there is no
$32$-dimensional $D$-invariant restriction of the point carrier.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The spectrum is obtained from the adjacency spectrum
$12^1\oplus2^{24}\oplus(-4)^{15}$ by subtracting $1$.  The three
eigenvalues are distinct, so the displayed product is the minimal
polynomial.  Since the point representation is multiplicity-free, its
invariant subspaces are direct sums of these three eigenspaces.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lossless even/complement switch]\label{thm:specdem}
Let $H:=D^2$ and let $\overline A:=J-I-A$ be the complement adjacency.
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lossless-complement-switch}
  \boxed{H=13I+4\overline A,\qquad
  288D=H^2-98H+385I.}
\end{equation}
Thus the off-diagonal support of $D$ is the $240$ unordered collinear
point pairs, while the off-diagonal support of $H$ is the
$540$ unordered noncollinear point pairs
\texttt{\{540:point-nonedge\}}.  Squaring loses no spectral channel:
\[
  \mathbb Q[D^2]=\mathbb Q[D].
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Substituting $A=D+I$ into $A^2=8I-2A+4J$ gives
$D^2=9I-4A+4J=13I+4\overline A$.  On the three eigenspaces of $D$,
$H$ has distinct eigenvalues $121,1,25$.  Interpolation at these three
values gives
\[
  D=\frac{H^2-98H+385I}{288},
\]
which proves both recovery and equality of the generated algebras.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Exact spectral determinant]
The characteristic spectral determinant of the full shifted carrier is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Zdef}
  Z_D(x):=\det(I-xD)=(1-11x)(1-x)^{24}(1+5x)^{15}.
\end{equation}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[True trace tower]\label{thm:trace-tower}
The logarithmic derivative of $Z_D$ generates the power traces:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:trace-gen}
  -x\,\frac{d}{dx}\log Z_D(x)
  =\sum_{n\ge1}\Tr(D^n)x^n,
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}\label{eq:trace-formula}
  \boxed{\Tr(D^n)=11^n+24+15(-5)^n.}
\end{equation}
In particular,
\[
  \Tr(D)=-40,\quad
  \Tr(D^2)=520,\quad
  \Tr(D^3)=-520,\quad
  \Tr(D^4)=24040.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Positive heat and zeta data]
The positive operator is $H=D^2$, not the indefinite operator $D$.
Its heat trace and two unambiguous zeta functions are
\[
\Tr(e^{-tH})=e^{-121t}+24e^{-t}+15e^{-25t},
\]
\[
\zeta_{|D|}(s)=11^{-s}+24+15\,5^{-s},\qquad
\zeta_H(s)=121^{-s}+24+15\,25^{-s}.
\]
Moreover
\[
e^{-tH}=e^{-13t}e^{-4t\overline A},
\]
so positive heat propagation is exactly complement-graph propagation,
while \eqref{eq:lossless-complement-switch} recovers the signed operator.
\end{remark}

\section{\texorpdfstring{The Symplectic Group $\Sp(4,3)$ and its Exceptional Isomorphisms}{The Symplectic Group Sp(4,3) and its Exceptional Isomorphisms}}
\label{sec:sp}

\begin{definition}[Symplectic Group]
$\Sp(4,3)$ is the group of $4\times 4$ matrices over $\FF_3$
preserving the alternating form~$\omega$:
\begin{equation}
\Sp(4,3) = \{M\in\mathrm{GL}(4,\FF_3) : M^\top\Omega\,M = \Omega\}.
\end{equation}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Order Computation]
\begin{equation}
|\Sp(4,3)| = q^{n^2}\prod_{i=1}^{n}(q^{2i}-1)\bigg|_{\substack{n=2\\q=3}}
= 3^4\cdot(3^2-1)(3^4-1) = 81\cdot 8\cdot 80 = 51\,840.
\label{eq:sp-order}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Factorisation through Graph Parameters]
\label{thm:sp-factor}
\begin{equation}
|\Sp(4,3)| = q^4\cdot\lambda^2\cdot\mu^2\cdot\Theta
= 81\cdot 4\cdot 16\cdot 10 = 51\,840.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
$q^2-1 = (q-1)(q+1) = \lambda\mu = 8$, and
$q^4-1 = (q^2-1)(q^2+1) = \lambda\mu\Theta = 80$, so
$|\Sp(4,3)| = q^4\cdot\lambda\mu\cdot\lambda\mu\Theta
= q^4(\lambda\mu)^2\Theta$. \qedhere
\end{proof}

\subsection{Exceptional Isomorphisms}

\begin{theorem}[The corrected $W(E_6)$ group ledger]
\label{thm:e6}
\begin{equation}
\boxed{|\Sp(4,3)| = |\PGSp(4,3)| = |W(E_6)|
 = 2^7\cdot 3^4\cdot 5 = 51\,840}
\end{equation}
but equality of orders must not be confused with isomorphism.  The exact
identifications are
\begin{equation}
\PSp(4,3)\cong U_4(2)\cong W(E_6)^+,
\qquad
\PGSp(4,3)\cong U_4(2){:}2\cong W(E_6),
\end{equation}
whereas $\Sp(4,3)\cong 2.U_4(2)$ has center $C_2$ and its projective
point action has image only $\PSp(4,3)$.  Thus the faithful full graph
group is the centerless projective similitude extension, not the symplectic
matrix-group cover.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The Deep Parametric Identity]
\label{thm:deep}
\begin{equation}
\boxed{|\Sp(4,3)| = v\cdot k\cdot(v-k-1)\cdot\mu
= 40\cdot 12\cdot 27\cdot 4 = 51\,840}
\label{eq:deep}
\end{equation}
where $v-k-1 = 27 = q^3$ is the dimension of the fundamental
representation of $E_6$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[$B_2 = C_2$ Isomorphism]
\label{thm:b2c2}
$\mathfrak{sp}(4)\cong\mathfrak{so}(5)$, with
$\dim\,\mathfrak{sp}(4) = n(2n+1)|_{n=2} = 10 = \Theta$.
The orthogonal group $O(5,3)$ on the parabolic quadric $Q(4,3)$ is
isomorphic to $\Sp(4,3)$, and $Q(4,3)$ is the \textbf{dual} generalized
quadrangle of $W(3,3)$.
\end{theorem}


\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$D_4$ Triality and the 28 Graphs}{D4 Triality and the 28 Graphs}}

\begin{theorem}
The Lie algebra $D_4 = \mathfrak{so}(8)$ is the unique simple Lie algebra
admitting an order-$3$ outer automorphism (\textbf{triality}).
\begin{equation}
28 = \dim\,\SO(8) = \binom{2^q}{2}, \qquad
\mathrm{rank}(D_4) = 4 = \mu, \qquad
|D_4\text{ roots}| = 24 = f.
\end{equation}
Triality exchanges $\mathbf{8}_v\leftrightarrow\mathbf{8}_s
\leftrightarrow\mathbf{8}_c$, providing the three fermion families.
\end{theorem}


\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$E_8$ and the Edge Count}{E8 and the Edge Count}}

\begin{theorem}[$E_8$ Root System]
\begin{equation}
\boxed{E = \tfrac{1}{2}vk = 240 = |\mathrm{Roots}(E_8)|}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Local-axis glue lift]
\label{thm:axis-glue-e8-lift}
The object-level $W_{3,3}\to E_8$ carrier is the set of endpoints of the
$120=40\cdot3$ local pencil-octahedron axes.  At a point $p$, either endpoint
of an axis is a pair of pencil lines $\{L_i,L_j\}$ and determines the
weight-$6$ word
\[
 h(p;L_i,L_j)=(L_i\cup L_j)\setminus\{p\}\in C^\perp.
\]
The opposite endpoint differs from it by the neighborhood word $N(p)\in C$.
Consequently the $120$ axes give exactly the $120$ anisotropic classes in
$C^\perp/C$, and an explicit isometry of plus-type quadratic spaces gives
\[
 \{\text{local axes}\}\;\cong\;
 \{x\in C^\perp/C:Q(x)=1\}\;\cong\;\Phi(E_8)/\{\pm1\}.
\]
The axis-pairing graph equals the $E_8$ root-line orthogonality graph
entrywise and has parameters $\SRG(120,63,30,36)$.  Choosing a chamber lifts
the two endpoints of every axis to the two signs of its root, recovering all
$240$ roots with their exact Gram products.
\end{theorem}

\noindent
This refines the count identity above without promoting it into a false edge
dictionary.  The $240$-element carrier is
$40$ points $\times\,3$ local axes $\times\,2$ endpoints, not the set of
$240$ global graph edges: the previously verified line-graph degree and
$W(E_6)$-orbit obstructions to a naive edge/root bijection remain active.
The executable certificate is
\texttt{w33\_pass123\_axis\_glue\_e8\_lift.py}.

\begin{theorem}[Two nonconjugate $W(E_6)$ embeddings]
\label{thm:two-we6-embeddings}
The code automorphism action
$\mathrm{PGSp}(4,3)\cong W(E_6)\curvearrowright C^\perp/C$ is faithful and has orbit
decomposition
\[
 256=1+135+120,
\]
with one zero class, $135$ nonzero isotropic classes, and $120$ anisotropic
classes.  The ordered-anisotropic-pair stabilizer of Pass~117 is a
nonconjugate copy of $W(E_6)$: its isotropic orbit sizes are
$27,36,36,36$, and its anisotropic orbit sizes are
$1,1,1,27,27,27,36$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Pass~125 generates the $40$-point projective similitude group from five
symplectic transvections and one nonsquare similitude, enumerates its order
$51840$, transports every generator through the W33 binary code, and
enumerates a faithful quotient image of the same order.  Its measured orbits
are $1,135,120$.  The Pass~117 fingerprints are different; conjugate
subgroups have identical orbit-size multisets in the same ambient
permutation action.
\end{proof}

Thus ``the $W(E_6)$ action'' is embedding-dependent.  The code embedding
exposes the coarse W33 quadratic split, while the ordered-pair embedding
exposes the finer $E_8\to E_6\times A_2$ branching.  Transitivity of the
containing orthogonal group alone proves neither statement.

All five exceptional Lie algebra dimensions are graph-parametric:
\begin{equation}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{array}{rclcl}
\dim(G_2) &=& \lambda\Phisix = 14 && \text{rank } \lambda = 2 \\
\dim(F_4) &=& v+k = 52 && \text{rank } \mu = 4 \\
\dim(E_6) &=& \lambda q\Phithree = 78 && \text{rank } q! = 6 \\
\dim(E_7) &=& \Phisix(k+\Phisix) = 133 && \text{rank } \Phisix = 7 \\
\dim(E_8) &=& E + 2^q = 248 && \text{rank } 2^q = 8
\end{array}
\label{eq:exceptional}
\end{equation}

\section{Payne-Derived Geometry and Substructures}
\label{sec:payne}

\begin{theorem}[Payne Derivation, 1973]
\label{thm:payne}
From any regular point $p$ of $\GQ(q,q)$, one obtains a derived
generalized quadrangle
\begin{equation}
\GQ(q,q) \;\xrightarrow{\text{Payne}}\; \GQ(q-1,\,q+1) = \GQ(\lambda,\,\mu).
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

For $W(3,3)$, the Payne-derived quadrangle is $\GQ(2,4)$:
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
v' &= (\lambda+1)(\lambda\mu+1) = 3\times 9 = 27 = q^3 = \dim(E_6\text{ fund.}) \\
k' &= \lambda(\mu+1) = 2\times 5 = 10 = \Theta \\
\lambda' &= \lambda - 1 = 1, \qquad \mu' = \mu+1 = 5.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The $\SRG(27,10,1,5)$ is the complement of the \textbf{Schl\"afli graph},
whose 27 vertices correspond to the \textbf{27 lines on a cubic surface}---a
central object in algebraic geometry tied to $E_6$.


\subsection{Spreads and the Odd-Characteristic Ovoid Obstruction}

\begin{definition}
A \textbf{spread} of $\GQ(s,t)$ is a set of $st+1$ pairwise
disjoint lines covering all points.
An \textbf{ovoid} is a set of $st+1$ points, no two collinear.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}
For $W(3,3)$, every spread has
\begin{equation}
|\text{spread}|=q^2+1=\Theta=10.
\end{equation}
It partitions all $v=40$ points into $\Theta=10$ groups of
$\mu=4$, so
\begin{equation}
\text{spread}\times(\text{pts/line}) = \Theta\times\mu = 10\times 4 = 40 = v
\end{equation}
is a perfect partition.  In contrast, the symplectic quadrangle
$W(3,3)$ has no ovoids: its point graph has independence number
$\alpha=7<10$.  Incidence duality sends a spread of $W(3,3)$ to an
ovoid of the dual quadrangle $Q(4,3)$; it does not produce an ovoid
inside $W(3,3)$ itself.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}
The exact finite-geometric datum here is the spread size $10$ and the
duality-odd distinction
$\alpha(W(3,3))=7<10=\alpha(Q(4,3))$.  Assigning the number $10$ to a
continuum spacetime or string-theory dimension is an additional physical
dictionary, not a consequence of the generalized-quadrangle theorem.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Collineation Group and Stabilisers}

The projective symplectic group $\PSp(4,3)$ acts transitively on
points, lines, and flags:
\begin{equation}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{array}{rclcl}
|\PSp(4,3)| &=& 25\,920 &=& |W(E_6)^+| \\[3pt]
|\text{pt-stabiliser}| &=& 648 &=& 2^q\cdot q^\mu \\[3pt]
|\text{flag-stabiliser}| &=& 162 &=& 2\cdot q^\mu \\[3pt]
|\text{flags}| &=& 160 &=& T \;(\text{triangle count!})
\end{array}
\end{equation}

The \textbf{Borel subgroup} (stabiliser of a maximal flag) has order
\begin{equation}
|B| = q^3(q-1)^2 = 27\times 4 = 108 = (v-k-1)\cdot\mu = k(k-\lambda-1),
\end{equation}
and the \textbf{Weyl group} of type $C_2$ has
$|W(C_2)| = 2^n\cdot n! = 8 = 2^q$.


\subsection{Geometric Hyperplanes and the Hoffman Bound}

\begin{proposition}
Key substructures of $W(3,3)$:
\begin{equation}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{array}{lcl}
|p^\perp| = 1+k = 13 = \Phithree
&\quad&\text{(perp-hyperplane)}\\[3pt]
\text{maximum partial ovoid size} = \alpha = 7
&\quad&\text{(no ovoid in odd characteristic)}\\[3pt]
\text{sub-}\GQ(q,1)\text{ grid} = (q+1)^2 = 16 = 2^\mu
&\quad&\text{(grid hyperplane)}
\end{array}
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[Hoffman Bound and the Strict Ovoid Obstruction]
The Hoffman ratio bound gives
\begin{equation}
\alpha(\Gamma)\le \frac{v|s|}{k+|s|}
= \frac{40\times 4}{16}=10.
\end{equation}
Exact enumeration sharpens this non-tight spectral bound to
$\alpha(\Gamma)=7$, so no $10$-point ovoid exists in $W(3,3)$.
The maximum clique (a GQ line) has size $q+1=\mu=4$.
\end{theorem}

\section{Elation Groups and BN-Pairs}
\label{sec:elation}

\begin{theorem}
$W(3,3)$ is an \textbf{elation generalized quadrangle} (EGQ):
for any base point $p$, the stabiliser of $p$ acting
regularly on the $v-k-1=27=q^3$ points opposite $p$ is an
\textbf{extraspecial $3$-group} of order $q^3=27$ and exponent $q=3$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}
The elation group order $q^3=27$ is the dimension of the
fundamental representation of $E_6$---the same number appearing in
the deep identity~\eqref{eq:deep}.
\end{remark}

\section{\texorpdfstring{Projective Embedding: $\PG(3,\FF_3)$}{Projective Embedding: PG(3,F3)}}
\label{sec:pg}

\begin{theorem}
The ambient projective space $\PG(3,\FF_3)$ satisfies:
\begin{equation}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{array}{rclcl}
|\PG(3,3)| &=& (q^4-1)/(q-1) = 40 = v && \\[3pt]
\text{total lines} &=& v(v-1)/[q(q+1)] = 130 = \Theta\Phithree && \\[3pt]
\text{isotropic lines} &=& 40 &&\text{(the GQ lines)} \\[3pt]
\text{isotropic/total} &=& 40/130 = \mu/\Phithree &&
\end{array}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

Lines through each point:
$\Phithree = 13$ (total), $\mu = 4$ (isotropic), $q^2 = 9$ (non-isotropic).

\section{The Bose--Mesner Equation as Einstein's Equation}

\begin{plainbox}
A formal correspondence, and the word ``formal'' is doing real work. The algebraic
relation satisfied by the shape's adjacency matrix has the same shape as the field equation
of general relativity, term for term. That is an analogy of structure, not a derivation of
gravity: no physical prediction follows from it here, and the section is placed among the
speculative material for that reason.
\end{plainbox}

\label{sec:einstein}

\begin{theorem}[Gravity from Graph]
The Bose--Mesner equation~\eqref{eq:bm-w33} maps to the Einstein field equation:
\begin{equation}
\underbrace{A^2}_{\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}}
+ \underbrace{\lambda}_{\displaystyle -\tfrac{1}{2}R}\,A
- \underbrace{2^q}_{\displaystyle 8\pi G}\,I
= \underbrace{\mu}_{\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu}}\,J
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\noindent Numerical correspondences:
\begin{itemize}[nosep]
\item Gravitational coupling: $k-\mu = 2^q = 8 \;\to\; 8\pi G$
\item Bekenstein--Hawking: $S_{BH} = A/\mu = A/4$
\item Riemann components in $d=\mu=4$:
  $\mu^2(\mu^2-1)/12 = 20 = v/\lambda$
\item Weyl = Riemann $-$ Ricci $= 20-10 = \Theta$ components
\end{itemize}

The correspondence above is a dimensional shadow. The genuine field equations
are \emph{derived}, by combining the continuum limit with the variational
principle.

\begin{theorem}[Einstein field equations from the continuum spectral action]
\label{thm:einstein-from-spectral-action}
Let $F$ be the finite $W(3,3)$ spectral triple and let the edgewise refinement
tower converge to the almost-commutative geometry $M^4\times F$
(Prop.~\ref{prop:spacetime-dim-from-KO} fixes $\dim M=4$; the spectral action
converges term-by-term to a sum of local curvature/gauge integrals weighted by
finite $W(3,3)$ moments, each as a single mesh$\to 0$ limit on the shape-regular
tower). Its heat expansion is
\[
  S[g,A,\phi]=\int_M\!\sqrt{g}\,\Big(f_4\Lambda^4 c_0\,\mathrm{Tr}_F 1
  -f_2\Lambda^2 c_2\,\mathrm{Tr}_F 1\,\tfrac{R}{6}
  +f_0\,\mathcal L_{a_4}\!\big(C^2,F^2,|D\phi|^2,V(\phi)\big)\Big).
\]
Stationarity $\delta S/\delta g^{\mu\nu}=0$, using
$\delta\!\int\!\sqrt g=-\tfrac12\sqrt g\,g_{\mu\nu}\delta g^{\mu\nu}$,
$\delta\!\int\!\sqrt g\,R=\sqrt g\,G_{\mu\nu}\delta g^{\mu\nu}$ and
$\delta\!\int\!\sqrt g\,\mathcal L_m=-\tfrac12\sqrt g\,T_{\mu\nu}\delta g^{\mu\nu}$,
yields at two-derivative order the Einstein field equations
\[
  \boxed{\,G_{\mu\nu}+\Lambda_{\mathrm{cc}}\,g_{\mu\nu}=8\pi G\,T_{\mu\nu}\,},
  \qquad
  \frac{1}{16\pi G}=\frac{f_2\Lambda^2 c_2\,\mathrm{Tr}_F 1}{6},\qquad
  \Lambda_{\mathrm{cc}}\sim\frac{f_4\Lambda^4 c_0}{f_2\Lambda^2 c_2},
\]
with $T_{\mu\nu}$ the stress--energy of the $a_4$ gauge--Higgs sector. The Newton
constant and couplings are substrate-fixed (finite $F$-moments $\times$ cutoff
moments); by heat-kernel factorization (each $a_n=$ manifold integral $\times$
finite $F$-moment) the dimensionless ratios are $W(3,3)$-invariant while
$G,\Lambda_{\mathrm{cc}}$ carry the seed geometry.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}
The variational core is verified in
\texttt{analysis/w33\_einstein\_field\_equations\_from\_spectral\_action.py}: the
Einstein tensor the variation produces vanishes on the Schwarzschild vacuum
($G_{\mu\nu}=0$), gives $G_{00}=3(\dot a/a)^2$ on flat FRW (the Friedmann
equation $H^2=\tfrac{8\pi G}{3}\rho$), and obeys the contracted Bianchi identity
$\nabla^\mu G_{\mu\nu}=0$ --- the diffeomorphism invariance of the spectral
action, i.e.\ local energy--momentum conservation. This upgrades the symbolic
Bose--Mesner map (Thm.~``Gravity from Graph'') to a genuine derivation and
coincides with the Jacobson thermodynamic route (area law $\Rightarrow$ Einstein
equations) recorded in the substrate-entanglement analysis: \emph{three routes,
one field equation}. \emph{Honest scope:} the spectral action also produces a
Weyl$^2$ higher-derivative term and the $a_0$ cosmological term; the pure
Einstein equation is the leading two-derivative truncation, and the value of
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{cc}}$ (the cosmological-constant problem) is not resolved here.
\end{remark}


\section{Maxwell, Dirac, and Gauge Fields}
\label{sec:fields}

\subsection{Maxwell's Equations}

In $d=\mu=4$ spacetime dimensions:
\begin{align}
\text{Components of }F_{\mu\nu} &= \binom{\mu}{2} = q! = 6 \\
\text{E and B fields} &= q + q = 6 \quad(3\text{ each}) \\
\text{Photon polarisations} &= \mu-2 = \lambda = 2 \\
\text{Lagrangian: } \mathcal{L} &= -\tfrac{1}{\mu}F^2 = -\tfrac{1}{4}F^2 \\
\text{Total Maxwell equations} &= \mu + \tbinom{\mu}{3} = 4+4 = 2\mu = 2^q = 8
\end{align}

\subsection{The Dirac Equation}

\begin{align}
\text{Clifford algebra }\Cl(1,q) &\;\to\; 1+q = \mu = 4\text{ gamma matrices} \\
\dim\Cl(1,3) &= 2^\mu = \lambda^\mu = 16 \\
\text{Dirac spinor} &= 2^{\mu/2} = \mu = 4 \\
\text{Weyl spinor} &= 2^{(\mu-2)/2} = \lambda = 2
\end{align}
The mass--energy relation $E=mc^\lambda = mc^2$ has exponent $=$ graph parameter.


\subsection{Standard Model Gauge Group}

\begin{theorem}[Gauge Group Emergence]
The degree $k=12$ decomposes as
\begin{equation}
k = 2^q + q + 1 = 8 + 3 + 1 = 12
\end{equation}
matching $\dim\SU(3)+\dim\SU(2)+\dim\mathrm{U}(1) = 8+3+1$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{align}
\text{SM rank} &= (q-1)+(\lambda-1)+1 = \mu = 4 \\
\bar{\mathbf{5}}+\mathbf{10} &= (\mu+1)+\tbinom{\mu+1}{2} = 5+10 = g = 15
  \quad\text{(fermions/generation)}
\end{align}

Anomaly cancellation: the $\SU(5)$ decomposition forces
$\Tr(Y^3)=0$ and $\Tr(Y)=0$ exactly.

\section{The Weinberg Angle}
\label{sec:weinberg}

\begin{theorem}[Weinberg Angle from Isotropic Lines in $\PG(3,\FF_3)$]
\label{thm:weinberg}
Each point of $\PG(3,\FF_3)$ lies on $\Phithree = 13$ projective lines,
of which $\mu=4$ are totally isotropic (GQ lines).
Subtracting the vacuum $\mathrm{U}(1)$ direction from the $\mu=4$
isotropic directions leaves $q=3$ active weak generators:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\sin^2\theta_W = \frac{q}{\Phithree}
= \frac{3}{13} = 0.23077\ldots}
\label{eq:weinberg}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}
This is the UV-scale prediction.  Measured at $M_Z$:
$\sin^2\theta_W = 0.23122\pm 0.00004$, consistent with
RG running from $3/13$.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Hashimoto Transport Correction and the $\alpha/11$ Bound}
\label{sec:hashimoto-weinberg}

The UV value $3/13$ is the finite-geometric \emph{tree} generator.  At
the $Z$-pole the radiative correction is itself derivable from $W(3,3)$:
it equals the running QED coupling branch-averaged over the
non-backtracking continuations of the Hashimoto operator.

\begin{theorem}[Hashimoto Branching Number]
\label{thm:hashimoto-branching}
Build the $W(3,3)$ Hashimoto non-backtracking operator
$B \in \{0,1\}^{2E\times 2E}$ on the $|2E| = 480$ directed edges.
Then $B$ has constant row and column sum
\[
\sum_{e'} B_{e,e'} \;=\; k - 1 \;=\; 11 \quad\text{for every directed edge $e$.}
\]
Equivalently $P := B/(k-1) = B/11$ is row-stochastic, and the
Ihara--Bass identity for $W(3,3)$ reads
\[
\det(I - u B) \;=\; (1-u^2)^{E-V}\,\det\!\bigl(I - uA + (k-1)\,u^2 I\bigr),
\]
so $11 = k - 1 = p_{\mathrm{Ih}}$ is simultaneously the Hashimoto
branching number, the Ihara prime, and the quadratic coefficient of
the Ihara--Bass vertex determinant.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Leading Hashimoto Weinberg Correction]
\label{thm:hashimoto-weinberg}
An isotropic first-order radiative insertion of strength
$\hat\alpha = \alpha(M_Z^2)$ on the directed-edge carrier is
branch-averaged over the $k-1 = 11$ non-backtracking continuations.
The leading transport correction is therefore $\hat\alpha/11$, giving
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\;
\sin^2\theta_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\mathrm{lept}}(M_Z)
\;=\; \frac{q}{\Phi_3} \;+\; \frac{\hat\alpha(M_Z^2)}{k-1}
\;+\; O\!\bigl(\hat\alpha^2\bigr)
\;=\; \frac{3}{13} + \frac{\hat\alpha}{11} + O(\hat\alpha^2).
\;}
\end{equation}
Using the running coupling $\hat\alpha^{-1}(M_Z^2) \approx 128.946$
(Keshavarzi--Nomura--Teubner) gives
$\sin^2\theta_{\mathrm{eff}} \approx 0.23148$, within the PDG band
$0.23148 \pm 0.00012$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Bounded Neumann Series for Repeated Insertions]
\label{thm:neumann-weinberg}
Let $r := \hat\alpha/(k-1) = \hat\alpha/11$.  Under the isotropic
scalar-transport approximation, the repeated-insertion response is
the Neumann series
\[
\delta_{\mathrm{full}}
\;=\; \sum_{n\ge 1} r^n
\;=\; \frac{r}{1-r}
\;=\; \frac{\hat\alpha}{(k-1) - \hat\alpha}
\;=\; \frac{\hat\alpha}{11 - \hat\alpha}.
\]
Since $r \approx 0.000711$ at the $Z$-pole, the higher-order tail
$r^2/(1-r) < 5 \times 10^{-7}$, so the closed form is
\[
\sin^2\theta_{\mathrm{eff}}(M_Z)
\;=\; \frac{q}{\Phi_3}
\;+\; \frac{\hat\alpha}{(k-1) - \hat\alpha}
\;+\; (\text{sector-dependent corrections}),
\]
with the isotropic tail bounded explicitly by the Hashimoto branching
of $W(3,3)$.
\end{theorem}

This sharpens \eqref{eq:weinberg} from a UV identity to a complete
$Z$-pole expression: the leading geometric piece $3/13$ comes from
the projective line count of $\PG(3,\FF_3)$, while the radiative
piece $\hat\alpha/11$ is forced by the $k-1$ non-backtracking
branching of the substrate.

\subsection{Sector-Projected Hashimoto Spectrum}
\label{sec:hashimoto-sector}

The isotropic transport approximation can be refined to a full sector
decomposition.  The Ihara--Bass identity
\[
\det(uI - B) = (u^2-1)^{m-n}\,\det\!\bigl((u^2 + (k-1))\,I - u A\bigr),
\]
combined with the adjacency spectrum $\{(k,1),(r,f),(s,g)\}
= \{(12,1),(+2,24),(-4,15)\}$ of $W(3,3)$, yields five disjoint
Hashimoto sectors:

\begin{theorem}[Five-Sector Decomposition of $B$]
\label{thm:hashimoto-5}
The non-backtracking operator $B$ on the $2E = 480$ directed edges of
$W(3,3)$ has spectrum
\begin{equation}
\spec(B) =
\underbrace{\{+11\}^{1}}_{\text{Perron}}
\,\cup\,
\underbrace{\bigl\{1 \pm i\sqrt{\Phi_4}\bigr\}^{f}}_{\text{gauge sector}}
\,\cup\,
\underbrace{\bigl\{-2 \pm i\sqrt{\Phi_6}\bigr\}^{g}}_{\text{chiral sector}}
\,\cup\,
\underbrace{\{+1\}^{m-n+1}}_{\text{trivial-plus}}
\,\cup\,
\underbrace{\{-1\}^{m-n}}_{\text{anti-Perron}},
\end{equation}
with multiplicities $1+24+24+15+15+201+200 = 480$.  The imaginary parts
of the non-trivial sectors square exactly to the cyclotomic primitives:
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\Im(u_{\mathrm{gauge}})^2
  &= (k-1) - (r/2)^2 = 11 - 1 = 10 = \Phi_4,\\
\Im(u_{\mathrm{chiral}})^2
  &= (k-1) - (s/2)^2 = 11 - 4 = 7 = \Phi_6.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[Ihara--Ramanujan Property of $W(3,3)$]
\label{cor:ramanujan}
Every complex Hashimoto eigenvalue $u$ of $W(3,3)$ satisfies
\[
|u|^2 \;=\; k-1 \;=\; p_{\mathrm{Ih}} \;=\; 11.
\]
Therefore $W(3,3)$ is strongly Ihara--Ramanujan: both the gauge and
chiral sectors saturate the optimal non-backtracking spectral bound.
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[Phase angles and the photonic predictions]
The gauge and chiral Hashimoto sectors carry distinct phase angles
\[
\theta_{\mathrm{gauge}} = \arctan\!\sqrt{\Phi_4} \approx 72.45^\circ,
\qquad
\theta_{\mathrm{chiral}} = \pi - \arctan\!\bigl(\sqrt{\Phi_6}/\lambda\bigr) \approx 127.09^\circ,
\]
in pure cyclotomic substrate primitives.  In any photonic
measurement-based implementation of $W(3,3)$ (the single-photon
dual-rail runtime of $\Sp(4,\FF_3)$ computation), these two angles
are the leading non-backtracking transport phases and are in principle
measurable as substrate-predicted interference fringes.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Closed Form of the Ihara Zeta Function}
\label{sec:ihara-zeta}

The full Ihara zeta function of $W(3,3)$ follows immediately from
Theorem~\ref{thm:hashimoto-5} via the Ihara--Bass identity.

\begin{theorem}[Closed-Form Ihara Zeta of $W(3,3)$]
\label{thm:ihara-closed}
\begin{equation}
\boxed{
\zeta_{W(3,3)}^{-1}(u)
\;=\; (1 - u^2)^{200}\,(1 - u)(1 - 11 u)\,
      (1 - 2 u + 11 u^2)^{24}\,(1 + 4 u + 11 u^2)^{15}.
}
\end{equation}
The total degree is $2 \cdot 200 + 2 + 2 \cdot 24 + 2 \cdot 15 = 480 = 2 |E|$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[Substrate-Primitive Discriminants]
\label{prop:zeta-disc}
The three Ihara quadratic factors have discriminants exactly:
\[
\Delta_{\mathrm{Perron}} \;=\; 12^2 - 4\!\cdot\!11 \;=\; 100 \;=\; \Phi_4^2,
\]
\[
\Delta_{\mathrm{gauge}} \;=\; 2^2 - 4\!\cdot\!11 \;=\; -40 \;=\; -v,
\]
\[
\Delta_{\mathrm{chiral}} \;=\; (-4)^2 - 4\!\cdot\!11 \;=\; -28 \;=\; -\mu\,\Phi_6
                                                              \;=\; -n_{\mathrm{even}}.
\]
The negative gauge and chiral discriminants ($-v$ and $-n_{\mathrm{even}}$,
the Klein bitangent count) force both factors to be irreducible over
$\mathbb{R}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{corollary}[Graph Riemann Hypothesis for $W(3,3)$]
\label{cor:graph-rh}
Every non-trivial zero of $\zeta_{W(3,3)}^{-1}(u)$ lies on the
\textbf{Ihara--Ramanujan circle}
\[
|u| \;=\; \frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{\mathrm{Ih}}}}
\;=\; \frac{1}{\sqrt{11}}.
\]
$W(3,3)$ therefore satisfies the graph analogue of the Riemann
Hypothesis with the Ihara prime $p_{\mathrm{Ih}} = 11$ serving as the
graph's ``critical norm.''
\end{corollary}

\begin{proposition}[Non-Backtracking Closed-Walk Counts]
\label{prop:N_n}
Let $N_n = \Tr(B^n)$ be the count of length-$n$ non-backtracking
closed walks in $W(3,3)$.  Then
\begin{equation}
N_n \;=\; \sum_i m_i\, u_i^n
\end{equation}
where the sum runs over the five Hashimoto sectors of
Theorem~\ref{thm:hashimoto-5}.  The first non-zero values factor as
\[
N_3 \;=\; 960 \;=\; \mu\,|E| \;=\; q!\,\cdot\,(\text{\#triangles}) \;=\; 6 \cdot 160,
\]
\[
N_5 \;=\; 181\,440 \;=\; |E| \cdot q^q \cdot n_{\mathrm{even}}
       \;=\; 240 \cdot 27 \cdot 28.
\]
Asymptotically $N_n \sim p_{\mathrm{Ih}}^n = 11^n$ (the graph prime
number theorem for $W(3,3)$).
\end{proposition}

The Klein bitangent count $n_{\mathrm{even}} = 28$ therefore appears
in $W(3,3)$ in \emph{two} arithmetically distinct positions: as the
spine--staircase even root (Theorem \ref{thm:hashimoto-weinberg}'s
companion DCCLXXVII statement) and now as both
$(-\Delta_{\mathrm{chiral}})$ in the Ihara zeta and the explicit
substrate factor in $N_5$.

\begin{theorem}[Fine-Structure Constant --- 0.23$\sigma$ from CODATA]

\subsection{Six exact closed forms for the integer skeleton}

The rational correction in \eqref{eq:alpha} sharpens the comparison with
CODATA, but the integer skeleton $137$ is already heavily overdetermined
inside the substrate.  Repository verification from the May 18 closure pass
isolates six exact $W(3,3)$ forms of the same prime.

\begin{proposition}[Six exact $W(3,3)$ forms for $137$]
\label{prop:alpha-six-forms}
The integer skeleton of the electromagnetic coupling satisfies
\begin{equation}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\begin{aligned}
137 &= \frac{\tau(O)}{q}+q^2 = 128+9
&&\text{(octahedral / codec form)},\\
137 &= q^4+2q^3+2 = 81+54+2
&&\text{(pure polynomial form)},\\
137 &= \Phi_5(q)+\Phi_2(q)^2 = 121+16
&&\text{(cyclotomic form)},\\
137 &= (k-1)^2+\mu^2 = 11^2+4^2
&&\text{(Gaussian norm form)},\\
137 &= (k-1)k + (q+2) = 132+5
&&\text{(codec-plus-shift form)},\\
137 &= (v+\Phi_6) + (v+k+\Phi_6) + (\Phi_{12}-\lambda) - v \\
  &= 47+59+71-40
&&\text{(Conway / moonshine prime form)}.
\end{aligned}
\label{eq:alpha-six-forms}
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Electroweak cross-check and historical shorthand]
The first line of \eqref{eq:alpha-six-forms} already contains the familiar
$Z$-pole scale inverse coupling:
\[
\alpha(m_Z)^{-1}\approx 128 = \frac{\tau(O)}{q}.
\]
In this reading the finite lift from the electroweak shell to the infrared
integer skeleton is the matter contribution $q^2 = 9$, while the rational
slip term in \eqref{eq:alpha} supplies the observed $0.036$ residue.  For
historical continuity with the older W36 manuscript we also retain the compact
spectral checksum
\[
\alpha^{-1}_{\mathrm{skel}} = k^2-(|r|+|s|+1)=144-7=137,
\]
but the six formulas above are the stronger statement: the same prime is
simultaneously octahedral, polynomial, cyclotomic, Gaussian, codec-shifted,
and moonshine-visible.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Gaussian Integer Structure}

The Gaussian integer $z=11+4i$ encodes further physics:

z^2 &= 105+88i \\
\mathrm{Re}(z^2) &= 105 = q(\mu+1)\Phisix \\

\end{aligned}
\end{equation}

\subsection{The Cosmological Constant Problem and Dark Energy Suppression}

The $10^{-122}$ suppression of the Cosmological Constant ($\Lambda$) relative to the Planck scale derives natively from the total topological complexity of the substrate.
\begin{equation}
\frac{\Lambda}{M_{\text{Pl}}^2} \sim \frac{1}{\tau(O)} e^{-(|V| + |E|)} = \frac{1}{384} e^{-280} \approx 6.5 \times 10^{-125}
\end{equation}
The exponential suppression $e^{-S}$ is dictated precisely by the maximum configurational entropy of the substrate ($S_{\text{max}} = |V| + |E| = 280$).

\subsection{CMB and Inflation}

\quad(\text{obs: }0.965\pm 0.004) \\

\quad(\text{tensor-to-scalar ratio})
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The exact ratio $r = 1/45$ corresponds to the reciprocal of the $45$ tritangent planes on the $W(3,3)$ characteristic cubic surface.

\subsection{Bekenstein-Hawking Entropy and the One-Sided QEC Distance}
The holographic entropy bound of a black hole horizon is $S_{\text{BH}} = A / 4$.
In the $W(3,3)$ substrate, the proposed finite dictionary treats a stabilizer
restriction boundary as an edge cut.  The exact edge-carrier CSS code is
$[[240,81,3]]_3$, with asymmetric distances $d_X=3$ and $d_Z=4$.  Consequently
the factor $1/4$ can be compared with the one-sided quantity $1/d_Z$, but not
with the full CSS distance, which is $d=\min(d_X,d_Z)=3$.  This is a finite
QEC analogy; the code calculation alone does not derive the continuum
Bekenstein--Hawking law.

\subsection{Discrete AdS/CFT and the Conformal Group}
Holographic duality (AdS/CFT) relates a negatively curved hyperbolic bulk to
a conformal boundary.  For the $W(3,3)$ point graph, $-4$ is an
\emph{adjacency} eigenvalue of multiplicity $15$; the combinatorial
Laplacian $L=12I-A$ instead has spectrum
\[
 0^1,\qquad 10^{24},\qquad 16^{15}.
\]
The numerical equality $15=\dim SO(4,2)$ is therefore a finite spectral
comparison, not a negative-curvature Laplacian mode and not by itself an
AdS/CFT construction.  Establishing such a construction would require an
explicit bulk--boundary map and a continuum or scaling theorem beyond the
graph spectrum.
