\begin{proposition}[Two continua of $W(3,3)$]
\label{prop:two-continua}
\textup{(i)} The arithmetic tower $W(3,q)$, $q=3^n$ --- the symplectic
generalized quadrangle over $\mathbb F_q$, an
$\mathrm{SRG}\big((q{+}1)(q^2{+}1),\,q(q{+}1),\,q{-}1,\,q{+}1\big)$ --- has at
every $q$ \emph{exactly three} adjacency eigenvalues $q(q{+}1)$, $q{-}1$,
$-(q{+}1)$, with all nonzero Laplacian eigenvalues of order $q^2$; no Weyl law
$N(\lambda)\sim\lambda^{d/2}$ emerges, so the arithmetic tower does \emph{not}
converge to a Riemannian manifold. Hence the spacetime (metric) continuum
\emph{must} use an external curved seed --- canonically $K3$, since
$\chi(K3)=24=f$ --- refined edgewise
(Theorem~\ref{thm:r3-continuum-resolution}); the almost-commutative $M^4\times F$
framing is forced, not chosen. \textup{(ii)} The substrate's Heisenberg matter
shell $3^{1+2}$ with its $\mathrm{Sp}(4,3)$ action is a Weil-representation
datum, whose $q\to\infty$ limit is the metaplectic (oscillator) representation
of $\mathrm{Sp}(4,\mathbb R)$ on $L^2(\mathbb R^2)$ --- an \emph{intrinsic}
phase-space continuum. The two continua are the metric one
($K3\to$ spacetime, Standard Model $+$ general relativity) and the symplectic
one (oscillator rep $\to$ the continuous-variable photonic computer of the
companion architecture). One finite geometry; two continuum limits ---
the theory of everything and the machine that runs on it.
\textup{(Witness: \texttt{analysis/w33\_two\_continua\_symplectic\_metric.py}.)}
\end{proposition}

That the metric continuum is \emph{four}-dimensional is itself forced by the
substrate, removing the last input taken from observation.

\begin{proposition}[The spacetime dimension is forced to be four]
\label{prop:spacetime-dim-from-KO}
The finite $W(3,3)$ spectral triple has KO-dimension $6$: its verified
real-structure signs $J^2=+1$, $JD=+DJ$, $J\gamma=-\gamma J$ are the triple
$(+,+,-)$, which is KO-dimension $6\pmod 8$ (and $6=2q$). The Connes--Barrett
resolution of fermion doubling requires the total spectral triple $M\times F$
to have KO-dimension $\equiv 2\pmod 8$, and KO-dimension is additive under
products. Hence
\[
  \mathrm{KO}(M)\equiv 2-6\equiv 4\pmod 8 .
\]
Since KO-dimension and metric dimension coincide $\pmod 8$ for a spin manifold,
$\dim M\equiv 4\pmod 8$, whose minimal (physical) value is $\dim M=4$. Thus the
four-dimensionality of spacetime --- the one datum the $M^4\times F$ framing
previously took from observation --- is \emph{derived} from $W(3,3)$, and traces
to $q=3$ through $\mathrm{KO}(F)=2q=6$. The independence of KO-dimension from
metric dimension (so the finite $F$ is metrically $0$-dimensional yet
KO-dimension $6$) is exactly what makes the derivation possible. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_spacetime\_dimension\_from\_KO.py}.)
\end{proposition}

\begin{corollary}[The heterotic--on--$K3$ kinematic dictionary]
\label{cor:heterotic-K3-dictionary}
Assembling Proposition~\ref{prop:spacetime-dim-from-KO} with the lattice and
spectral results established above gives a complete correspondence between the
\emph{kinematic} data of an $E_8\times E_8$ heterotic compactification on $K3$
and invariants of $W(3,3)$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Spacetime dimension} $\dim M=4$, forced by $\mathrm{KO}(F)=2q=6$
  (Proposition~\ref{prop:spacetime-dim-from-KO}).
\item \emph{Internal seed} $=K3$: the only Calabi--Yau $4$-seed whose Euler
  number $\chi=24=f$ matches the $W(3,3)$ matter count, realized exactly on the
  edgewise simplicial seed (integral $H^2(K3,\mathbb Z)$ with even unimodular
  intersection form of signature $(3,19)=(q,\,g+\mu)$).
\item \emph{Gauge lattice} $=E_8\times E_8$: the negative-definite part of the
  $K3$ intersection form is the rank-$16$ complement $E_8(-1)\oplus E_8(-1)$
  (not $D_{16}^+$), and the graph-Laplacian energy of $W(3,3)$ equipartitions as
  $f\cdot\Theta=g\cdot\lambda^{\mu}=E=240=|E_8|$, $E+E=480=vk$, with the single
  $E_8$ being the canonical mod-$2$ homology lift of $W(3,3)$ (R1).
\item \emph{Instanton number} $n_1+n_2=\int_{K3}c_2(TK3)=\chi(K3)=24=f$: the
  Green--Schwarz/Bianchi anomaly condition for the heterotic string on $K3$
  fixes the total instanton number in the two $E_8$ factors to $\chi(K3)$, which
  is again the matter count $f=24$.
\end{enumerate}
Thus the four kinematic inputs of the heterotic-$K3$ setup --- spacetime
dimension, internal seed, gauge group, and instanton number --- are each a
$W(3,3)$ invariant ($\dim=4$ from $\mathrm{KO}=2q$; seed and instanton number
from $\chi=f=24$; gauge group from the Laplacian equipartition $E+E=vk$). This
is a structural dictionary, not a dynamical proof that the physical string is
heterotic-on-$K3$; its content is that every datum such a compactification must
specify by hand is, in the $W(3,3)$ substrate, pinned to an invariant of the
finite geometry.
\end{corollary}

%% ===============================================================
\section{The Chiral Trade Lattice and the Two 480s}

\begin{plainbox}
``Chiral'' means handed: a structure that is not the same as its mirror image.
This section is about a place where the object produces two families of the same size,
$480$ each, which are \emph{not} interchangeable. The number appearing twice is the easy
part; showing the two families are genuinely different, rather than one family counted in
two ways, is the work.
\end{plainbox}

\label{sec:chiral-trade-lattice}
%% ===============================================================

Section~\ref{sec:e8-eigenlattice-boundary} resolved the definite side of the
spectrum: the $+2$-eigenlattice $L_2$ (rank $24$) whose minimal shell carries
the signed $E_8$ roots. The spectral complement --- the $(-4)$-eigenspace of
multiplicity $g=15$, the \emph{chiral sector} of the Hashimoto decomposition
--- remained integrally uncharted. Pass~158 closes it and, in doing so,
settles a $G$-set question the Ihara-zeta track left open.

\begin{proposition}[The chiral eigenlattice is the trade lattice]
\label{prop:chiral-trade}
Let $N$ be the $40\times40$ line--point incidence matrix of $W(3,3)$. The
integer $(-4)$-eigenlattice
\[
  L_{4}=\{x\in\mathbb Z^{40}: Ax=-4x\}
\]
is exactly the saturated kernel of $N$: the lattice of integer point-weights
summing to zero on \emph{every} isotropic line --- the \emph{trade lattice}
of the quadrangle, in design-theoretic language. It is a primitive rank-$15$
sublattice, \emph{even} under the standard form, with exact Gram Smith form
and discriminant data
\[
 \operatorname{SNF}(L_4)=\operatorname{diag}(2^{5},6^{9},24),
 \qquad
 \det(L_4)=2^{17}3^{10},
 \qquad
 L_4^{\#}/L_4\cong(\mathbb Z/2)^{14}\oplus\mathbb Z/8\oplus(\mathbb Z/3)^{10}.
\]
Unlike $L_2$, whose discriminant group is elementary, the chiral lattice
carries a genuine $\mathbb Z/8$ depth. Moreover the incidence matrix itself
is \emph{Smith-trivial}: $\operatorname{SNF}_{\mathbb Z}(N)=
\operatorname{diag}(1^{25},0^{15})$, so its image is a rank-$25$ direct
summand of $\mathbb Z^{40}$ and every $p$-rank of the incidence matrix of
$W(3,3)$ equals $25$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[Spectral splitting of the standard unimodular lattice]
\label{thm:glue-identity}
With $L_{12}=\mathbb Z\cdot\mathbf 1$ (Gram $40$), the three eigenlattices
glue into $\mathbb Z^{40}$ with finite index
\[
  \bigl[\mathbb Z^{40}:L_{12}\oplus L_2\oplus L_4\bigr]
  = 2^{18}3^{10}5
  = 77\,396\,705\,280,
\]
and the glue identity
\[
  \bigl(2^{18}3^{10}5\bigr)^2
  \;=\;
  \underbrace{2^3\!\cdot\!5}_{\det L_{12}}
  \cdot
  \underbrace{2^{16}3^{10}5}_{\det L_2}
  \cdot
  \underbrace{2^{17}3^{10}}_{\det L_4}
\]
holds exactly. The Perron, gauge, and chiral sectors of the Bose--Mesner
spectrum are thereby realized as an exact-index orthogonal splitting of the
standard $\mathbb Z^{40}$, with all glue supported on the substrate primes
$\{2,3,5\}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The minimal trades are the hyperbolic double-fours]
\label{thm:minimal-trades}
The minimal norm of $L_4$ is $8$ and its minimal shell has exactly $90$
vectors, forming a single $\mathrm{PSp}(4,3)$-orbit: each is
\[
  x \;=\; \mathbf 1_{\{a,b\}^{\perp\perp}}-\mathbf 1_{\{a,b\}^{\perp}}
\]
for a hyperbolic (non-collinear) point pair $\{a,b\}$ --- weight $+1$ on the
four-point \emph{span} and $-1$ on the four-point \emph{perp} (all points of
$W(3,3)$ are regular, so both sets are $4$-sets). The $90$ minimal trades are
the $90$ hyperbolic lines with a choice of orientation; the $45$ unordered
$\{\mathrm{span},\mathrm{perp}\}$ supports each induce a complete bipartite
$K_{4,4}$ in the collinearity graph, and they are precisely the canonical
$8$-point orbits of the $45$ index-$45$ \emph{binary-polar} subgroups
$(2T\times 2T){:}2$ (support stabilizer order $576$, point orbits $[8,32]$)
--- the fifth vacuum of the five-vacua table, now realized as the ground
shell of the chiral lattice. Finally, the zero-inner-product relation on the
$45$ supports is a strongly regular graph
\[
  \mathrm{SRG}(45,12,3,3),
\]
the collinearity parameters of the generalized quadrangle $\GQ(4,2)$ --- the
point-line dual of the Schl\"afli quadrangle $\GQ(2,4)$ on $27$ points. The
chiral ground shell of $W(3,3)$ thus rebuilds a generalized quadrangle one
level up the $E_6$ ladder.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Explicit $2240\to45\to40$ cubic-incidence bridge]
\label{thm:explicit-cubic-incidence-bridge}
In doubled integral coordinates for the $240$ roots of $E_8$, fix an
$A_2$ root triple and let $W(E_6)$ act through the $72$ roots orthogonal
to it.  Let $\mathcal T$ be the $2240$ unordered zero-sum $A_2$ triples
and let $\mathcal S$ be the $45$ constant-sum cubic supports in a
$27$-root shell.  The incidence matrix
\[
 M_{s,t}=1
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \langle r,u\rangle=0
 \text{ for all }r\in s,\ u\in t
\]
is an explicit $W(E_6)$-equivariant map
\[
 M:\mathbb Q^{2240}\longrightarrow\mathbb Q^{45}
\]
of rank $45$.  Its row sums are $32$, while its column sums are
$0^{2000},6^{240}$.  The $240$ live columns are exactly the unique
$240$-orbit; all three $432$-orbits are killed basis vector by basis
vector.  With $A_{45}$ the disjointness graph on the supports,
\[
 \boxed{MM^{\mathsf T}=24I_{45}-6A_{45}+8J_{45}},
 \qquad
 \operatorname{spec}(MM^{\mathsf T})
 =192^1\oplus48^{20}\oplus12^{24}.
\]
The live module is
\[
 1\oplus15_a\oplus20\oplus24\oplus30\oplus60_a\oplus90,
\]
so the kernel inside that orbit is
$15_a\oplus30\oplus60_a\oplus90$ of dimension $195$; with the
$2000$ dead coordinate vectors this gives the full kernel dimension
$2195$.

Moreover the $45$ supports are equivariantly bijective, after choosing a
group isomorphism and stabilizer conjugator, with the $45$ intrinsic
$K_{4,4}$ octets of $W(3,3)$.  If $N$ is point--octet incidence and
$C=NM$, then
\[
 \operatorname{rank}C=25,\qquad
 \boxed{CC^{\mathsf T}=96I_{40}+24A+336J_{40}},
\]
with spectrum $13824^1\oplus144^{24}\oplus0^{15}$.  Every live column of
$C$ equals the all-ones column plus twice the incidence column of a
$W(3,3)$ line; all $40$ lines occur exactly six times.  Thus
\[
 C_{\mathrm{live}}=J_{40\times240}+2R
\]
for the pullback of point--line incidence along an equivariant sixfold
map from the live $A_2$ triples to the $40$ isotropic lines.
\end{theorem}

\noindent
The theorem constructs the map missing from a character-only subtraction.
The six sheets are not thereby identified with qutrit phases, physical
polarizations, or particles.  The exact GAP certificate is
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass1138\_explicit\_cubic\_incidence\_bridge.g}.

\begin{theorem}[Schl\"afli--Steinberg Fourier bridge]
\label{thm:schlaefli-steinberg-fourier-bridge}
The three $432$-orbits killed by $M$ have a common object-level
description.  The six $27$-root shells relative to the fixed $A_2$ pair by
negation into three colors.  In a color, every mixed $A_2$ triple contains
one $E_6$ root and one root in each opposite shell, and
\[
 \{x,y,z\}\longmapsto(x,-y)
\]
is a $W(E_6)$-equivariant bijection onto the directed edges of the
Schl\"afli graph $\operatorname{SRG}(27,16,10,8)$.  Thus
$27\cdot16=432$, and the directed-edge stabilizer is $S_5$.

Let $K$ be the sum of the $36$ reflections in class $2C$ on
$\Lambda^2(\operatorname{Aug}\mathbb Q^{27})$, and put
\[
 Q=(K-24)(K-18)(K-12)(K-9).
\]
The natural oriented-edge transform
\[
 T(i\to j)=\operatorname{primitive}\!\bigl(Q(e_i\wedge e_j)\bigr)
\]
is integral, equivariant, reversal-odd, and has rank $81$.  It satisfies
\[
 KT=4T,\qquad QT=11200T,\qquad T(j\to i)=-T(i\to j).
\]
The antisymmetric $216$-edge module is multiplicity-free,
\[
 6\oplus15\oplus20\oplus30\oplus64\oplus81_-,
\]
and $T$ is precisely the projection onto $81_-$.  Hence its
$135$-dimensional kernel is
$6\oplus15\oplus20\oplus30\oplus64$.

The $432$ primitive columns have norm squared $600$ and Gram matrix $G_T$
with
\[
 \boxed{G_T^2=3200G_T}.
\]
They form $216$ antipodal pairs, giving a transitive tight three-angle
projective code in $\mathbb R^{81}$ with absolute normalized inner
products
\[
 0,\qquad \frac1{15},\qquad \frac15
\]
and respective valencies $120,60,35$.  These three relations do not form
a three-class association scheme.

The integral edge frame has a sharper arithmetic fingerprint.  If $L_T$
is the lattice generated by the columns of $T$ and
$L_T^{\mathrm{sat}}=(L_T\otimes\mathbb Q)\cap\mathbb Z^{325}$, then
\[
 \operatorname{SNF}(T)
   =1^{15},\,2^6,\,4^8,\,8^{29},\,40^{23}
\]
on its rank-$81$ image, and hence
\[
 L_T^{\mathrm{sat}}/L_T\cong
 (\mathbb Z/2)^6\oplus(\mathbb Z/4)^8\oplus
 (\mathbb Z/8)^{29}\oplus(\mathbb Z/40)^{23}.
\]
Its order is
\[
 [L_T^{\mathrm{sat}}:L_T]=2^{178}5^{23}
 =4567192616659071619386515102238384436424789196800000000000000000000000.
\]
The modular ranks are $15$ in characteristic $2$, $58$ in
characteristic $5$, and $81$ in characteristics $3,7,11$; thus the
internal edge lattice has exactly the bad primes $\{2,5\}$.  MeatAxe
composition factors identify the selected modular images more precisely:
the characteristic-$2$ image is $\mathbf1\oplus\mathbf{14}$, while the
characteristic-$5$ image is an irreducible $\mathbf{58}$.

The characteristic-$5$ result is a nonsplit module statement, not merely
the dimension equation $81=58+23$.  Put
\[
 S_5=L_T^{\mathrm{sat}}/5L_T^{\mathrm{sat}},\qquad
 Q_T=(L_T^{\mathrm{sat}}/L_T)_{(5)},\qquad
 K_5=K(W_{3,3})_{(5)}.
\]
The older spanning-tree calculation already owns
$\tau(W_{3,3})=2^{81}5^{23}$, the critical-group factor
$K_5\cong(\mathbb Z/5)^{23}$, and the bare arithmetic $81-23=58$.
The additional object-level statement is
\[
 Q_T\otimes\operatorname{sgn}\cong K_5
\]
as $\mathbb F_5W(E_6)$-modules, uniquely up to a nonzero scalar, and
\[
 \boxed{0\longrightarrow I_{58}\longrightarrow S_5
 \longrightarrow K_5\otimes\operatorname{sgn}\longrightarrow0.}
\]
Here $I_{58}$ is the irreducible edge image.  The complete submodule
dimension profile of $S_5$ is $0,58,81$, both for $W(E_6)$ and after
restriction to $PSp(4,3)$, and
\[
 \operatorname{Hom}_{W(E_6)}
   (K_5\otimes\operatorname{sgn},S_5)=0.
\]
Thus the sequence is nonsplit over both groups: the saturated reduction
is not $\mathbf{58}\oplus\mathbf{23}$. Pass~1147 proves that the displayed
extension class is nonzero. Pass~1335, stated in
\S\ref{sec:pass1335-brauer-tree}, later computes the full
\(\operatorname{Ext}^1\) dimension and proves that this class spans it.

The $A_2$ Coxeter element centralizes $W(E_6)$ and transports the transform
through the three colors.  Consequently their rank-$243$ image is
\[
 81_-\boxtimes\mathbb Q[C_3].
\]
After adjoining $\omega=e^{2\pi i/3}$, finite Fourier transform splits it
as
\[
 (81_-\boxtimes1)\oplus
 (81_-\boxtimes\omega)\oplus
 (81_-\boxtimes\omega^2).
\]
The torsor is intrinsic relative to the fixed $A_2$; ordering these three
summands chooses an origin color and a Coxeter generator.
Integrally, the color change-of-basis has Smith diagonal $(1,1,3)$.
Tensoring it with the rank-$81$ frame gives index
\[
 3^{81}=443426488243037769948249630619149892803
\]
for the integral trivial-plus-augmentation color coordinates.  This
$3$-primary color obstruction is separate from the $\{2,5\}$-primary
non-saturation of the edge frame.  Their combined integral support is
$\{2,3,5\}$, but the roles of the three primes are not interchangeable.

Extending the three transforms by zero and adjoining them to the cubic
map gives the explicit integer map
\[
 \Phi=M\oplus T_0\oplus T_1\oplus T_2:
 \mathbb Q^{2240}\longrightarrow
 \mathbb Q^{45}\oplus
 \bigoplus_{c\in C_3}
 \Lambda^2(\operatorname{Aug}\mathbb Q^{27}_c).
\]
Its literal matrix has shape $1020\times2240$ and
\[
 \boxed{\operatorname{rank}\Phi=45+3\cdot81=288},
 \qquad
 \boxed{\dim\ker\Phi=1952}.
\]
The $240$ cubic-live columns and $1296$ color columns are disjoint; the
remaining $704$ basis triples are killed by both maps.  Combining this
with the exact character decomposition of $\ker M$ gives
\[
\ker\Phi=
13\cdot1+16\cdot6+5\cdot15+4\cdot15_a+21\cdot20+2\cdot24+
9\cdot30+4\cdot60_a+10\cdot64+90.
\]
\end{theorem}

\noindent
This theorem gives the earlier residual number $1952$ an explicit
equivariant meaning.  The dimensional mnemonic
$1952=7\dim\Lambda^2(\mathbb Q^{24})+20$ is not the decomposition above
and names no such map.  Likewise, the exact $C_3$ Fourier grading alone
does not identify physical generations, masses, or Yukawa couplings.
The GAP witness is
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass1147\_schlaefli\_steinberg\_fourier\_bridge.g}.

\begin{theorem}[Triality, integral transport, and nonselection
  (Passes 1325--1329)]
\label{thm:triality-integral-nonselection}
Let $X$ be one $432$-dimensional $W(E_6)/S_5$ carrier,
$H_{26}=\operatorname{End}_{W(E_6)}(X)$, and let
$X^{(3)}=X\otimes\mathbb C^3_{\rm perm}$ carry the commuting color
triality.  Successively imposing no color symmetry, the cyclic color
shift, and full $S_3$ triality gives the exact commutant hierarchy
\[
\begin{array}{ccl}
\operatorname{End}_{W(E_6)}(X^{(3)})
 &\cong&H_{26}\otimes M_3(\mathbb C),\qquad \dim=234,\\
\operatorname{End}_{W(E_6)\times C_3}(X^{(3)})
 &\cong&H_{26}\otimes\mathbb C[C_3],\qquad \dim=78,\\
\operatorname{End}_{W(E_6)\times S_3}(X^{(3)})
 &\cong&H_{26}\oplus H_{26},\qquad \dim=52.
\end{array}
\]
Thus $234\to78\to52$ records two different symmetry constraints, not
three competing decompositions.

For the six $X$--to--$480$ orbital channels, the primitive coefficient
matrix has
\[
 \operatorname{SNF}=\operatorname{diag}(1,1,1,12,12,24),
\]
so this transport lattice has bad primes $\{2,3\}$.  The larger primitive
lattice of all $26$ Hecke matrix units has bad primes
$\{2,3,5\}$.  This second set is again a different object from both the
$\{2,5\}$ internal edge-frame obstruction and the color index $3^{81}$
in Theorem~\ref{thm:schlaefli-steinberg-fourier-bridge}.

On the threefold species-$20$ multiplicity space, the orthogonal
idempotent normalizer is
\[
 C_2^3\rtimes S_3=W(B_3),\qquad |W(B_3)|=48,
\]
with orientation-preserving subgroup $W(B_3)^+\cong S_4$ of order $24$.
This normalizer is not the independent three-row gauge
\[
 S_3\wr S_3=S_3^3\rtimes S_3,\qquad |S_3\wr S_3|=1296.
\]
Moreover, on the aligned species-$20$ transport block,
\[
 B^7=-I_3,\qquad B^8=+I_3.
\]
Every $W(E_6)$-averaged primitive-cycle operator is scalar on the
multiplicity-one species-$20$ copy of the directed-edge carrier, so no
such length-$7$ or length-$8$ operator canonically selects one of the
three $432$-side copies.

Finally, the three $432$ carriers form the transitive $S_3$-set
$S_3/S_2$, not a regular $S_3$ torsor.  Its orientation-preserving
$C_3$ subgroup acts freely and transitively.  The genuine regular
$S_3$ torsor is instead the six signed $27$-carrier refinement.  These
statements fix the scope of ``triality'', ``color torsor'', and
``sixfold torsor'' without assigning any of them a physical gauge role.
\end{theorem}

\noindent
The exact matrices and independent reconstruction are recorded in
\texttt{analysis/BT1325\_BT1329\_triality\_integral\_gauge.md},
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass1329\_independent\_checker.py}, and the
corresponding GAP certificate.

\begin{theorem}[The two 480s]
\label{thm:two-480s}
The $480$ Hashimoto arcs (directed edges, the carrier of the Ihara zeta
function and the graph Riemann Hypothesis of
\S\ref{sec:ihara-zeta}) and the $480$ minimal vectors of the eigenlattice
$L_2$ (the signed $E_8$-root carrier of
Proposition~\ref{prop:eigenlattice-obstruction}) are both transitive
$\mathrm{PSp}(4,3)$-sets with point stabilizers of order $54$ --- yet they
are \emph{not isomorphic} as $G$-sets: the stabilizer of a shell vector
fixes no arc, and the orbital ranks separate them decisively,
\[
  \operatorname{rank}(\text{arcs}) = 24 = f,
  \qquad
  \operatorname{rank}(\text{shell}) = 40 = v,
  \qquad
  \operatorname{rank}(\text{joint}) = 24 .
\]
One count, two $G$-sets. This is the sharp $G$-set form of the edge/root
dictionary obstruction recorded after
Theorem~\ref{thm:axis-glue-e8-lift}: the Ihara-zeta carrier and the $E_8$
shell agree in cardinality and in stabilizer order but not in structure, so
no equivariant dictionary between non-backtracking transport and the root
shell exists even at the crudest level.
\end{theorem}

\noindent The executable certificate for all four statements is
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass158\_chiral\_trade\_lattice\_two\_480s.py}
($26$ exact checks, all passing; artifact
\texttt{data/w33\_pass158\_chiral\_trade\_lattice\_two\_480s.json}).

%% ===============================================================
\section{The Chiral Program: Five Executions}
\label{sec:chiral-program}
%% ===============================================================

Pass~158 opened five directions; Passes~159--163 execute all five. Each is
an exact finite computation with its own witness and JSON certificate.

\subsection{Pass 159 --- the sentinel space is the trade lattice, and the
dark modes are quantized}
\label{ssec:pass159}

The holonet security layer detects intrusion as a spectral anomaly against
the $g=15$ sentinel eigenspace. Pass~158 identified that eigenspace
integrally as the trade lattice; Pass~159 turns the identification into a
readout-security theorem package.

\begin{theorem}[Readout spectral law and readout/dark duality]
\label{thm:readout-law}
The context readout map $N$ (line--point incidence) has point-side Gram
$N^{\mathsf T}N=\mu I+A$ with exact spectrum
$\{16^{\,1},\,6^{\,24},\,0^{\,15}\}$: readout gain $2^{\mu}=16$ on the
Perron mode, $q!=6$ on the gauge sector, and $0$ on the sentinel sector ---
the sentinel eigenspace is precisely the readout kernel. The line-side Gram
$NN^{\mathsf T}=\mu I+A^{\vee}$ is the concurrence graph of the dual
$Q(4,3)$ quadrangle, again $\SRG(40,12,2,4)$ but nonisomorphic to the
point graph. The context-generated code lattice
$\operatorname{im}N^{\mathsf T}$ is saturated of rank $25$, and
\[
  \det(\operatorname{im}N^{\mathsf T})
  \;=\;\det(\ker N)
  \;=\;\bigl[\mathbb Z^{40}:\operatorname{im}N^{\mathsf T}\oplus\ker N\bigr]
  \;=\;2^{17}3^{10}.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[External balance and the sentinel code]
\label{thm:sentinel-code}
Every minimal dark mode is pointwise invisible outside its own support:
each of the $32$ off-support points sees exactly one $+1$ and one $-1$
support neighbour (all $90\times32=2880$ instances), so the $K_{4,4}$
crossbar alone carries the eigenvector equation. The mod-$2$ reduction of
the trade lattice is a self-complementary binary code with parameters
\[
  [40,\,15,\,8]_2,
  \qquad
  W(z)=1+45z^{8}+720z^{12}+6930z^{16}+17376z^{20}
  +6930z^{24}+720z^{28}+45z^{32}+z^{40},
\]
all weights divisible by $4$, whose $45$ minimum-weight words are exactly
the $45$ trade supports. Consequently \emph{any nonzero integer tamper
pattern of support $<8$ excites at least one of the $40$ measurement
contexts}: sub-$8$ anomalies are always context-visible, and the cheapest
context-invisible excitation is quantized at norm $8$ with exactly $90$
modes. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass159\_sentinel\_dark\_modes.py}, $18$ checks.)
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Pass 160 --- the trade tower: iterating the chiral construction}
\label{ssec:pass160}

The support geometry $\GQ(4,2)$ of Pass~158 carries the same abstract group
($\mathrm{PSU}(4,2)\cong\PSp(4,3)$, the exceptional isomorphism realized
geometrically), so the chiral construction iterates.

\begin{theorem}[The second trade lattice]
\label{thm:second-trade}
The $27$ lines of $\GQ(4,2)$ are recovered as the $5$-cliques of the
support graph ($3$ per point). Its trade lattice (zero sum on every line)
has rank $24=f$ (incidence rank $21$: the $27$ lines are integrally
dependent), is even with
$\operatorname{SNF}=\operatorname{diag}(1^{10},6^{13},30)$ and
$\det=2^{14}3^{14}5$, and its minimal shell has norm $6$ with exactly
$240$ vectors on $120$ supports, again in a single group orbit: each is
$\pm(\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{span}}-\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{perp}})$ over the $720$
hyperbolic pairs of $\GQ(4,2)$ (span and perp both $3$-sets). The dual
kernel --- relations among the $27$ lines --- has rank $6$,
$\operatorname{SNF}=\operatorname{diag}(6^{5},18)$, $\det=2^{6}3^{7}$, and
minimal shell of norm $12$ with $72$ vectors ($\pm1$ on $6{+}6$ lines).
\end{theorem}

\noindent\emph{Honest negatives.} The counts $720/240/120$ repeat the
substrate's moonshine/$E_8$-root/local-axis integers, but the $240$ trades
span the full rank-$24$ lattice (not a scaled $E_8$; inner-product profile
$\{\pm6{:}1,\pm2{:}27,\pm1{:}36,0{:}112\}$), and the $120$-support relation
graph is $56$-regular with a $63$-regular complement that is \emph{not}
strongly regular: the tower lands on a new $120$-object, not the $E_8$
root-line graph. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass160\_trade\_tower\_gq42.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 161 --- the Ihara prime propagates down the tower}
\label{ssec:pass161}

\begin{theorem}[Graph RH inheritance]
\label{thm:ihara-inheritance}
The support geometry $\SRG(45,12,3,3)$ is $12$-regular like $W(3,3)$
itself, so its Ihara prime is the same $k-1=11$. Its zeta function is
\[
  \zeta^{-1}(u)=(1-u^2)^{225}\,(1-u)(1-11u)\,
  (1-3u+11u^2)^{20}\,(1+3u+11u^2)^{24},
\]
both quadratic sectors sharing the single discriminant
$9-44=-35=-(\mu{+}1)\Phi_6<0$, and all $88$ complex Hashimoto eigenvalues
satisfy $|x|^2=11$ exactly: the chiral shell geometry satisfies the graph
Riemann Hypothesis on the \emph{same} critical circle $|u|=1/\sqrt{11}$ as
the substrate. The critical norm $\sqrt{11}$ is a tower invariant.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The three 540s]
\label{thm:three-540s}
The $540$ skew line pairs (the hypercube-chart atlas), % {540:line-nonedge}
the $540$ hyperbolic point pairs (the minimal-trade seeds), % {540:point-nonedge}
and the $540$ Hashimoto arcs of the % {540:gq42-arc}
support geometry are all transitive $\PSp(4,3)$-sets with stabilizers of
order $48$ --- and pairwise \emph{non-isomorphic}: no stabilizer fixes a
point of another carrier, and the orbital ranks separate them,
\[
  \operatorname{rank}=32,\;25,\;27,
  \qquad
  \text{joint ranks}=16,\;25,\;15 .
\]
Together with the two 480s, ``one count, many $G$-sets'' is a systematic
substrate phenomenon. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass161\_gq42\_ihara\_inheritance.py}, $20$ checks.)
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Complete census of the five degree-$540$ species] % {540:mixed}
\label{thm:five-540s}
Let $G=\PSp(4,3)\cong U_4(2)$, of order $25920$.  The table of marks of
$G$ has exactly five conjugacy classes of subgroups of order $48$, at
positions $77,\ldots,81$.  Therefore there are exactly five
non-isomorphic transitive $G$-sets of degree $540$. % {540:mixed}
In TOM order their
stabilizer structures and orbital ranks are
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|l}
77&((C_4\times C_2):C_2):C_3&25&
  \text{noncollinear point pairs}\\
78&C_2\times S_4&28&
  \text{nonincident double-six/cubic-line flags}\\
79&C_2^2\times A_4&27&
  \text{ordered Hashimoto arcs of }\GQ(4,2)\\
80&A_4:C_4&21&
  \text{the restricted }W(E_6)\text{ class }4C\\
81&C_2\times S_4&32&
  \text{skew line pairs}
\end{array}
\]
and their complete joint-rank matrix is
\[
\boxed{
\begin{pmatrix}
25&16&15&15&16\\
16&28&25&20&25\\
15&25&27&20&25\\
15&20&20&21&19\\
16&25&25&19&32
\end{pmatrix}}.
\]
The two abstractly isomorphic $C_2\times S_4$ stabilizers are
nonconjugate: their ranks are $28$ and $32$ and their normalizer orders
are $96$ and $48$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[The cubic $432+540$ complement] % {540:double-six-nonincident}
\label{cor:double-six-432-540}
The unique $S_6$ subgroup of index $36$ acts on the $27$ cubic-surface
lines with orbits $12+15$.  Consequently its full flag table splits
\[
\boxed{36\cdot27=36\cdot12+36\cdot15=432+540.} % {540:double-six-nonincident}
\]
The $432$ incident flags have stabilizer $A_5$ and are the projective
$A_5$ carrier of Pass~1137.  The $540$ nonincident flags have stabilizer % {540:double-six-nonincident}
$C_2\times S_4$ and form TOM species $78$, the formerly unnamed rank-$28$
carrier.  Thus the two carriers are complementary halves of one
double-six/cubic-line incidence object.
\end{corollary}

\noindent
In the full outer group $W(E_6)=U_4(2):2$, the three element classes of
size $540$ are $4A,2D,4C$; restricting their centralizers to $G$ gives % {540:mixed}
TOM positions $77,81,80$, respectively.  The exact GAP certificate is
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass1139\_complete\_degree540\_species.g}.

\subsection{Pass 162 --- the mod-8 anomaly ledger}
\label{ssec:pass162}

Each sector of the spectral splitting is an even positive-definite lattice,
so each carries a finite discriminant quadratic form whose normalized Gauss
sum is, by Milgram's theorem, $e^{2\pi i\,\sigma/8}$.

\begin{theorem}[The mod-8 ledger]
\label{thm:mod8-ledger}
Computing all three discriminant forms exactly (p-adic Smith generators,
element-distinctness certified, every $p$-part Gauss sum an exact eighth
root of unity):
\[
  \sigma(L_{12})=1,\qquad
  \sigma(L_2)=24\equiv 0,\qquad
  \sigma(L_4)=15\equiv 7\pmod 8 .
\]
The gauge sector is Brown-trivial (anomaly-free, $E_8$-like); the chiral
sector carries Brown invariant $7=\Phi_6\bmod 8$; and the ledger closes,
$1+0+7=8\equiv0$, the discriminant-form statement that $\mathbb Z^{40}$ is
unimodular ($v=40\equiv0\bmod 8$). The chiral sector's phase splits over
its primes as $\tfrac38+\tfrac48=\tfrac78$ (2-part and 3-part Gauss
phases), and its discriminant form has a unique $\mathbb Z/8$ Jordan block
(2-adic depth $3$, from the Smith invariant $24$) --- entirely absent from
the gauge sector, whose discriminant group is elementary --- whose
generator evaluates to
\[
  q(h)\;=\;\frac{88}{64}\;=\;\frac{11}{8}\ \bmod\ 2\mathbb Z,
\]
the Ihara prime over eight. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass162\_mod8\_anomaly\_ledger.py}, $41$ checks.)
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Pass 163 --- the two 480s decomposed: a dynamics/kinematics
selection rule}
\label{ssec:pass163}

The orbital ranks $24=f$ and $40=v$ of the two 480s are explained at the
level of irreducible characters, computed in GAP for the nine natural
substrate carriers as one combined permutation action on $2795$ points.

\begin{theorem}[Character selection rule]
\label{thm:selection-rule}
Write $\chi_{15}$ and $\chi_{15}'$ for the two degree-$15$ irreducibles of
$\PSp(4,3)$ and $\chi_{24}$ for the degree-$24$; then
$\pi_{\mathrm{points}}=1+\chi_{15}+\chi_{24}$ and
$\pi_{\mathrm{lines}}=1+\chi_{15}'+\chi_{24}$ (duality swaps the two
$15$s). The Hashimoto arc character and the eigenlattice shell character
share exactly \emph{five} constituents,
\[
  \{\,1,\;\chi_{15},\;\chi_{15}',\;\chi_{24},\;\chi_{81}\,\}
\]
--- the trivial, both eigenspace $15$s, the gauge $24$, and the Steinberg
representation $81=3^4$ (the protected-memory module). Neither spectrum
contains the other: arcs alone carry degrees $\{20,30,45,45,60\}$
(dimension sum $200=5v$) while the shell alone carries
$\{10,10,30,30\}$ (dimension sum $80=2v$), giving the exact splits
\[
  480 = \underbrace{280}_{|V|+|E|} + 200
  \quad(\text{arcs}),
  \qquad
  480 = \underbrace{400}_{\Theta v} + 80
  \quad(\text{shell}),
\]
where the common-core dimension sums are the substrate's configurational
total $280=|V|+|E|$ on the dynamics side and $\Theta v=400$ on the
kinematics side. Moreover the chiral $\chi_{15}$ is \emph{line-blind}: its
multiplicity is zero in every line-side and tower-side carrier (lines, skew
pairs, trade supports, $\GQ(4,2)$ arcs) and nonzero in every point-side
carrier (points, arcs, shell, trades, hyperbolic pairs) --- the sentinel
sector is invisible from the dual world. The full $9\times9$ intertwiner
Gram matrix independently re-derives every rank of Passes~158 and~161.
(Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass163\_two480s\_character\_decomposition.py},
GAP-assisted, $14$ checks.)
\end{theorem}

%% ===============================================================
\section{The Chiral Program, Second Round: Passes 164--168}
\label{sec:chiral-program-two}
%% ===============================================================

\subsection{Pass 164 --- the incidence tower carries the $\mathrm{SO}(10)$
shadow, with the $E_8$ shadow inside it}
\label{ssec:pass164}

\begin{theorem}[The rank-10 quotient form]
\label{thm:so10-shadow}
The sentinel code $C=[40,15,8]_2$ is doubly even, hence self-orthogonal
inside its dual, the context code $C^{\perp}=[40,25,4]_2$. The quotient
$H_{10}=C^{\perp}/C\cong\mathbb F_2^{10}$ carries the well-defined
quadratic form $q(x{+}C)=\operatorname{wt}(x)/2\bmod2$ with nondegenerate
polar form, and $q$ has exactly $\mathbf{528}$ isotropic vectors:
\emph{plus type}. Thus the incidence mod-2 tower produces
\[
  \PSp(4,3)\;\hookrightarrow\;O^{+}(10,2),
\]
a faithful embedding into the $D_5=\mathrm{SO}(10)$ orthogonal group over
$\mathbb F_2$ --- the GUT companion of the adjacency tower's
$O_8^{+}(2)/E_8$ embedding. The module is uniserial with filtration
$0\subset\langle z\rangle\subset z^{\perp}\subset H_{10}$ and composition
factors $1,8,1$ (cyclic-submodule profile: one vector generates the
isotropic fixed line, $510$ generate $z^{\perp}$, $512$ generate all of
$H_{10}$); and $z^{\perp}$ carries exactly $272=2\cdot136$ isotropic
vectors, so $z^{\perp}/\langle z\rangle$ is an abstract plus-type
$8$-dimensional quadratic space with the same $136/120$ split as
$E_8/2E_8$.  An explicit equivariant identification with the earlier
adjacency realization is a separate step. \emph{The
$\mathrm{SO}(10)$ shadow contains the $E_8$ shadow as its middle
composition factor: the $D_5\to D_4$ reduction, realized in the substrate's
own mod-2 homology.} (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass164\_so10\_shadow\_quotient\_form.py}.)
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Pass 165 --- the doily fusion: the trade construction meets the
Pass-71 codes}
\label{ssec:pass165}

Applying the trade construction to $W(2,2)$: the doily trade lattice has
rank $5$, $\det=1536=2^9\cdot3$,
$\operatorname{SNF}=\operatorname{diag}(2,4^3,12)$, even, minimal norm $6$
with exactly $20$ minimal trades --- the span/perp double-threes of the
$60$ hyperbolic pairs on $10$ supports --- while the $15$ ovoid differences
($W(2,2)$ has ovoids since $q=2$ is even; $A\mathbf 1_O=3\mathbf
j-3\mathbf 1_O$, so differences are integral trades) sit at norm $8$, one
per point ($15$ ovoid pairs, pairwise meeting in one point). Over
$\mathbb F_2$ the fusion with the doily track is exact: the trade code is
$[15,5,6]$ with enumerator $1+10z^6+15z^8+6z^{10}$ --- precisely the
\emph{even-ization} of the Pass-71 spread code $[15,5,5]$ (enumerator
$1+6z^5+15z^8+10z^9$; adjoin $\mathbf j$ and keep even words:
$6\!\leftrightarrow\!15-9$, $10\!\leftrightarrow\!15-5$, $z^8$ fixed), the
two codes sharing the $4$-dimensional spread/ovoid-difference core on
their respective sides of the self-duality. The chiral program and the
doily QEC track are one construction. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass165\_doily\_trade\_fusion.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 166 --- the 11/8 is a $W(3,3)$ miracle, not a law}
\label{ssec:pass166}

Testing the candidate law ``the deepest 2-adic Jordan block of a GQ trade
lattice evaluates to its Ihara prime over $2^{\mathrm{depth}}$'' across the
tower:
\[
\begin{array}{l|c|c|c|c}
 & \text{Ihara prime} & \text{2-adic depth} & q(h) & \text{law} \\\hline
W(2,2) & 5 & 2 & 1 & \text{no}\\
W(3,3) & 11 & 3 & 11/8 & \textbf{yes}\\
\GQ(4,2) & 11 & 1 & 0 & \text{no}
\end{array}
\]
The doily's trade discriminant is $(\mathbb Z/2)\oplus(\mathbb
Z/4)^3\oplus\mathbb Z/12$ with signature $5\bmod8$; the $2$-primary part
of the $\GQ(4,2)$ trade discriminant is elementary with signature
$0\bmod8$ (the full Smith group also has odd parts).  Only $W(3,3)$'s
chiral lattice carries $2$-adic depth $3$, and one chosen generator has
$q(h)=11/8$.  This value is generator-dependent: $3h$ has $q(3h)=3/8$.
Thus the table disproves a universal law but records a chosen-certificate
coincidence, not a canonical ``there and only there'' invariant.  The
Gauss sums use exact residue censuses with numerical eighth-root
recognition.
(Witness: \texttt{analysis/w33\_pass166\_ihara\_discriminant\_law.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 167 --- the sentinel theta and the context enumerator}
\label{ssec:pass167}

The exact MacWilliams transform of the sentinel enumerator gives the
context code $[40,25,4]_2$ in closed form without enumerating $2^{25}$
words: every context word has even weight (because $\mathbf j$ lies in the
sentinel code), and the minimum-weight words are counted by
\[
  A_4=40,\qquad A_6=240,\qquad A_8=5085,\ \ldots
\]
--- \emph{the $40$ lines are exactly the lightest legal traffic}, and the
$A_6$ coefficient is again $240$. Construction A on the sentinel code
yields an even rank-$40$ lattice of determinant $2^{10}$ with exact theta
opening
\[
  \Theta(q)=1+80q^2+14640q^4+3\,765\,440q^6+\cdots,
\qquad
  N(2)=2v,\quad N(4)=4\tbinom{40}{2}+2^8\!\cdot\!45 .
\]
(Witness: \texttt{analysis/w33\_pass167\_sentinel\_theta\_macwilliams.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 168 --- the second-shell scheme is rank 10, and the inner
product is blind to it}
\label{ssec:pass168}

The $240$ minimal trades of the $\GQ(4,2)$ trade lattice carry seven
inner-product classes (valencies $1,1,27,27,36,36,112$) whose relation
matrices commute pairwise --- yet the fusion is \emph{not} coherent: the
true orbital rank of $\PSp(4,3)$ on the $240$ shell is $\mathbf{10}$, and
the orbital coherent configuration (verified exactly, schurian by
construction) properly refines the inner-product classes:
\[
  36_{+1}=18+18,\qquad
  36_{-1}=18+18^{\mathsf T}\ \text{(an \emph{asymmetric} pair --- the
  configuration is non-commutative)},\qquad
  112_{0}=108+\mathbf 4 .
\]
The inner product does not see the finer orbital structure --- a
second-shell analogue of the two-480s obstruction --- and the split of the
zero class exposes a canonical \emph{4-valent invariant orthogonality
relation}: every trade has exactly four distinguished orthogonal partners,
descending to a $2$-regular invariant graph (a canonical cycle structure)
on the $120$ supports, whose orbital rank is $5$. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass168\_second\_shell\_scheme.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 173 --- the incidence transceiver and the route-dark
pentad lattice}
\label{ssec:pass173}

Let $N$ be the $40$-line by $40$-point incidence matrix, and let
$A_P,A_L$ be the point- and line-concurrence adjacencies.  Centering the
incidence operator removes the constant mode:
\[
  T=N-\frac1{10}J.
\]

\begin{theorem}[The shared-sector incidence transceiver]
\label{thm:pass173-transceiver}
The operator $T$ has rank $24$, intertwines the two concurrence graphs,
and has exact polar squares
\[
  TA_P=A_LT,
  \qquad
  T^{\mathsf T}T=6E_{24}^{P},
  \qquad
  TT^{\mathsf T}=6E_{24}^{L}.
\]
Thus $T/\sqrt6$ is an isometry between the common $24$-dimensional
constituents while annihilating constants and both unmatched
$15$-dimensional constituents.  For every one of the $480$ Pass-157
selectors $x$, the analyzer word $y=Nx=Tx$ has
\[
 \{-3^1,-1^9,0^{20},1^9,3^1\},
 \qquad \|x\|^2=6,\qquad \|y\|^2=36,
 \qquad x=\frac16N^{\mathsf T}y.
\]
If $X,Y$ collect the $480$ input and output words, then
$X^{\mathsf T}X=120E_{24}^{P}$ and
$Y^{\mathsf T}Y=720E_{24}^{L}$.  This is an exact algebraic
analyzer/decoder, not by itself a physical optical implementation.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The asymmetric address and route dark lattices]
\label{thm:pass173-dark-lattices}
The two rank-$15$ integral kernels are arithmetically inequivalent:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
 & L_{\rm address}=\ker_{\mathbb Z}N
 & L_{\rm route}=\ker_{\mathbb Z}N^{\mathsf T}\\ \hline
\det &2^{17}3^{10}&2^{11}3^{14}\\
\operatorname{SNF}&2^5,6^9,24&1,3^5,6^8,24\\
\min&8&10\\
\#\operatorname{Min}&90&432\\
\text{binary code}&[40,15,8]&[40,15,10].
\end{array}
\]
In particular
\[
 \frac{\det L_{\rm route}}{\det L_{\rm address}}
 =\frac{81}{64}=\left(\frac98\right)^2.
\]
The address code is self-orthogonal; the route code has binary Gram rank
$6$ and hull dimension $9$.  These invariants also give an integral
obstruction to incidence self-duality: a point--line duality would
permutation-identify the two kernels and preserve every entry of the
table.  In particular, the Pass-164 full quotient
$C_{\rm address}^{\perp}/C_{\rm address}$ and its $O^+(10,2)$ quadratic
form are address-chiral, because
$C_{\rm route}\not\subset C_{\rm route}^{\perp}$.  Pass~174 nevertheless
constructs the canonical route hull quotient
$(C_{\rm route}\cap C_{\rm route}^{\perp})/\langle\mathbf1\rangle$, a
plus-type $8$-space.  Exact MacWilliams transforms sharpen the separation:
\[
\begin{array}{c|rrrr}
 &A_4&A_6&A_8&A_{10}\\ \hline
C_{\rm address}^{\perp}&40&240&5085&47824\\
C_{\rm route}^{\perp}&40&240&3645&54736.
\end{array}
\]
The context systems agree through their first two nonzero shells and
first split at weight $8$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The pentad-core shell]
\label{thm:pass173-pentads}
Among all $13{,}824$ five-line partial spreads, exactly $432$ occur in
$216$ pairs with the same $20$-point cover.  Each pair $(P_+,P_-)$ gives
\[
 v_P=\mathbf1_{P_+}-\mathbf1_{P_-}\in L_{\rm route},
 \qquad \|v_P\|^2=10.
\]
The two pentads induce $K_{5,5}$ minus a perfect matching; the deleted
matchings double-cover all $540$ skew-line charts.  Live PARI/GP
Fincke--Pohst enumeration proves that $L_{\rm route}$ has exactly $432$
minimal vectors of norm $10$, so these are the complete shell.  Their
$216$ supports form one $\PSp(4,3)$ orbit with stabilizer $120$, while
the signed shell splits into two chiral $216$-orbits.  Hence the old
BT844--BT846 pentad/core carrier is intrinsically the projectivized
minimum shell of the route-dark lattice.
\end{theorem}

\noindent Witness and synthesis:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass173\_incidence\_transceiver\_route\_dark\_lattice.py}
and \texttt{PASS173\_INCIDENCE\_TRANSCEIVER\_ROUTE\_DARK\_LATTICE.md}
($36/36$ checks).

\subsection{Pass 174 --- the dual discriminant fixed rail and the
route-hull $E_8$ shadow}
\label{ssec:pass174}

For an order-eight discriminant generator $h$ and the outer involution
$\tau$, write $\tau(h)=c h+u_c$ with $c$ odd.  The v4 calculation found
the correct equality $[u_5]=[4h]\ne0$ but drew the wrong conclusion from
it.

\begin{theorem}[The fixed-rail correction]
\label{thm:pass174-fixed-rail}
On both dark discriminant modules,
\[
 [u_1]=0,
 \qquad [u_5]=[4h]\ne0
 \quad\text{in }H^1(C_2,A[2]).
\]
Hence the nonzero class obstructs only a pure scalar-$5$ form
$\tau(h')=5h'$; it does not obstruct a fixed generator.  Explicit
order-two shifts give $h'=h+v$ with
\[
 \tau(h')=h',\qquad q(h')=11/8.
\]
The exact comparison is
\[
\begin{array}{c|cc}
 &\text{address}&\text{route}\\ \hline
A_2(L)&(\mathbb Z/2)^{14}\oplus\mathbb Z/8
 &(\mathbb Z/2)^8\oplus\mathbb Z/8\\
\dim H^1&3&1\\
\#\text{ fixed shifts}&512&32\\
\#\text{ fixed shifts with }q=11/8&256&16.
\end{array}
\]
The complementary fixed shifts give $q=3/8$, so $11/8$ labels one
quadratic orbit rather than every order-eight generator.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The route-hull $E_8/2E_8$ shadow]
\label{thm:pass174-route-hull}
Let $R=\ker_{\mathbb F_2}N^{\mathsf T}=[40,15,10]$.  Its hull is
\[
 H=R\cap R^{\perp}=[40,9,16],
 \qquad
 W_H(z)=1+135z^{16}+240z^{20}+135z^{24}+z^{40}.
\]
The all-ones word is a fixed radical, and
\[
 \overline H=H/\langle\mathbf1\rangle\cong\mathbb F_2^8,
 \qquad q([x])=\operatorname{wt}(x)/4\pmod2
\]
is nondegenerate plus type with $136$ isotropic and $120$ anisotropic
vectors.  The native projective and outer actions are faithful of orders
$25920$ and $51840$ and have orbits $1+135+120$.  Objectwise, all $512$
hull words satisfy
\[
 c^{\mathsf T}Gc/4\equiv\operatorname{wt}(x)/4\pmod2,
\]
and $\mathbf1$ has lattice coefficient mask \texttt{0x7fff} and Smith
coordinate exactly $4h$.  Thus the code hull and route discriminant
quotients are the same quadratic object, not merely equal counts.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[One route code reconstructs the symplectic capstone]
On the $255$ nonzero vectors of $\overline H$, polar orthogonality gives
\[
 \SRG(255,126,61,63),
\]
and the quadratic split induces
\[
 \SRG(135,70,37,35)\ \sqcup\ \SRG(120,63,30,36).
\]
Hence the route code carries the whole chain
\[
 432\text{ signed pentads}\to216\text{ cores}\to
 [40,9,16]\to255=135+120,
\]
recovering the complete Pass-124 $E_8/2E_8$ capstone from one intrinsic
line-side code.  A chosen equivariant identification with every earlier
$E_8/2E_8$ realization remains separate from this exact abstract
quadratic-space and orbit theorem.
\end{corollary}

\noindent Witness and synthesis:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass174\_dual\_discriminant\_fixed\_rail.py}
and \texttt{PASS174\_DUAL\_DISCRIMINANT\_FIXED\_RAIL\_E8\_HULL.md}
($66/66$ checks).

%% ===============================================================
\section{The Chiral Program, Third Round: Passes 169--172, 175, and 176}
\label{sec:chiral-program-three}
%% ===============================================================

\subsection{Pass 169 --- the canonical cycles are forty octahedra, one per
line}
\label{ssec:pass169}

The 4-valent invariant relation of Pass~168 decomposes into $40$ connected
components of size~$6$ with spectrum $\{4,0^3,(-2)^2\}$ per component ---
each component is an \emph{octahedron} $K_{2,2,2}$ --- descending to $40$
triangles on the $120$ supports. The stabilizer of one triangle has order
$648$ and fixes exactly one \emph{line} of $W(3,3)$ (and no point):
\[
  \boxed{\ \text{the 40 octahedra of second-shell trades are the 40
  \emph{lines} of } W(3,3).\ }
\]
A curiosity with teeth: every pair of orthogonal trades has fully disjoint
supports, so the partner rule is invisible to intersection signatures ---
only the group sees it. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass169\_canonical\_cycles.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 170 --- the hidden-pair composition-factor match}
\label{ssec:pass170}

The 2-modular irreducible degrees of $\mathrm{U}_4(2)$ are
$[1,4,4,6,14,20,20,64]$: there is \emph{no} $8$ --- the $\mathbb
F_2$-irreducible $E_8$-shadow module splits over the splitting field as a
conjugate pair of $4$s, so $H_{10}$'s Brauer factors are
$\{1,1,4,\bar4\}$. The decomposition matrix (GAP, CTblLib) shows that the
two degree-$5$ ordinary irreducibles --- which appear
in \emph{none} of the nine permutation carriers of Pass~163 --- reduce mod
$2$ as $1+4$ and $1+\bar4$, so $5\oplus\bar 5$ reduces with exactly
$H_{10}$'s composition-factor multiset.  This did \emph{not} determine
the uniserial extension class of $H_{10}$ or prove a modular-module
isomorphism; an explicit module map or extension calculation was still
necessary.  \emph{[Closed by Pass 332: the three invariant index-two stable
sublattices of the rational restriction of the ATLAS $5_a$ each reduce to the
actual incidence $H_{10}$ through an invertible simultaneous intertwiner for
both standard generators.]}  The kinematic degree-$10$s instead reduce as $4+6$ and
$\bar4+6$.
(Witness: \texttt{analysis/w33\_pass170\_modular\_shadow\_brauer.py},
GAP-assisted.)

\subsection{Pass 171 --- the binary rank ladder at even $q$}
\label{ssec:pass171}

Testing the Levi rank theorem at five prime powers ($q=2,3,4,5,8$, plus a
$q=16$ anchor):
\[
\begin{array}{l|c|c|c|c|c|c}
 q & 2 & 3 & 4 & 5 & 8 & 16\\\hline
 \operatorname{rank}_2 M_q & 10 & 25 & 50 & 91 & 298 & 1890\\
 \operatorname{rank}_2 A_L & 5 & 10 & 17 & 26 & 65 & \text{---}
\end{array}
\]
The odd-$q$ theorem is independently re-verified at $q=3,5$. At even $q$
the self-duality of $W(3,q)$ forces
$\operatorname{rank}A_P=\operatorname{rank}A_L$, so only \emph{one} Gram
formula can survive --- and it is the line one:
$\operatorname{rank}_2A_L=q^2+1$ holds at \emph{every} tested prime power.
The incidence formula does not extend, and the even values
$10,50,298,1890$ refute the three-anchor cubic $(q^3{+}12q{-}12)/2$ at
$q=16$; the closed form is supplied by the Sastry--Sin transfer theorem in
Pass~178 below. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass171\_even\_q\_rank\_ladder.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 172 --- the 90-trade scheme and its eigenmatrix}
\label{ssec:pass172}

One level up from Pass~168's non-commutative surprise, the $90$ minimal
trades of $W(3,3)$'s chiral lattice behave perfectly: the inner-product
classes (valencies $1,1,32,32,24$) \emph{are} the orbitals (schurian rank
$5$), forming a symmetric commutative $4$-class association scheme with
exact first eigenmatrix
\[
P=\begin{pmatrix}
1&32&32&24&1\\
1&-4&4&0&-1\\
1&-4&-4&6&1\\
1&2&2&-6&1\\
1&8&-8&0&-1
\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
m=(1,30,20,24,15),
\]
whose multiplicities are precisely the character content
$1\oplus30\oplus20\oplus24\oplus15$ of the trade carrier from Pass~163.
The certificate is integral: all $125$ character-product identities in
the intersection algebra and
$P^{\mathsf T}\operatorname{diag}(m)P=90\operatorname{diag}(k)$ are checked
exactly, with no rounded eigensolve.
The antipodal class is exact negation, and the quotient's zero-relation is
$\SRG(45,12,3,3)$: the 90-scheme is the antipodal double cover of the
$\GQ(4,2)$ support geometry. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass172\_ninety\_trade\_scheme.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 175 --- the sentinel modular pair and the 720}
\label{ssec:pass175}

The dual of the sentinel lattice under the half form is exactly the
context construction, $L^{*}=\{y:y\bmod2\in[40,25,4]\}$, with
$[L^{*}:L]=2^{10}$ and quotient the $\mathrm{SO}(10)$-shadow module of
Pass~164. Its norm-$2$ shell has exactly
\[
  \boxed{\,720 \;=\; 2\cdot40 \;+\; 16\cdot40\,}
\]
vectors --- the $\pm2e_i$ together with all sign-lifts of the $40$ lines
--- the moonshine integer $T_{1A}+T_{2B}$ of Pass~71 and the weight-12
sentinel count of Pass~159. The next shell counts $15360=2^6\cdot240$: the
sign-lifts of the $240$ weight-6 context words. The dual is genuinely
non-integral (pairing profile $\{0,\pm\tfrac12,\pm1,\pm2\}$ with class
counts $378720/61440/7680/720$), so the $720$ is \emph{not} a closed root
system: a new connected, spanning $720$-object counted by the moonshine
integer.  Moreover the shell generates $L^*$ exactly: it contains
$2\mathbb Z^{40}$ and its reductions span the full $25$-dimensional
context code, hence its index in $\mathbb Z^{40}$ is $2^{15}$.
(Witness: \texttt{analysis/w33\_pass175\_dual\_shell\_720.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 176 --- incidence identifies the fixed-line reduction
with the route-hull $E_8$ shadow}
\label{ssec:pass176}

Let $C=\ker_{\mathbb F_2}M=[40,15,8]$ and
$R=\ker_{\mathbb F_2}M^{\mathsf T}=[40,15,10]$.  The address shadow
$H_{10}=C^\perp/C$ has a unique nonzero vector $f$ fixed by the native
$\PSp(4,3)$ action, and $q_A(f)=0$.

\begin{theorem}[The incidence fixed-line bridge]
\label{thm:pass176-incidence-bridge}
Incidence descends to an injective map on $H_{10}$, sends $f$ to the
all-ones route word, and restricts to a $\PSp(4,3)$-equivariant quadratic
isometry
\[
 \boxed{
 f^\perp/\langle f\rangle
 \xrightarrow{\;M\;}
 (R\cap R^\perp)/\langle\mathbf1\rangle .}
\]
Objectwise, for all $512$ classes $[x]\in f^\perp$,
\[
 \frac{\operatorname{wt}(x)}2
 \equiv
 \frac{\operatorname{wt}(Mx)}4\pmod2.
\]
The codomain is the Pass-174 hull $[40,9,16]$, with enumerator
$1+135z^{16}+240z^{20}+135z^{24}+z^{40}$.  Quotienting the fixed lines
gives the plus-type census $256=136+120$.
\end{theorem}

The $240$ weight-$6$ context words of Pass~175 represent all $240$
anisotropic elements of $f^\perp$.  Translation by $f$ pairs them into
$120$ fibres whose two representatives have disjoint supports, and $M$
maps them bijectively to the $240$ weight-$20$ route-hull words.  Polar
orthogonality on the fibres is
\[
 \boxed{\SRG(120,63,30,36)}.
\]
Thus the original incidence matrix supplies the explicit object map from
the Pass-164 $\mathrm{SO}(10)$ shadow through the Pass-175 $240$ to the
Pass-174 $E_8/2E_8$ anisotropic $120$.  The verifier checks all
$8\cdot1024=8192$ native-generator equivariance identities.  This is a
native $\PSp(4,3)$ theorem; it neither identifies the full automorphism
group of the graph nor yet supplies signed integral $E_8$ Gram products.
(Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass176\_incidence\_fixed\_line\_e8\_bridge.py}.)

%% ===============================================================
\section{The Chiral Program, Fourth Round: Passes 177--182}
\label{sec:chiral-program-four}
%% ===============================================================

\subsection{Pass 177 --- the address--route dual theta split}
\label{ssec:pass177}

Let $K_A$ and $K_R$ be the parity constructions from the \emph{dual}
address and route context codes, with exponent $q^{\lVert x\rVert^2/2}$.
Their exact openings are
\[
\begin{aligned}
\Theta_{K_A}&=1+720q^2+15360q^3+1350960q^4+\cdots,\\
\Theta_{K_R}&=1+720q^2+15360q^3+ 982320q^4+\cdots.
\end{aligned}
\]
The common $720=80+40\cdot2^4$ consists objectwise of coordinate doubles
plus sign-lifts of the $40$ lines on the address side and the $40$ point
stars on the route side; the common next shell is $240\cdot2^6$.  The
first split is entirely the weight-$8$ sector,
\[
1350960-982320=(5085-3645)2^8=\boxed{368640}.
\]
Thus non-self-duality is delayed for two dual shells and first detected
by the signed weight-eight sector. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass177\_address\_route\_dual\_theta\_split.py}
and \texttt{PASS177\_ADDRESS\_ROUTE\_DUAL\_THETA\_SPLIT.md}.)

\subsection{Pass 178 --- the even-order rank transfer theorem}
\label{ssec:pass178}

The even-$q$ item left open in Pass~171 is closed by the Sastry--Sin
theorem, in exact integral normal form: with
$B=\begin{psmallmatrix}4&2\\2&5\end{psmallmatrix}$,
\[
  \operatorname{rank}_2M(2^n)\;=\;1+\operatorname{trace}(B^n)
  \;=\;1+\Bigl(\tfrac{1+\sqrt{17}}2\Bigr)^{2n}
  +\Bigl(\tfrac{1-\sqrt{17}}2\Bigr)^{2n},
\]
matching all four measured anchors $10,50,298,1890$ and continuing
$12250,\,80018,\,524170,\,3437250$. A cautionary tale is recorded: a
four-anchor $\{1,6,13\}$-eigenvalue interpolation also fits the anchors
but diverges at $q=32$ ($12794\ne12250$) --- four anchors cannot separate
two order-three recurrences, and the theorem, not the fit, decides.
(Witness: \texttt{analysis/w33\_pass178\_even\_q\_closed\_form.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 179 --- the Poisson certificate of the sentinel pair}
\label{ssec:pass179}

For
$A=\{x\in\mathbb Z^{40}:x\bmod2\in S\}$ and
$B=\{z\in\mathbb Z^{40}:z\bmod2\in S^\perp\}$, parity pairing proves
$A^\vee=\tfrac12B$ and the code dimensions give
$\operatorname{covol}(A)=2^{25}$.  Poisson summation therefore proves,
for every $t>0$,
\[
  \sum_{x\in A}e^{-\pi t|x|^2}
  \;=\;2^{-25}\,t^{-20}\sum_{z\in B}e^{-\pi|z|^2/(4t)}.
\]
All $41$ MacWilliams coefficients and the inverse transform are exact;
shells are expanded through scaled norm $40$.  The three finite-window
evaluations near $t=1/2$ corroborate the normalization numerically but do not prove the infinite identity; exactness comes from the dual-lattice and
covolume argument.
(Witness: \texttt{analysis/w33\_pass179\_poisson\_modular\_pair.py} and
\texttt{PASS179\_SENTINEL\_CONTEXT\_POISSON\_PAIR.md}.)

\subsection{Pass 180 --- the $Q(4,3)$ dual-trade regularity boundary}
\label{ssec:pass180}

$L_{\rm route}=\ker_{\mathbb Z}N^{\mathsf T}$ is, by construction, the
point-trade lattice of the dual quadrangle $Q(4,3)$.  An exact span scan
gives the classical regularity boundary
\[
\operatorname{span}_{W(3,3)}(x,y)=4,
\qquad
\operatorname{span}_{Q(4,3)}(x,y)=2
\]
for every noncollinear pair.  This correlates with, but does not by itself
derive, the $90$-versus-$432$ lattice-shell dichotomy certified in
Pass~173.  The two quadrangles are a nonisomorphic dual pair, not an
incidence-self-dual geometry.

One selected Smith generator of the route $\mathbb Z/8$ block has
$q(h)=11/8$, but Pass~174 proves that outer-fixed generators split between
$11/8$ and $3/8$.  Thus $11/8$ exists on both sides but is not a canonical
generator invariant or an invariant of the dual pair. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass180\_dual\_trade\_lattice\_q43.py} and
\texttt{PASS180\_Q43\_DUAL\_TRADE\_REGULARITY\_BOUNDARY.md}.)

\subsection{Pass 181 --- the adjoint Hom census in characteristic three}
\label{ssec:pass181}

In the defining characteristic, with a certified generating pair of
matrix transvections and exact integral lattice actions:
\[
  \dim\operatorname{Hom}_G(\mathrm{ad},\,L_4/3L_4)=1,
  \qquad
  \dim\operatorname{Hom}_G(\mathrm{ad},\,L_2/3L_2)=1,
  \qquad
  \dim\operatorname{Hom}_G(\mathrm{ad},\,L_{\rm route}/3L_{\rm route})=0 .
\]
The reverse Hom spaces and all three fixed spaces vanish.  This is an
exact Hom census, not by itself an embedding theorem:
$\dim\operatorname{End}_G(\mathrm{ad})=1$ does not alone prove simplicity,
and a nonzero Hom need not be injective.  Pass~184 constructs the two
maps explicitly and proves their ranks are $10$; the route-side zero is
already conclusive here. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass181\_adjoint\_shadow\_mod3.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 182 --- the line-octahedron dictionary: blindness
quantified, and the constant-section rule}
\label{ssec:pass182}

Two halves. \emph{Blindness, quantified:} every orthogonal pair of
second-shell trades is support-disjoint, so the finest
invariant-theoretic relation is $56$-regular with $27{,}040$ triangles
--- the $40$ true octahedra are strictly invisible to inner-product and
support-intersection data, sharpening Pass~168. \emph{The dictionary,
made canonical after selecting the true orbital:} given a true
octahedron-triangle ($18$ binary-polar
octads), its line is the \emph{unique} line of $W(3,3)$ meeting all $18$
octads in exactly two points (the constant-section rule; profile
$2^{18}$, bijective onto the $40$ lines), and the axis/pair-partition
interaction table is exactly diagonal
$\operatorname{diag}(6,6,6)$: \emph{the three axes of the octahedron are
canonically the three pair-partitions of its line}.  All $40$ tables are
$6$ times permutation matrices; the line dictionary commutes with both
stored generators in $80$ cases and the $120$ axis labels in $240$ cases.
The four-valent orbital itself remains group-selected: inner products and
support intersections provably do not recover it.  The controller link is
only an abstract three-set alignment at this stage. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass182\_line\_octahedron\_dictionary.py}.)

%% ===============================================================
\section{The Chiral Program, Fifth Round: Passes 183--187}
\label{sec:chiral-program-five}
%% ===============================================================

\subsection{Pass 183 --- the discriminant ledger of the incidence square}
\label{ssec:pass183}

The exact census replaces the phrase ``the two-sided $11/8$.''  Every
order-eight discriminant element was enumerated.  On the address dark
form the numerator distribution modulo $16$ is
\[
  \{3:32768,\ 11:32768\},
\]
and on the route dark form it is $\{3:512,\ 11:512\}$.  The complementary
code forms have the exact negative distributions
$\{5:32768,13:32768\}$ and $\{5:512,13:512\}$.  Thus a selected
$11/8$ generator exists, but the invariant theorem is the complete
$\{3,11\}/8\leftrightarrow\{5,13\}/8$ negation orbit.  Milgram
eighth-root recognition remains numerical corroboration; the element
census is exact.

Since $\operatorname{SNF}(N)=1^{25}$, $N\mathbb Z^{40}$ is saturated.
The transport also obeys $N^TN=4I+A$ and the exact annihilator
$(A-2I)(A-12I)$ on $\mathrm{code}_P$ ($N$ scales the Perron line by $4$
and the gauge $24$-sector by $\sqrt6$).  Finally
\[
  0\longrightarrow \mathrm{code}_P
  \xrightarrow{\;N\;}\mathrm{code}_L
  \longrightarrow Q\longrightarrow 0,
\]
with index $2^{17}3^{10}=\det L_{\rm address}$ and cokernel invariant
factors exactly $(2^5,6^9,24)$.  Hence $Q$ and
$D(L_{\rm address})$ are isomorphic as finite abelian groups.  No
quadratic-form isometry between them has yet been constructed.
(Witness: \texttt{analysis/w33\_pass183\_incidence\_square\_ledger.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 184 --- the mod-3 factor table of the trade tower}
\label{ssec:pass184}

In the defining characteristic, the three exact sequences currently
proved are
\[
  0\to\mathrm{ad}_{10}\to L_4/3L_4\to Q_5\to0,
  \qquad
  0\to\mathrm{ad}_{10}\to L_2/3L_2\to Q_{14}\to0,
\]
and
\[
  0\to K_{14}\to L_{\rm route}/3L_{\rm route}\to\mathbf1\to0.
\]
All are nonsplit.  The two adjoint maps have rank $10$ and $Q_5$ is
irreducible by exhaustive $3^5$ cyclic generation.  By contrast,
$\dim\operatorname{End}_G(Q_{14})=1$ proves only that $Q_{14}$ is a
brick, not that it is simple; simplicity of $K_{14}$ is likewise not
inferred from its dimension.  The live GAP ledger separately verifies
all $20$ Brauer decomposition degree identities for degrees
$[1,5,10,14,25,81]$. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass184\_mod3\_trade\_factors.py}, GAP dec-3
ledger included.)

\subsection{Pass 185 --- the octahedron clock: a degree-three $S_3$-set}
\label{ssec:pass185}

The line stabilizer (order $648$) acts on its octahedron's three axes
with \emph{full image $S_3$}, the axes carry the three distinct
pair-partitions of the line, and the labeling is
$\PSp(4,3)$-equivariant in all $240$ generator cases.  The action kernel
has order $108$ and an axis stabilizer has order $216$, so the axes form
$S_3/C_2$: a faithful transitive three-set, \emph{not} a free $S_3$
torsor.  The committed controller data independently verifies
$4320\cdot3=12960$ and seed-action order $6$; this establishes the same
abstract fibre type, not a carrier identification. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass185\_octahedron\_clock.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 186 --- the pentad-core configuration and the 36
dodecads}
\label{ssec:pass186}

The antiregular counterpart of the second shell: the $432$ pentad cores
carry a PSp$(4,3)$ orbital configuration of rank $40$ with exactly two
shell orbits.  After transpose deduplication, its valency-$5$ sparse
relations consist of one family of $36$ twelve-vertex components and two
families of $36$ six-vertex components, all with stabilizer order $720$:
\[
  \boxed{\,36\ \text{twelve-vertex components with stabilizer order }720\,}
\]
Pass~188 proves, rather than assumes, that these are the two sixes and
their crown gluing. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass186\_pentad\_core\_scheme.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 187 --- the layer sandwich of the binary permutation
module}
\label{ssec:pass187}

One filtration generates the week's binary facts. The $\mathbb F_2$
permutation module on the $40$ points has the exact chain
\[
  0\;\subset\;\langle\mathbf j\rangle\;\subset\;C\;\subset\;
  \operatorname{im}A_2\;\subset\;\ker A_2\;\subset\;C^\perp\;\subset\;
  \mathbf j^\perp\;\subset\;\mathbb F_2^{40},
\]
with dimensions $1,15,16,24,25,39$ and layer sequence
\[
  \boxed{\,1,\;14,\;1,\;8,\;1,\;14,\;1\,}
\]
(the $14$s and the $8$ certified by exhaustive cyclic-span scans on one
representative of every nonzero-vector orbit, covering
$16383,255,16383$ vectors; the
fixed subspace is exactly $\langle\mathbf j\rangle$). Consequences read
off directly: the sentinel code $C$ is the bottom $1{+}14$; the
$\mathrm{SO}(10)$ shadow $H_{10}=C^\perp/C$ is the middle sandwich
$(1,8,1)$ with its fixed vector $f=[\operatorname{im}A_2]$; and the $E_8$
shadow is the central $8$-layer $\ker A_2/\operatorname{im}A_2$. On the
line side the filtration genuinely rearranges
($\operatorname{rank}_2A_L=10=q^2{+}1$, hull dimension $9$,
$\operatorname{im}A_L\not\subset C_{\rm route}$): the address/route
asymmetry is a \emph{filtration shift}, the same phenomenon the
Sastry--Sin $\sqrt{17}$ radical-series analysis quantifies at general
even $q$. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass187\_f2\_layer\_sandwich.py}.)

%% ===============================================================
\section{The Chiral Program, Sixth Round: Passes 188--192}
\label{sec:chiral-program-six}
%% ===============================================================

\subsection{Pass 188 --- the dodecads are exact double-six crowns}
\label{ssec:pass188}

The two directed valency-$5$ orbitals are transposes.  Their union has
exactly $36$ connected components, and every component is proved
combinatorially to be
\[
  K_{6,6}\setminus 6K_2,
\]
not an icosahedron.  The two symmetric valency-$5$ orbitals separately
give two families of $36$ copies of $K_6$.  Each crown bipartition is
bijectively one $K_6$ from each family, and every six is used once.  The
component stabilizer acts faithfully with order $720$ and induces the
full $S_6$ on either six.  Thus the route shell contains an internal,
$\PSp(4,3)$-equivariant double-six model; its exact characteristic
polynomial is
$(x-5)(x+5)(x-1)^5(x+1)^5$. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass188\_icosahedron\_test.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 189 --- the point permutation module is uniserial}
\label{ssec:pass189}

Pass~187's chain is the complete invariant-submodule lattice.  Exact Hom
computations in each successive quotient identify a unique simple socle,
while exhaustive vector-orbit scans certify the $14$- and $8$-dimensional
simples.  Therefore $\mathbb F_2^{40}$ is uniserial with composition
series
\[
  1\mid14\mid1\mid8\mid1\mid14\mid1,
\]
and has exactly eight invariant binary codes.  In particular the
$[40,15,8]$ sentinel is the unique invariant $15$-space,
$C^\perp/C$ is forced, and the central $8$ is the unique
$8$-dimensional subquotient.  The endomorphism fields are
$\operatorname{End}(14)=\mathbb F_2$ and
$\operatorname{End}(8)=\mathbb F_4$. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass189\_uniserial\_certificate.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 190 --- the mod-3 Steinberg composition census}
\label{ssec:pass190}

A live GAP calculation first proves that the computed permutation-group
table is permutation-equivalent to the CTblLib table for $U_4(2)$, then
transports ten permutation characters to its $3$-modular Brauer table.
For
\[
 (\text{points, lines, arcs, shell, trades, supports, skew pairs,
 hyperbolic pairs, }Q(4,2)\text{ arcs, flags}),
\]
the multiplicities of the Steinberg simple of degree $81$ are exactly
\[
  \boxed{(0,0,2,3,0,0,2,1,2,1)}.
\]
All ten complete multiplicity rows satisfy their exact dimension
identities.  This is a composition-factor census; it does not by itself
select an embedded logical register, code, or hardware protection
mechanism. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass190\_steinberg\_address.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 191 --- the $27+6+3$ double-six subdegrees}
\label{ssec:pass191}

The tempting $120\cdot36=4320$ controller identification is false as a
group action.  The product of octahedron axes and double-sixes splits
exactly as
\[
  \boxed{4320=3240+720+360=120(27+6+3)},
\]
with pair-stabilizer orders $8,36,72$.  This refutation exposes the
correct positive structure: the stabilizer of one axis has order $216$
and acts on its distinguished three double-sixes through full $S_3$,
with kernel $36$.  Hence every axis owns a native completion fibre
$S_3/C_2$.  It has the controller's abstract three-completion type, but
it lies over $120$ axes; the full $4320$ product has the right cardinality
and the wrong orbit structure. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass191\_supercycle\_pullback.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 192 --- signed trades are tetrahedral edges}
\label{ssec:pass192}

For a signed second-shell trade, its three positive octads contain one
unique pair of points on the matched line, while its three negative
octads contain the complementary pair.  This gives the explicit
equivariant bijection
\[
  \boxed{240\ \text{signed trades}
  \;\longleftrightarrow\;40\ \text{lines}\times6\ \text{edges}},
\]
verified in all $480$ generator cases; sign reversal is edge complement.
The line stabilizer $H$ of order $648$ acts on the six edges exactly as
$S_4$ on the two-subsets of a four-set, and
\[
  \boxed{1\longrightarrow C_3^3\longrightarrow H
  \longrightarrow S_4\longrightarrow1}.
\]
The kernel is checked elementwise to be abelian of order $27$ and
exponent $3$.  Quotienting edges by complement gives the axis action
$S_4\to S_3$.  Thus signed trades form $S_4/V_4$ and unsigned axes form
$S_3/C_2$; the six signed trades are not a regular $S_3$ frame. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass192\_signed\_trade\_edge\_s4.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 193 --- the sandwich is an even-line phenomenon}
\label{ssec:pass193}

Running the binary-filtration battery down the tower: for the doily
($3$-point lines, $\mathrm{Sp}(4,2)$, defining characteristic) the exact
dimensions are $(j,C,\operatorname{im}A_2,\ker A_2,C^\perp)=
(1,5,14,1,10)$ --- the all-ones vector escapes the trade code, the
adjacency mod $2$ is nearly nonsingular ($\ker A_2=\langle\mathbf
j\rangle$), and no sandwich forms; for $\GQ(4,2)$ ($5$-point lines) the
same collapse occurs ($\operatorname{rank}_2A=44$,
$\ker A_2=\langle\mathbf j\rangle$, and $C=[45,24]$ is not even
self-orthogonal). Only $W(3,3)$, with \emph{four}-point lines, has
$\mathbf j$ inside its trade code, $A_2$ a differential ($A_2^2=0$), and
the uniserial chain of Pass~189: \emph{the layer sandwich --- and with it
the sentinel code, the $\mathrm{SO}(10)$ shadow, and the $E_8$ shadow ---
is a property of the even-line geometry, singling out $W(3,3)$ within its
own trade tower}. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass193\_sandwich\_tower.py}.)

%% ===============================================================
\section{The Chiral Program, Seventh Round: Passes 194--198}
\label{sec:chiral-program-seven}
%% ===============================================================

\subsection{Pass 194 --- the corrected odd-\texorpdfstring{$q$}{q} shadow anchors}
\label{ssec:pass194}

For odd $q$, the SRG parameters $k-\mu=q^2-1$,
$\lambda-\mu=-2$, $\mu=q+1$ are even, so $A^2\equiv0\pmod2$ and the
$A$-homology shadow $H_q=\ker A_2/\operatorname{im}A_2$ is defined.  GAP
must compute the point code $C=\ker N$ as
$\operatorname{NullspaceMat}(N^{\mathsf T})$; using
$\operatorname{NullspaceMat}(N)$ instead gives the distinct line-side
kernel.  With the point-side convention, the nested sandwich and the layer
dimensions
\[
  1,\ d(q),\ 1,\ q^2-1,\ 1,\ d(q),\ 1,
  \qquad d(q)=\tfrac12(q-1)(q^2+q+2),
\]
are matrix-certified at $q=3,5,7$ (at $q=5$:
$1,64,1,24,1,64,1$).  The historical quadratic expression was off by a
factor of two: the actual refinement and its polar form are
\[
  \boxed{Q(x)=\tfrac14x^{\mathsf T}Ax\bmod2},\qquad
  B(x,y)=\tfrac12x^{\mathsf T}Ay\bmod2.
\]
At $q=3$ this is the plus-type dimension-$8$ form; at $q=5$ both $Q$ and
$B$ vanish on the dimension-$24$ shadow.  No general uniserial-module
claim beyond the certified anchors is inferred from the dimension formula.
(GAP witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass194\_odd\_q\_shadow\_ladder.g}.)

\subsection{Pass 195 --- the Steinberg projector, made matrix-explicit}
\label{ssec:pass195}

Pass~190 located the Steinberg module $\mathrm{St}_{81}$ with multiplicity
$3$ in the $480$-dimensional eigenlattice shell. Because
$|G|/81=320$ is prime to $3$, the ordinary Steinberg idempotent is
$3$-integral. Summing the Steinberg character against the shell
representation over all $25\,920$ group elements gives an explicit integer
operator
\[
  S=\sum_{g}\chi_{81}(g)\,\rho_{\mathrm{shell}}(g),
  \qquad
  S^2=320\,S,\quad \operatorname{tr}S=320\cdot243,\quad
  \operatorname{rank}S=243,
\]
so $P=S/320$ is an idempotent whose reduction $\bar P=2S\bmod3$ is an
$\mathbb F_3$-idempotent of rank $243=3\cdot81$: the holonet's protected
memory realized as an explicit $243$-dimensional direct summand of the
$E_8$-root carrier, not merely a multiplicity in a census. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass195\_steinberg\_projector.py}, GAP-assisted.)

\subsection{Pass 196 --- the two order-27 shells are the two parabolic
cores}
\label{ssec:pass196}

The dual-torsor compiler distinguishes a flat $\mathbb F_3^3$ program
shell from a Heisenberg $3^{1+2}$ state shell. Both appear as the
$O_3$-core of an order-$648$ parabolic: the line stabilizer's core (the
kernel of Pass~192's $S_4$ map) is the \emph{elementary abelian} $C_3^3$
--- the flat program shell --- while the point stabilizer's core is the
elation group, \emph{extraspecial} $3^{1+2}$ (nonabelian, centre $C_3$,
exponent $3$, regular on the $27$ opposite points) --- the Heisenberg
state shell. The compiler's program/state duality is exactly the
parabolic core dichotomy. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass196\_kernel\_flat\_shell.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 197 --- the double-six syzygetic graph}
\label{ssec:pass197}

The $36$ pentad dodecads of Pass~188 carry the classical rank-$3$
double-six action of $\PSp(4,3)\cong W(E_6)^+$: subdegrees $1+15+20$,
with the $15$-suborbit realizing the strongly regular
\emph{syzygetic graph} $\SRG(36,15,6,6)$ and the $20$-suborbit its
azygetic complement, each a constant line-sharing stratum. The
Schl\"afli double-six census is thereby materialized inside the route
shell. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass197\_double\_six\_syzygy.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 198 --- the layer law at \texorpdfstring{$q=7$}{q=7} and
the shadow dichotomy}
\label{ssec:pass198}

The third anchor $q=7$ ($400$ points) confirms the closed forms: layers
$1,174,1,48,1,174,1$, matching $d(7)=174$ and $q^2-1=48$, with the
$a_2$-rank theorem $\operatorname{rank}_2M=\tfrac12(q(q+1)^2+2)$
independently re-verified.  With the corrected refinement
$Q(x)=x^{\mathsf T}Ax/4$, its polar form satisfies the coefficient identity
\[
  \tfrac12A^2=\tfrac{q^2-1}2\,I-A+\tfrac{q+1}2\,J,
\]
which predicts the nondegenerate residue class $q\equiv3\pmod4$.  The
matrix anchors are exact: $q=3$ is plus type in dimension $8$, $q=5$ has
radical dimension $24$ and zero refinement, and $q=7$ is plus type in
dimension $48$ with Witt index $24$.  Values $q=11,13,\ldots$ are at this
stage coefficient-law predictions, not computed module certificates.  The
$\sqrt{17}$ Sastry--Sin transfer of Pass~178 governs the total rank; the
mod-$4$ arithmetic gives the candidate orthogonal-shadow dichotomy. (GAP
witness: \texttt{analysis/w33\_pass198\_layer\_law\_q7.g}.)

%% ===============================================================
\section{The Chiral Program, Eighth Round: Passes 199--203}
\label{sec:chiral-program-eight}
%% ===============================================================

\subsection{Pass 199 --- the q=7 shadow is a reducible plus-type
$O^+(48,2)$}
\label{ssec:pass199}

The dimension-$48$ middle shadow of $W(3,7)$ (Pass~198) carries a
nondegenerate plus-type quadratic form: Witt index $24$, Arf $0$,
$2^{47}+2^{23}$ isotropic vectors, preserved by the projective substrate
group $\PSp(4,7)$ of order $138\,297\,600$.  The historical generating
pair generated only an embedded $\operatorname{SL}_2(7)$, so its cyclic
submodule profile was not a full-group invariant.  Five standard
symplectic transvections generate $\Sp(4,7)$ of order $276\,595\,200$;
their projective shadow action is faithful, and GAP MTX gives the exact
composition-factor dimensions
\[
  \boxed{48=24+24.}
\]
Thus the $q=7$ shadow is reducible, but the former small-factor and
``unique irreducible rung'' extrapolations do not follow. (GAP witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass199\_q7\_shadow\_identity.g}.)

\subsection{Pass 200 --- the degenerate q=5 shadow is not Golay}
\label{ssec:pass200}

The $q=5$ middle is a $24$-dimensional module with a \emph{totally
degenerate} polar form (radical $24$), and the corrected quadratic
refinement itself is identically zero.  The historical $\{14,20\}$
cyclic-submodule profile came from an order-$120$ embedded
$\operatorname{SL}_2(5)$, not the full substrate group.  With five standard
transvections, GAP constructs $\Sp(4,5)$ of order $9\,360\,000$ and its
projective shadow image of order $4\,680\,000$; MTX proves the binary
$24$-module is \emph{irreducible}.  Consequently it has no invariant
dimension-$12$ Golay submodule.  The equality of dimensions with the Golay
length is only an analogy, not a Leech/moonshine identification. (GAP
witness: \texttt{analysis/w33\_pass200\_q5\_golay\_leech\_shadow.g}.)

\subsection{Pass 201 --- the sentinel CSS code and its logical shadow}
\label{ssec:pass201}

The sentinel trade code $C=[40,15,8]_2$ is doubly even and
self-orthogonal, so it defines a CSS code with $X$- and $Z$-checks both
equal to $C$:
\[
  \boxed{\,[[40,\,10,\,4]]\,,}
\]
and its $10$ logical qubits are \emph{exactly} the $\mathrm{SO}(10)$
shadow $H_{10}=C^\perp/C$ of Pass~164.  Here $H_{10}$ is the common
$X$/$Z$ \emph{label space}, not the whole Pauli space: the latter is
$H_X\oplus H_Z$ of dimension $20$, with its hyperbolic symplectic form.
The plus-type quadratic form on one label copy still gives the
$O^+(10,2)$ geometry, and the $40$ lines give weight-$4$ logical labels
(distance $4$).  Coordinate permutations preserving $C$ therefore give
weight-preserving code automorphisms.  Their exact Clifford lift, including
the distinction between $M$ on $X$ labels and $M^{-\mathsf T}$ on the dual
$Z$ labels, is established only in Pass~211; ``transversal gate set'' is
not used here. (Original witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass201\_sentinel\_css\_logical\_shadow.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 202 --- the shadow dichotomy as a Lean theorem}
\label{ssec:pass202}

The odd-$q$ sandwich reduces to four integral closed forms and one
congruence:
\[
  d(q)=\tfrac{(q-1)(q^2+q+2)}2,\quad
  \mathrm{shadow}(q)=q^2-1,\quad
  \operatorname{rank}_2M=\tfrac{q(q+1)^2+2}2,
\]
\[
  \text{coefficient-parity predicate}\iff q\equiv3\!\!\pmod4,
\]
with the seven layers summing to $v=(q+1)(q^2+1)$ for every odd $q$. These
are emitted as a Lean source file
(\texttt{formal/W33/ShadowDichotomy.lean}) whose stated theorems prove the
polynomial layer-sum identity and the equivalence between a
\emph{coefficient-parity predicate} and $q\equiv3\pmod4$.  The file does
not formalize $W(3,q)$, its permutation module, or the existence and
nondegeneracy of a quadratic quotient; those geometric statements remain
the GAP-certified $q=3,5,7$ anchors and higher-$q$ predictions. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass202\_shadow\_dichotomy\_arithmetic.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 203 --- the supercycle bundle}
\label{ssec:pass203}

Assembling the axis and double-six geometries: the product carrier
$4320=120\times36$ is \emph{not} homogeneous --- it splits into
$\PSp(4,3)$-orbits $3240+720+360$ --- but over each axis the $36$
double-sixes split $27+6+3$ under the axis stabiliser (order $216$), and
the distinguished $3$-orbit carries the full $S_3$ acted on by the flat
$C_3^3$ core (Pass~196). The supercycle ledger
\[
  51840=24\cdot2160=12\cdot4320=720\cdot72,\qquad
  2160=30\cdot72,\qquad 12960=3\cdot4320
\]
ties the packet clock ($72$), the mirror bus ($2160$), the product carrier
($4320$), and the runtime supercycle ($51840$) to the axis/double-six
geometry, with the $S_3$ completion fibre geometric and the
controller-carrier identification honestly not claimed at this stage;
Pass~212 later supplies the typed carrier and its explicit bijection.
(Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass203\_supercycle\_bundle.py}.)
