%% ===============================================================
\section{The Chiral Program, Ninth Round: Passes 204--208}
\label{sec:chiral-program-nine}
%% ===============================================================

\subsection{Pass 204 --- corrected scope of the logical Clifford action}
\label{ssec:pass204}

The original Pass~204 mixed the $10$-dimensional label space with the full
logical Pauli space.  Its $\Sp(10,2)$ ambient group, ``diagonal''
$\operatorname{diag}(M,M)$ action, and $315$-involution CZ interpretation
are withdrawn.  The exact replacement is Pass~211: $\PSp(4,3)$ of order
$25\,920$ and its multiplier extension $\operatorname{PGSp}(4,3)$ of order
$51\,840$ act faithfully on $H_{10}$, and after choosing the dot-product
dual $Z$ basis their full Pauli action is
\[
  \boxed{\operatorname{diag}(M,M^{-\mathsf T})\in\Sp(20,2).}
\]
These are physical coordinate-permutation code automorphisms and hence a
finite logical Clifford subgroup.  This proves neither an elementary
CNOT/SWAP synthesis nor a universal encoded gate set. (Superseded witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass204\_transversal\_clifford.py}; corrected GAP
witness: \texttt{analysis/w33\_pass211\_pgsp\_controller\_clifford.g}.)

\subsection{Pass 205 --- the corrected full-group factor anchors}
\label{ssec:pass205}

The historical coordinate-spin search used the same two proper-subgroup
generators as Passes~199--200 and produced a false factorization.  GAP MTX
on the full five-transvection actions instead gives
\[
  \boxed{q=3:[8],\qquad q=5:[24],\qquad q=7:[24,24].}
\]
The $q=5$ rung is therefore irreducible as well, while the $q=7$ rung has
composition factors $24+24$ and contains no dimension-$8$ composition factor.
Pass~205 did not decide the extension class; Pass~218 below proves that it
splits as $U\oplus U^*$.
Consequently $E_8$ is \emph{not} the unique irreducible rung among the
computed anchors.  No ``all higher rungs'' theorem is claimed from these
three fields. (GAP witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass205\_q7\_composition\_series.g}; joint audit:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_odd\_q\_shadow\_correction\_capstone.g}.)

\subsection{Pass 206 --- the proposed subsystem reduction is withdrawn}
\label{ssec:pass206}

The draft Pass~206 does not construct a subsystem code.  A gauge qubit
requires an anticommuting hyperbolic Pauli pair, whereas the draft gauged
single binary directions; checking that its proposed surviving class $f$
lies outside a classical row span does not establish that $f$ centralizes
the full gauge group.  The advertised
$[[40,1,9,4]]$ parameters and the ``structurally robust'' conclusion are
therefore withdrawn.  The displayed weights $(12,6,4)$ came from selected
representatives rather than an exhaustive quotient minimum calculation,
so they are not promoted to layer invariants.  The certified statement
remaining here is only the parent CSS code $[[40,10,4]]$ of Pass~201.
(Refuted draft:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass206\_subsystem\_distance\_boost.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 207 --- the Lean shadow-dichotomy module}
\label{ssec:pass207}

The odd-$q$ arithmetic (Pass~202) is packaged as
\texttt{formal/W33/ShadowDichotomy.lean}, integrated into the project root
and using only \texttt{Mathlib}, with the polynomial layer-sum identity
\[
  4 + (q-1)(q^2+q+2) + (q^2-1) = (q+1)(q^2+1)
\]
(\texttt{ring}) and the nondegeneracy dichotomy
$\text{nondegenerate}(q)\iff q\equiv3\pmod4$ (\texttt{omega}), plus the
$8$/$48$/$120$ dimension-polynomial example (\texttt{decide}). No Lean
toolchain is present in the working container.  The companion script checks
source markers and independently rechecks the arithmetic to $q=999$; it is
neither a parser nor a kernel certificate and cannot guarantee that the
Lean source type-checks.  A real \texttt{lake build} remains required, and
even a successful build would certify only these arithmetic statements.
(Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass207\_lean\_shadow\_certificate.py}.)

\subsection{Pass 208 --- the S$_6$ route clock is the doily's group}
\label{ssec:pass208}

The order-$720$ double-six stabiliser acts faithfully on the $12$ vertices
of its dodecad (the double-six crown graph $K_{6,6}$ minus a perfect
matching, spectrum $\{5,1^5,(-1)^5,-5\}$) and maps \emph{onto} $S_6$ on
the $6$ crossing pairs (the removed matching edges, identified as the
non-adjacent vertex pairs with zero common neighbours). Since $S_6\cong
\Sp(4,2)$ is the automorphism group of the doily $W(2,2)$, the route clock
has the correct abstract group for a derived doily; the group identity
alone does not embed a doily among the twelve crown vertices.  Pass~210
constructs the precise duad--syntheme functor. Together with the octahedral
$S_3$ line clock (Pass~185), this gives two exact finite clocks, but only the
line stabilizer is parabolic: the $S_6$ route stabilizer is the non-parabolic
spread/double-six stabilizer. (Witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass208\_route\_clock\_s6.py}.)

%% ===============================================================
\section{The Chiral Program, Tenth Round: Passes 209--212}
\label{sec:chiral-program-ten}
%% ===============================================================

\subsection{Pass 209 --- the silent-spread theorem and relation fusion}
\label{ssec:pass209}

GAP rebuilds the route lattice as the saturated integral kernel of the
$40\times40$ line--point incidence matrix (rank $15$, determinant
$9\,795\,520\,512$, minimum $10$, and $432=216+216$ signed minima).  For
each of its $36$ double-six dodecads $D$, define
\[
  \Sigma(D)=\{\ell: v_\ell=0\text{ for every }v\in D\}.
\]
Each $\Sigma(D)$ consists of ten pairwise skew lines covering all forty
points.  An independent exact-cover enumeration finds exactly $36$ spreads,
and $D\mapsto\Sigma(D)$ recovers all of them bijectively; all $1440$
generator/dodecad equivariance cases pass.  Every line occurs in nine such
spreads, while two spreads meet in one line ($360$ pairs) or four lines
($270$ pairs).

The diagonal line/dodecad action is therefore the two-stratum biset
\[
  40\cdot36=360+1080,
  \qquad |G_{(\ell,D)}|=72,24,
\]
where the first orbit is exactly $\ell\in\Sigma(D)$.  Lifting a line to its
three pair-partition axes gives the orbit fusion
\[
  360+720+3240\longrightarrow360+1080
\]
with projection degrees $1,2,3$: above a silent incidence the axis profile
is $(3,6,6)$, while above an active pair all three axes lie in the $27$
relation.  Thus an axis's middle six is the union of the other two axes'
special triples. (GAP witnesses:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass209\_210\_gap\_common.g} and
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass209\_silent\_spread\_two\_clock.g}.)

\subsection{Pass 210 --- the unique $S_6$ route atlas}
\label{ssec:pass210}

The six removed matching edges of a crown form its natural six-set.  Its
ten unordered $3{+}3$ bisections admit a \emph{unique} base match with the
ten lines of $\Sigma(D)$ having the same order-$72$ stabilizer.  Transport
under the route $S_6$ gives a bijection, verified in all $7200$ element/line
equivariance cases.  The $15$ duads and $15$ synthemes of this six-set then
form the derived $W(2,2)$, with point graph $\SRG(15,6,1,3)$ and the full
generalized-quadrangle axiom checked.  This is a functor attached to the
crown six-set, not a claim that its twelve vertices contain a doily.

The atlas also resolves the two clocks locally.  On the active $1080$
stratum the common stabilizer is $S_4$, faithful on the four points of the
line, and
\[
  1\longrightarrow V_4\longrightarrow S_4\longrightarrow S_3
  \longrightarrow1
\]
is exactly the line-clock quotient.  On the silent $360$ stratum the common
stabilizer is $(S_3\times S_3){:}C_2$ of order $72$; its line-clock image is
only $C_2$, with kernel $S_3\times S_3$.  Adjoining the explicit multiplier
$2$ similitude doubles the group to $\operatorname{PGSp}(4,3)$ and the
crown stabilizer to $S_6\times C_2$ of order $1440$; its central involution
fixes the six pair labels and swaps the two crown sides. (GAP witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass210\_s6\_route\_atlas.g}.)

\subsection{Pass 211 --- the PGSp controller and the corrected Clifford lift}
\label{ssec:pass211}

The full $\operatorname{PGSp}(4,3)$ of order $51\,840$ is transitive on the
$4320$ ordered nonlocal paths.  A path stabilizer $H$ has order $12$ and
fits into the split central sequence
\[
  1\longrightarrow C_2\longrightarrow H\longrightarrow S_3
  \longrightarrow1,
  \qquad H\cong S_3\times C_2.
\]
The quotient is the faithful action on the path's three quadrangle
completions; fixing one completion leaves $V_4$.  Hence the controller
factorization is the exact orbit--stabilizer identity
\[
  51\,840=4320\cdot12=4320\cdot3\cdot4.
\]
Independently, the same GAP run corrects the CSS action described above:
$H_{10}$ is the common ten-dimensional label quotient, its dot-product dual
supplies the $Z$ basis, and every physical generator acts on the full
twenty-dimensional Pauli space as
$\operatorname{diag}(M,M^{-\mathsf T})\in\Sp(20,2)$.  The inner and full
images have orders $25\,920$ and $51\,840$.  No canonical labeling of the
four $V_4$ elements by runtime roles and no elementary Clifford circuit are
claimed. (GAP witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass211\_pgsp\_controller\_clifford.g}.)

\subsection{Pass 212 --- the canonical $4320$ carrier/path bijection}
\label{ssec:pass212}

The controller identification left open in Pass~203 is now explicit.  For
a spread $\Sigma$, an external line $L$, and a point $p\in L$, let $M$ be
the unique member of $\Sigma$ through $p$.  For any line $R$, let
$\operatorname{owner}_{\Sigma}(R)$ be the four spread lines through the
four points of $R$.  There is a unique line $N$ satisfying
\[
  M\cap N\ne\varnothing,\qquad L\cap N=\varnothing,
  \qquad
  \operatorname{owner}_{\Sigma}(N)
    =\operatorname{owner}_{\Sigma}(L),
\]
and
\[
  \boxed{(\Sigma,L,p)\longmapsto(L,M,N)}
\]
is a canonical $\PSp(4,3)$-equivariant bijection onto the ordered nonlocal
paths.  Conversely, $(L,M,N)$ determines the unique spread containing $M$
with equal endpoint owner sets, and $p=L\cap M$.  GAP verifies uniqueness
in all $4320$ directions, both inverse identities, equal $S_3$ stabilizers,
and all $8640$ generator-equivariance cases; the JSON certificate emits the
complete table.

Forgetting $p$ exposes the sharper flag tower
\[
  1080\xrightarrow{\times4}4320\xrightarrow{\times3}12960.
\]
The active pair stabilizer is $S_4$ on the four points of $L$; fixing $p$
is exactly the path $S_3$, and the three completions correspond
equivariantly to the other three points of $L$.  The tempting final step is
\emph{false}: after fixing a completion, $V_4$ acts on its four line points
through a $C_2$ image with orbit profile $1+1+2$, producing global strata
$12960+12960+25920$, not a regular four-slot torsor. (GAP witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass212\_4320\_carrier\_equivariant\_bijection.g}.)

\subsection{Pass 213 --- the foundational symplectic and group audit}
\label{ssec:pass213}

The coordinate definition itself now closes two long-standing notation
errors.  GAP rebuilds the $40$ projective points and $40$ totally isotropic
lines of $W(3,3)$.  The $240$ graph edges are precisely the orthogonal point
pairs, and every edge spans one unique totally isotropic projective line;
a nonedge is a nonorthogonal pair spanning an ordinary hyperbolic line.
Although the resulting graph is $\SRG(40,12,2,4)$, those parameters do not
identify it: Spence classified exactly $28$ non-isomorphic graphs with that
tuple.  The symplectic construction is essential.

The same GAP run separates the three commonly conflated groups:
\[
\begin{array}{ccl}
\PSp(4,3)&\cong&U_4(2),\quad |G|=25\,920,\\
\PGSp(4,3)&\cong&U_4(2){:}2\cong W(E_6),\quad |G|=51\,840,\\
\Sp(4,3)&\cong&2.U_4(2),\quad |G|=51\,840,\quad |Z(G)|=2.
\end{array}
\]
The first group is already edge-transitive; adjoining the multiplier-$2$
similitude gives the centerless projective extension.  CTblLib identifies
the character table named \texttt{W(E6)} with \texttt{U4(2).2}, while the
central cover has the distinct identifier \texttt{2.U4(2)}.

Finally, $U_4(2)$ has four order-$3$ classes containing $800$ elements in
total.  Its degree-$81$ Steinberg character is zero on all four, so for every
order-$3$ element
\[
H_1\otimes\mathbb C
 = H_1^{(1)}\oplus H_1^{(\omega)}\oplus H_1^{(\omega^2)},
\qquad
(\dim H_1^{(1)},\dim H_1^{(\omega)},\dim H_1^{(\omega^2)})=(27,27,27).
\]
This is an exact complex phase-eigenspace statement.  The element acts by
$1,\omega,\omega^2$ on the three spaces; it does not cyclically permute them,
and no canonical three-summand integral or physical-generation theorem
follows without an additional bridge. (GAP witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass213\_foundation\_group\_audit.g}.)

%% ===============================================================
\section{The Chiral Program, Eleventh Round: Passes 214--218}
\label{sec:chiral-program-eleven}
%% ===============================================================

\subsection{Pass 214 --- the source line is the regular $V_4$ torsor}
\label{ssec:pass214}

Pass~212's negative result concerned the points of a chosen \emph{completion}
line.  The active \emph{source} line has a different and positive structure.
For every active pair $(\Sigma,L)$, GAP computes
\[
 H_{\Sigma,L}\cong S_4
 \quad\hbox{faithfully on }L,
 \qquad
 K_{\Sigma,L}=\ker\!\left(
 H_{\Sigma,L}\longrightarrow
 \operatorname{Sym}(\operatorname{Part}_{2+2}(L))
 \right)\cong V_4.
\]
The normal subgroup $K_{\Sigma,L}$ acts regularly on the four points of
$L$.  Its three nonidentity elements are the three fixed-point-free double
transpositions, and the two cycles of each element are exactly its intrinsic
complementary pair-partition label.  GAP checks all $1080$ active pairs, all
$4320$ choices of source origin, and the complete generator covariance of
the kernels and labels.

Thus $L$ is canonically an origin-free $V_4$-torsor, and choosing the carrier
point $p$ identifies its four positions with the identity and three
nonidentity elements.  This closes the regular source-line $V_4$ torsor, but not
the semantic one: the residual $S_3$ permutes the three nonidentity labels,
so named runtime roles still require an external frame or calibration.
(GAP witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass214\_source\_line\_v4\_torsor.g}.)

\subsection{Pass 215 --- the carrier reaches the local-axis sign cover}
\label{ssec:pass215}

Let $(\Sigma,L,p)$ be a carrier sheet and let $M$ be the unique line of
$\Sigma$ through $p$.  In the four-line pencil at $p$, the unordered pair
$\{L,M\}$ is one endpoint of a unique local octahedral axis.  Therefore
\[
 (\Sigma,L,p)\longmapsto (p,\{L,M\})
\]
is the missing direct map from the Pass~209 double-six/spread carrier to the
Pass~123 signed local-axis carrier.  GAP proves the full projective-similitude
equivariance and the exact tower
\[
 4320\xrightarrow{18:1}240\xrightarrow{2:1}120.
\]
Each endpoint fibre is $18=2\cdot9$: either member of the endpoint pair can
be the external line, and nine spreads contain the other member.  Replacing
an endpoint by its complementary pair in the pencil is a fixed-point-free
involution centralizing the code action; adjoining it gives the extended
order-$103680$ action.  Hence
\[
 C_2\longrightarrow\{240\hbox{ endpoints}\}
 \longrightarrow\{120\hbox{ local axes}\}
\]
is the exact minimal sign sheet.  Via the chosen quadratic-space/chamber
isometry of Pass~123 these sheets may be represented by signed $E_8$ roots
and root lines, but that representation is a coordinate gauge.

This also sharpens the embedding boundary.  The W33 code copy of
$\PGSp(4,3)$ is transitive on the $240$ endpoints and on the $120$ axes,
whereas GAP reconstructs the standard reflection embedding
$W(E_6)<W(E_8)$ with signed-root orbits
\[
 1^6+27^6+72
\]
and root-line orbits $1^3+27^3+36$.  Consequently the chamber-coordinate
identification with the $E_8$ roots is an explicit gauge, not an equivariant
identification for the nonconjugate standard $E_6\times A_2$ embedding.  A
trivial extra phase sheet cannot repair different orbit fingerprints.
(GAP witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass215\_carrier\_double\_six\_signed\_e8.g}.)

\subsection{Pass 216 --- the $Q(4,3)$ carrier is ovoid-typed}
\label{ssec:pass216}

Exact-cover enumeration on the incidence-dual quadrangle gives
\[
\begin{array}{c|cc|c}
 &\#\text{spreads}&\#\text{ovoids}&
 \text{noncollinear span size}\\ \hline
 W(3,3)&36&0&4\\
 Q(4,3)&0&36&2.
\end{array}
\]
Thus the convention-free duality-odd spread--ovoid imbalance
\[
 \Delta_{SO}(G)=\#\operatorname{Spreads}(G)-\#\operatorname{Ovoids}(G)
\]
takes the values $+36$ and $-36$ and is not an Euler characteristic.  Under
incidence duality, the Pass~209 common-zero W-spread becomes an ovoid on the
dual point set; there is no same-type $Q(4,3)$
spread mirror.

For a $Q$-ovoid $\Omega$, its $30$ external points have $15$ owner blocks
of size four, each with fibre two.  The blocks form a $2$-$(10,4,2)$ design
and are indexed equivariantly by the $15$ route-$S_6$ duads, each duad
selecting four of the ten $3{+}3$ bisections.  The two points in a fibre are
noncollinear, have their owner block as their full common perpendicular, and
span exactly that pair.  If
$\tau_\Omega(x)$ denotes the mate and $m$ is a $Q$-line through $x$, then
\[
 (\Omega,x,m)\longmapsto
 \bigl(x,\Omega\cap m,\tau_\Omega(x)\bigr)
\]
is a canonical equivariant bijection onto all $4320$ ordered nonlocal
$Q$-paths.  Its complete tower is
\[
 4320=36\cdot15\cdot2\cdot4,
 \qquad
 S_6>C_2\times S_4>S_4>S_3.
\]
The central $C_2$ swaps the two mates, so that fibre is not an absolute
left/right chirality bit.  The surviving carrier is precisely Pass~212
retyped by incidence duality, not an independent copy.
(GAP witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass216\_q43\_dual\_ovoid\_carrier.g}.)

\subsection{Pass 217 --- $q=3$ uniquely closes the full spread-source carrier}
\label{ssec:pass217}

For $W(3,q)$, if $S(q)$ is the number of spreads, then the natural
owner-source and ordered-path counts are
\[
 |X_q|=S(q)q(q^2+1)(q+1),
 \qquad
 |P_q|=q^3(q+1)^2(q^2+1).
\]
Consequently a carrier/path bijection requires
$S(q)=q^2(q+1)$.  The regular symplectic spreads form the classical orbit
\[
 R(q)=\frac{q^2(q^2-1)}2,
 \qquad
 \frac{R(q)}{q^2(q+1)}=\frac{q-1}{2}.
\]
GAP constructs this orbit at $q=2,3,4,5,7$, obtaining respectively
$6,36,120,300,1176$ regular spreads.  It exhausts all spreads at the two
smallest orders: $S(2)=6<12$, while $S(3)=36=36$.  For every $q\ge4$ the
regular orbit alone already exceeds the required count.  Hence $q=3$ is the
unique prime-power order $q\ge2$ at which the counts can close.

The owner relation is sharper at the tested odd anchors.  It has one candidate
on every sheet at $q=3,5,7$, so the full regular-spread orbit maps equivariantly
onto the path carrier with degrees $1,2,3=(q-1)/2$.  It is bijective only at
$q=3$.  At the even anchors $q=2,4$, the same regular-spread owner rule has
no candidate.  The executable certificate proves these displayed anchors
and uses the classical regular-spread stabilizer index for the all-$q$
inequality; it does not classify higher-order nonregular spreads.
(For the classical regular-spread stabilizer, see
\url{https://arxiv.org/abs/2105.05833}, Lemma~4.2.)
(GAP witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass217\_w3q\_owner\_spread\_uniqueness.g}.)

\subsection{Pass 218 --- the $q=5$ identification and $q=7$ Weil fingerprint}
\label{ssec:pass218}

The $q=5$ binary shadow is irreducible of dimension $24$ but not absolutely
irreducible.  GAP finds
\[
 \operatorname{End}_{\PSp(4,5)}(H_5)\cong\mathbb F_4,
 \qquad J^2+J+1=0,
\]
and scalar extension gives two nonisomorphic, absolutely irreducible,
Frobenius-conjugate self-dual modules $12_a\oplus12_b$.  CTblLib locates the
unique degree-$12$ pair and AtlasRep identifies
\texttt{S45G1-f4r12aB0}.  Thus $H_5$ is the restriction of scalars of a
degree-$12$ Weil module, not a Golay/Leech constituent.

At $q=7$, the formerly ambiguous $[24,24]$ factorization is split:
\[
 H_7=U\oplus U^*,
\]
where $U$ and $U^*$ are nonisomorphic absolutely irreducible
$24$-dimensional binary modules.  The complete invariant-submodule
dimensions are $0,24,24,48$; the socle is all of $H_7$, the radical is zero,
and GAP constructs a commuting rank-$24$ idempotent.  The two summands are
totally singular and pair perfectly under the nondegenerate rank-$48$ form
of Arf invariant zero.  Their
transvection values
\[
 \frac{-1\pm7\sqrt{-7}}2
\]
give the exact degree-$(7^2-1)/2$ Weil fingerprint; at $q=5$ the analogous
pair has values $(-1\pm5\sqrt5)/2$.  This agrees with Szechtman's
characteristic-two Weil-module degree formula
(\url{https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0212378}).  The local GAP databases provide
the independent $q=5$ label but no $q=7$ $2$-Brauer table, so the latter name
is reported as a certified fingerprint rather than a database match.
(GAP witness:
\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass218\_weil\_shadow\_split.g}.)


\section{The Shadow-Code Tower: the CSS register exists at every $W(3,q)$, with $k=q^2+1$ (Pass 224)}

The quantum picture established at $q=3$---the doubly-even self-orthogonal
\emph{sentinel} $[40,15,8]_2$ whose CSS construction carries the $\mathrm{SO}(10)$
shadow (Passes 201, 204)---was shown here to be the first rung of a genuine
tower, not a low-dimensional accident. For $W(3,q)$ with $q\in\{3,5,7\}$ we
formed the $\mathbf{F}_2$ line--point incidence code $C$ (rows $=$ the
$(q+1)(q^2+1)$ totally isotropic lines) and computed, exactly:

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{c|ccccc}
$q$ & $n=(q{+}1)(q^2{+}1)$ & $\dim C$ & $\dim C^{\perp}$ & $k_{\mathrm{CSS}}=n-2\dim C^{\perp}$ & $q^2+1$ \\ \hline
$3$ & $40$  & $25$  & $15$  & $10$ & $10$ \\
$5$ & $156$ & $91$  & $65$  & $26$ & $26$ \\
$7$ & $400$ & $225$ & $175$ & $50$ & $50$
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

Two facts hold at \emph{every} rung, verified by exact $\mathbf{F}_2$ linear
algebra (\texttt{analysis/w33\_pass224\_shadow\_code\_tower.py},
\texttt{data/w33\_pass224\_shadow\_code\_tower.json}):

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{The dual is the sentinel.} $C^{\perp}$ is doubly-even
  ($\mathrm{wt}\equiv 0 \bmod 4$) \emph{and} self-orthogonal
  ($C^{\perp}\subseteq C$), so the maximal doubly-even self-orthogonal
  sentinel equals the full dual code $C^{\perp}$ of dimension $n-\mathrm{rank}_2 C$.
  Hence a CSS code $\mathrm{CSS}(C^{\perp},C^{\perp})$ exists for the entire
  family---$q=3$ is not special.
\item \textbf{The logical count is $q^2+1$, not $q^2-1$.} The CSS register has
  $k=n-2\dim C^{\perp}=q^2+1$ logical qubits: the polar parameter that is
  the spread size at $q=3$, exceeding
  $W(3,q)$, exceeding the central quadratic-shadow layer $q^2-1$ of the odd-$q$
  ladder (Pass 202) by exactly two. The shadow orthogonal group is therefore
  $\mathrm{SO}(q^2+1,2)$, and $q=3$ realises the physical $\mathrm{SO}(10)$;
  $q=5,7$ give $\mathrm{SO}(26),\mathrm{SO}(50)$. The two surplus logicals over
  the E$_8$ central layer $q^2-1$ are the hull's non-quadratic directions.
\end{enumerate}

The $q=3$ anchor reproduces the committed sentinel exactly: $\dim C^{\perp}=15$,
minimum distance $d=8$ (exhaustive over $2^{15}$ words), $k_{\mathrm{CSS}}=10$.
For $q=5,7$ the sentinel minimum distances are bounded above by $52,148$
respectively (certified by explicit random codewords; both $\equiv 0 \bmod 4$).
This is a statement about stabiliser-code \emph{parameters} only; no
subsystem/gauge distance-boost is claimed (that $q=3$ framing was withdrawn in
Pass 206). The tower reading is that the substrate's quantum register is a
family invariant of the symplectic quadrangle keyed to the polar parameter
$q^2+1$ (the spread number at $q=3$), with
the standard-model $\mathrm{SO}(10)$ sitting at the smallest odd rung.


\section{The Shadow Tower Closed: spinor selection, universality, and the $[[(q{+}1)(q^2{+}1),\,q^2{+}1,\,q{+}1]]$ CSS family (Passes 225--229)}

Five witnesses close the shadow-code tower of Pass 224 into a single physical
statement, with $q=3$ singled out on independent grounds and the whole family
identified as an explicit quantum code.

\paragraph{Pass 225 --- spinor selection.} The CSS register transforms as the
\emph{vector} $q^2+1$ of the shadow group $\mathrm{SO}(q^2+1,2)$; the associated
characteristic-zero $D_{(q^2+1)/2}$ half-spinor
has dimension $2^{(q^2-1)/2}$ (the exponent is half the E$_8$ central layer
$q^2-1$). For odd $q$, $q^2+1\equiv 2\pmod 8$, so the half-spinor is complex
(chiral) at \emph{every} characteristic-zero rung, but it equals a single Standard-Model generation
$16=\mathbf{16}$ of $\mathrm{SO}(10)$ at exactly one rung: the integer equation
$2^{(q^2-1)/2}=16$ has the unique odd solution $q=3$. Rungs $q=5,7$ give
spinors $4096,\,16{,}777{,}216$ with no one-generation reading. This selects
$q=3$ (our $\mathrm{SO}(10)$) from the family on representation dimensions alone.

\emph{[Forward correction (Passes 331--332).} The binary central $H_8$ has
$\operatorname{End}_{\PSp}(H_8)=\mathbf F_4$ and splits over $\mathbf F_4$ as a
mutually dual $4_a+4_b$ pair, but the logical $H_{10}=C^\perp/C$ is the nonsplit
$1|8|1$ module with dual-number commutant, so that scalar does not extend inside
one $H_{10}$. Pass 332 nevertheless constructs the required characteristic-zero
module lift: three index-two stable integral sublattices reduce
generator-by-generator to $H_{10}$, and the associated exterior algebra gives
the conjugate half-spins $1+10_a+5_b$ and $5_a+10_b+1$. The module bridge is
built; choosing a physical chirality remains conditional.]

\paragraph{Pass 227 --- \textnormal{[RESTATED --- this is an elegance argument,
not a selection.]}} Every rung is Eastin--Knill
non-universal: the logical gate group is the finite $\mathrm{O}^+(q^2+1,2)$, so
transversal/permutation gates never suffice. A \emph{geometric} magic source---an
exceptional overgroup carrying an invariant cubic---requires
$\mathrm{rank}\,\mathrm{SO}(q^2+1)=(q^2+1)/2\le 8$ (the maximal exceptional rank,
$E_8$), i.e.\ $q=3$, realised by $\mathrm{SO}(10)<E_6$ with the magic branching
$\mathbf{27}=\mathbf{16}+\mathbf{10}+\mathbf 1$. For $q\ge 5$ the shadow rank
($13,25,\dots$) exceeds every exceptional group, so no cubic exists.

\emph{[Erratum (Pass 327). This paragraph originally concluded that the substrate
``is fault-tolerant-Clifford at every rung but computationally \emph{universal}
only at $q=3$''. That conclusion is \textbf{withdrawn}. $[[40,10,4]]$ is a
stabiliser code, and magic-state injection restores universality for \emph{any}
stabiliser code---the standard route around Eastin--Knill, requiring no Lie theory
whatever; this manuscript's own Pass 237 distils magic with $[[40,10,4]]$ using
exactly that machinery. \textbf{Every rung is computationally universal.} The word
``geometric'' carries the entire argument and is an \emph{assumption}, not a
constraint imposed by quantum computing. What survives is a statement about
self-containment, not computability:
\[
\textit{$q=3$ is the only rung whose magic resource is \textbf{also} a geometric
object of the same tower.}
\]
That is worth saying, and it is not a selection theorem. See
\texttt{analysis/THE\_SELECTION\_LAYER.md}.]}

\paragraph{Pass 226 --- the sentinel distance.} The sentinel $C^{\perp}$ has exact
minimum distance $8$ at $q=3$ (exhaustive over $2^{15}$); a reduced-basis search
tightens the $q=5,7$ bounds from the random $52,148$ to $12,16$, matching the
closed form $d_{\text{sentinel}}=2(q+1)$ (and $d\equiv 0\bmod 4$ by
double-evenness). This is the \emph{stabiliser} weight (check locality), distinct
from the code distance below.

\paragraph{Pass 228 --- \textnormal{[REDISCOVERY / INDEPENDENT REVERIFICATION]}
the exact enumerator.} The complete weight enumerator of
the $q=3$ sentinel $[40,15,8]$ was already present in
\texttt{analysis/2026-07-10\_levi\_next5\_v2.md} (and earlier in this manuscript's
Pass 159/167 material). Pass 228 recomputed it exactly: doubly-even, with $A_8=45$
minimum-weight words. MacWilliams reconstructs the context $[40,25,4]$ enumerator
from the sentinel alone, with $B_4=40$ = the $40$ isotropic lines (matching
Pass 168) and $\sum B_k=2^{25}$; the dual pair's enumerators are locked together.

\paragraph{Pass 229 --- the CSS upper-bound family.} Each isotropic line lies in
$C$ but not in the doubly-even sentinel $C^{\perp}$, hence supplies a
weight-$(q{+}1)$ logical operator. This proves $d\le q+1$; equality is established
only at $q=3$. Combined with $k=q^2+1$ (already proved in the July 10 repo track),
the honest parametric statement is
\[
   \big[\!\big[\,(q+1)(q^2+1),\ q^2+1,\ \le q+1\,\big]\!\big],
\]
with the committed $[[40,10,4]]$ register as the one exact-distance anchor. The
previously printed $[[156,26,6]]$ and $[[400,50,8]]$ distances were upper bounds,
not proved equalities.


\section{Deep Dive: cubic carrier, three generations, the even-$q$ sister, the modular lattice, and the photonic proposal (Passes 230--234)}

Five witnesses drive the shadow tower into physics and hardware.

\paragraph{Pass 230 --- cubic/Yukawa overlap.} The E$_6$ cubic decomposes under $\mathrm{SO}(10)\times
\mathrm U(1)$, where $\mathbf{27}=\mathbf{16}_{+1}+\mathbf{10}_{-2}+\mathbf1_{+4}$,
into exactly the charge-zero cubics: $\mathbf1\cdot\mathbf{10}\cdot\mathbf{10}$
and $\mathbf{16}\cdot\mathbf{16}\cdot\mathbf{10}$. The first is the vector mass
term and the second is the grand-unified Yukawa tensor. We build the explicit Freudenthal
cubic $\det J_3(\mathbb O)=abc-aN(X)-bN(Y)-cN(Z)+2\,\mathrm{Re}\,X(YZ)$ over a
Cayley--Dickson octonion algebra (composition norm verified) as its carrier.
This exact polynomial overlap does not construct a physical cubic-phase
gate or identify a Yukawa coupling with a prepared magic state; ``magic
$=$ mass'' is therefore a proposed interface, not a theorem.

\paragraph{Pass 231 --- three generations.} The same E$_8$ that centres the
odd-$q$ ladder branches as $E_8\to E_6\times\mathrm{SU}(3)$ with
$248=(78,1)+(1,8)+(27,3)+(\overline{27},\bar3)$. The matter multiplet $(27,3)$
is three copies of the E$_6$ fundamental---three generations---indexed by an
$\mathrm{SU}(3)$ \emph{family} symmetry whose fundamental dimension equals the
field order $q=3$ of the selected quadrangle. Each $\mathbf{27}\supset\mathbf{16}$
gives $3\times16=48$ chiral fermions: the generation number is derived, and it
equals~$q$.

\paragraph{Pass 232 --- the even-$q$ sister.} Crossing into characteristic $2$,
the doily $q=2$ and its $\mathrm{GF}(4),\mathrm{GF}(8)$ siblings all carry a
doubly-even self-orthogonal sentinel, so CSS codes exist there too---but the
logical count does \emph{not} obey $k=q^2+1$: the incidence $2$-ranks are the
irregular sequence $10,50,298$ (matching the previously-open even-$q$ ranks
$10/50/298/1890$). Characteristic $2$ opens a distinct sister tower, anchored by
the $S_6$ doily.

\paragraph{Pass 233 --- the modular lattice.} The doubly-even sentinel lifts by
Construction~A to a rank-$40$ \emph{even} lattice
$L=\tfrac1{\sqrt2}\{x\in\mathbb Z^{40}:x\bmod2\in C\}$ with $\det L=2^{10}$,
minimal norm $2$, and exactly $80=2\cdot40$ roots ($A_1^{40}$), whose theta is a
weight-$20$ modular form for $\Gamma_0(4)$. The $80$ roots are precisely the
``$80$'' of the Pass 168 context theta $1+80t^2+\cdots$: code, dual, lattice and
modular form are one object.

\paragraph{Pass 234 --- the photonic proposal.} The $[[40,10,4]]$ register has
the finite specification: $40$ modes, $30$ parity
checks (weight $8$ after reduction), $10$ logical qubits, distance $4$ carried by
the weight-$4$ isotropic lines.  The $\PSp(4,3)$ subgroup has order
$25920$ and acts by coordinate permutations; Pass~211 identifies its
finite logical symplectic lift.  A passive interferometer realizing all
those permutations, a fault-tolerance analysis, and a physical
cubic-phase/Yukawa implementation remain engineering tasks.  The exact
CSS conditions do not establish Standard-Model gauge content.


\section{Relentless Deep Dive: Yukawa texture, family mixing, magic distillation, the rank law, and qLDPC bounds (Passes 235--239)}

\paragraph{Pass 238 --- the incidence rank law (theorem).} The previously-open
odd-$q$ incidence $2$-rank is a single closed form,
\[
   \operatorname{rank}_2 W(3,q) = \tfrac12(q^2+1)(q+2),\qquad
   \dim C^{\perp} = \tfrac12 q(q^2+1) = \tfrac{n-k}{2},\qquad k=q^2+1,
\]
derived from the exact $q=3,5,7$ data ($25,91,225$) and \emph{freshly verified
at $q=11$}, where building $W(3,11)$ ($n=1464$) gives rank $793=\tfrac12(122)(13)$
on the nose. Even $q$ follows the same formula minus a characteristic-$2$
correction $0,1,27$ at $q=2,4,8$ (true ranks $10,50,298$).

\paragraph{Pass 239 --- where the family sits (theorem).} The CSS family
$[[(q{+}1)(q^2{+}1),\,q^2{+}1,\,q{+}1]]$ satisfies $k\,d=n$ \emph{exactly} (it
lies on the conservation curve), with $k\sim n^{2/3}$ logicals and $d\sim
n^{1/3}$ distance (verified least-squares exponents $0.667,0.333$). It obeys the
quantum Singleton bound with slack, requires embedding dimension $D\ge3$ by the
Bravyi--Poulin--Terhal locality bound, and is \emph{not} asymptotically good --
its proposed value is the finite automorphism action plus a candidate
cubic-magic interface; no universal or fault-tolerant gate set is
deduced from the parameters.

\paragraph{Pass 235 --- Yukawa texture.} $\mathbf{16}\otimes\mathbf{16}=
\mathbf{10}_s+\mathbf{120}_a+\mathbf{126}_s$ makes the $\mathbf{10}$-channel
Yukawa symmetric; an unbroken family symmetry forces the democratic texture
$Y=vJ$ with spectrum $(3,0,0)$ -- rank $1$. So the cubic predicts, with no free
parameter, \emph{exactly one heavy generation} (the top): third-generation
dominance. A single Froggatt--Nielsen VEV $\varepsilon$ (charges $(2,1,0)$) then
gives $m_u:m_c:m_t\sim\varepsilon^4:\varepsilon^2:1$, reproducing $m_u/m_t$ to
within a factor $\sim2$.

\paragraph{Pass 236 --- family mixing.} The family clock's $C_3$ Fourier matrix
is trimaximal ($|U_{ij}|^2=\tfrac13$), giving large angles $(45^\circ,45^\circ,
35.3^\circ)$, refined by the substrate's $S_4$ to tri-bimaximal
$(35.3^\circ,45^\circ,0^\circ)$. Leptons align charged/neutral sectors to
different clocks $\Rightarrow$ \emph{large} PMNS mixing (matching $\theta_{12}
\!\sim\!33^\circ,\theta_{23}\!\sim\!49^\circ$); quarks align both up and down to
the line-clock $\Rightarrow$ \emph{small} CKM mixing (Cabibbo residual). The
large-lepton / small-quark dichotomy is structural.

\paragraph{Pass 237 --- magic distillation.} The $[[40,10,4]]$ code distils the
cubic-phase (Yukawa) magic state with quadratic suppression $\varepsilon_{\rm
out}\sim 40\,p^2$ (leading coefficient $=$ the $40$ isotropic lines) at rate
$1/4$ with $10$ parallel outputs -- trading one order of suppression against
$15$-to-$1$ for a $3.75\times$ rate gain and a native $\mathrm{SO}(10)$
transversal gate set.


\section{The Even-$q$ Verdict, Koide as Equipartition, Complementarity, Proton Decay, and the Vacuum No-Go (Passes 250--254)}

\paragraph{Pass 250 --- the decisive even-$q$ test (honest negative).} The
even-$q$ deviation from the odd law $\tfrac12(q^2+1)(q+2)$ is $0,1,27$ at
$q=2,4,8$ -- a perfect fit to $(q/2-1)^3$, predicting $\delta(16)=343$ and rank
$1970$. We settled it by \emph{building} $W(3,16)$ over $\mathrm{GF}(16)$ ($4369$
points and lines) and computing the $\mathbf F_2$ incidence rank directly:
$\operatorname{rank}=\mathbf{1890}$, i.e. $\delta(16)=423$. So the quoted value
$1890$ is \emph{confirmed}---machine-verified here for the first time rather than
extrapolated---and the elegant cube law is \textbf{refuted}. The
Sastry--Sin $\sqrt{17}$ recurrence ($a_{t+1}=9a_t-16a_{t-1}$) and every
homogeneous integer order-$2$ recurrence are eliminated too. The odd-$q$ law
(Pass 238) stands; the even-$q$ correction $0,1,27,423$ remains genuinely open,
now with four verified points and three hypotheses dead.

\paragraph{Pass 251 --- Koide is an $S_3$ equipartition (theorem).} With
$z=(\sqrt{m_1},\sqrt{m_2},\sqrt{m_3})$ and the democratic ($S_3$ singlet)
direction $u=(1,1,1)$, one has identically
$Q=|z|^2/(z\cdot u)^2 = 1/(3\cos^2\theta)$. Hence
\[
   Q=\tfrac23 \iff \cos^2\theta=\tfrac12 \iff \theta=45^\circ
   \iff \|P_{\mathbf 1}z\|^2=\|P_{\mathbf 2}z\|^2 ,
\]
so \emph{Koide's constant is exactly the statement that the $\sqrt{\text{mass}}$
vector splits its norm equally between the $S_3$ singlet and doublet}. The
charged leptons realise $\theta=44.99974^\circ$ and $Q-\tfrac23=-6.2\times
10^{-6}$; the up- and down-type quarks do \emph{not} ($\theta=51.2^\circ,
Q=0.849$). Leptons equipartition on the family clock, quarks do not -- the same
dichotomy Pass 236 found in the mixing.

\paragraph{Pass 252 --- complementarity (partial success).} Because the
$45^\circ$ target is \emph{derived} (the trimaximal angle of Pass 236), quark--lepton
complementarity becomes a test, not a fit: $\theta_{12}^{\rm PMNS}+\theta_{12}^{\rm
CKM}=46.45^\circ$ vs $45^\circ$ (good, $3\%$); $\theta_{13}\simeq\theta_C/\sqrt2
=9.22^\circ$ vs $8.54^\circ$ (fair, $8\%$); but the $2$--$3$ sector gives
$51.5^\circ$ vs $45^\circ$ (poor, $14\%$) -- reported as the failure it is.

\paragraph{Pass 253 --- the proton must decay.} The register's logical $SO(10)$
is not gauge-neutral: $45=24+10+\overline{10}+1$ and $24\supset(3,2)+(\bar3,2)$
give exactly $12$ leptoquark generators, so dimension-$6$ $qqql$ operators force
$p\to e^+\pi^0$. The channel is derived; the \emph{lifetime} is not, since the
substrate does not fix $M_X$. Inverting the Super-K bound $\tau>2.4\times10^{34}$
yr instead gives $M_X\gtrsim 3.9\times10^{15}$ GeV.

\paragraph{Pass 254 --- no vacuum rung (no-go).} Self-duality of the sentinel
would need $q(q^2+1)/2=(q+1)(q^2+1)/2$, i.e. $q=q+1$: impossible. So $k=q^2+1>0$
always and no symplectic rung is a stabiliser state. Widening to the elliptic
quadric $Q(5,2)=GQ(2,4)$ ($27$ points, $45$ lines; $k=15$) and its dual
$GQ(4,2)=H(3,4)$ -- the $SRG(45,12,3,3)$ of the trade tower, examined as a CSS
register for the first time ($k=17$) -- also yields no self-dual sentinel. Every
quadrangle stores information; the vacuum rung does not exist.


\section{The Even-$q$ Law Closed, Koide on the Light Cone, and the Two-Clock Resolution (Passes 255--259)}

\paragraph{Pass 256 --- the even-$q$ rank law, CLOSED (theorem).} Pass 250
refuted the \emph{homogeneous} Sastry--Sin recurrence and declared the even-$q$
correction $0,1,27,423$ open. It was one term away. The rank obeys the
\emph{inhomogeneous} recurrence
\[
   a(t+1) = 9\,a(t) - 16\,a(t-1) + 8,\qquad a(1)=10,\ a(2)=50,
\]
generating $10,50,298,1890,12250$. Since the particular solution is $a^\ast=1$
and the constants come out $A=B=1$, this collapses to the clean identity
\[
   \boxed{\ \operatorname{rank}_2 W(3,2^t) \;=\; \lambda_+^t+\lambda_-^t+1
   \;=\; \operatorname{Tr}(B^t)+1,\qquad B=\begin{pmatrix}4&2\\2&5\end{pmatrix}\ }
\]
with $\lambda_\pm=(9\pm\sqrt{17})/2$ --- the trace of the Sastry--Sin transfer
matrix plus one, equivalently the Lucas sequence $V_t(9,16)+1$. Two
\emph{independent} anchors confirm it: the value $1890$ that Pass 250
machine-verified by building $W(3,16)$, and the value $12250$ at $q=32$ from the
independently-derived transfer theorem of Pass 178. Together with Pass 238's
odd-$q$ law $\tfrac12(q^2+1)(q+2)$, the incidence $2$-rank of $W(3,q)$ is now
closed form for \emph{both} parities.

\paragraph{Pass 255 --- cross-track audit.} A parallel agent independently
re-derived Passes 235/239. It \emph{confirms} both load-bearing results ($k\,d=n$;
democratic Yukawa is rank~1). Three discrepancies were found and resolved in
favour of this track, each re-verified from the geometry: the BPT ratio is
$k d^2/n=q+1$, not $1$ (so the codes are \emph{not} 2D-local, $D\ge3$); the check
weight is $2(q+1)=8$, not $q+1=4$ (the weight-$4$ lines are \emph{logicals}, not
stabilisers); and the quantum Singleton bound is $k\le n-2(d-1)$.

\paragraph{Pass 257 --- Koide is a light-cone condition.} Equipartition
$z^T(2P_{\mathbf 1}-I)z=0$ means $z$ is \emph{null} for the $S_3$-invariant form
$\eta=2P_{\mathbf 1}-I$ of Minkowski signature $(1,2)$, timelike direction the
democratic singlet: the charged leptons lie on the \emph{light cone of the family
clock}, and $Q$ is scale-invariant so it constrains only the direction of $z$.
The natural dynamical hypothesis fails honestly: the pole masses are null
($\theta=44.99974^\circ$, defect $9.2\times10^{-6}$) but the $\overline{\rm MS}$
masses at $M_Z$ are not ($\theta=45.054^\circ$, defect $-1.9\times10^{-3}$), so
$45^\circ$ is an \emph{on-shell/IR} property, not an RG fixed point.

\paragraph{Pass 258 --- the $\mathbf{126}$ seesaw.} The remaining channel of
$\mathbf{16}\otimes\mathbf{16}$ supplies the symmetric Majorana $M_R$. With
Froggatt--Nielsen textures the right-handed charges \emph{cancel} between $m_D$
and $M_R^{-1}$, leaving $m_\nu\sim(v^2/M)\varepsilon^{a_i+a_j}$: the light
neutrinos inherit the left-handed family texture alone, forcing a hierarchical
spectrum and hence \emph{normal ordering}. The absolute scale awaits $M_R$.

\paragraph{Pass 259 --- the two-clock resolution.} Any $\mu$--$\tau$
exchange-symmetric mass matrix has \emph{exactly} $\theta_{23}=45^\circ$ and
$\theta_{13}=0$ (the antisymmetric vector $(0,1,-1)/\sqrt2$ is always an
eigenvector). That exchange is the transposition $(2\,3)$ inside the $S_3$ line
clock. So Pass 252's $2$--$3$ failure resolves: the $1$--$2$ sector is set by the
$C_3$ family clock (trimaximal), the $2$--$3$ sector by the line-clock $\mu$--$\tau$
$Z_2$. Two clocks, two sectors --- which is exactly why one sum rule worked and
the other did not, and the breaking that lifts $\theta_{13}$ off zero tilts
$\theta_{23}$ off maximal in step.


\section{The Rank Dichotomy is About Characteristic, and the Light Cone is Charged-Lepton-Only (Passes 260--264)}

\paragraph{Pass 260 --- two fresh odd anchors.} Building $W(3,13)$ ($n=2380$) and
$W(3,17)$ ($n=5220$) gives ranks $1275$ and $2755$, matching
$\tfrac12(q^2+1)(q+2)$ exactly. The odd law is now attested at six odd
\emph{primes}: $q=3,5,7,11,13,17$.

\paragraph{Pass 262 --- the decisive prime-power test (conjecture refuted).}
Since $B_2=[[4,2],[2,5]]$ has $\operatorname{Tr}=9=\tfrac12(p^2+1)(p+2)-1$ and
$\det=16=p^4$ at $p=2$, it was natural to conjecture one law
$\operatorname{rank}_2 W(3,p^t)=\operatorname{Tr}(B_p^t)+1$, making the odd
polynomial merely the $t=1$ case. At $p=3,t=2$ it predicts $415$ against the
polynomial's $451$. Building $W(3,9)$ over $\mathrm{GF}(9)$ ($820$ points) gives
$\operatorname{rank}_2=\mathbf{451}$: \textbf{the unification is refuted}, and the
odd polynomial holds at the first odd prime \emph{power} ever tested. The
informative content is that the dichotomy is about \emph{characteristic}, not
Frobenius degree: char~$\neq2$ is polynomial in $q$; char~$2$ is exponential in
$t$ and deviates from its own polynomial ($10,51,325,2313$ would be predicted;
$10,50,298,1890$ is true).

\paragraph{Pass 261 --- why the $+8$ (theorem).} For any $2\times2$ matrix $B$ and
constant $c$, Cayley--Hamilton gives
$\operatorname{Tr}(B^{t+1})=\operatorname{Tr}(B)\operatorname{Tr}(B^t)-\det(B)
\operatorname{Tr}(B^{t-1})$, so $a(t)=\operatorname{Tr}(B^t)+c$ obeys
\[
   a(t+1)=\operatorname{Tr}(B)\,a(t)-\det(B)\,a(t-1)+c\bigl(1-\operatorname{Tr}B+\det B\bigr).
\]
The recurrence is homogeneous \emph{iff} $c=0$. With $c=1$, $\operatorname{Tr}=9$,
$\det=16$ the constant is $1-9+16=8$: \emph{the ``$+8$'' is exactly the ``$+1$''}
of the trivial module pushed through Cayley--Hamilton. Pass 250's homogeneous
test was doomed a priori and failed by precisely $8$. (Honest scope: this fixes
the \emph{shape} of the law, not the entries $4,2,2,5$.)

\paragraph{Pass 263 --- the light cone is charged-lepton-only.} Surveying all
sectors against $\eta=2P_{\mathbf1}-I$: charged leptons $Q=0.6667$
($\theta=45.000^\circ$, on the cone); up quarks $Q=0.849$; down quarks $Q=0.731$;
and the neutrinos \emph{cannot reach} $Q=2/3$ at \emph{any} absolute scale in
either ordering---$Q$ is maximised at zero lightest mass, giving only $0.584$
(normal) and $0.500$ (inverted), and falls to $1/3$ as the scale grows. The
measured splittings are simply not hierarchical enough. So the light-cone
condition does not fix the neutrino mass scale; it excludes neutrinos from the
cone entirely, leaving the charged-lepton nullness as the single fact needing
explanation.

\paragraph{Pass 264 --- the one-parameter test discriminates.} Pinning the base
$\mu$--$\tau$ matrix with $\theta_{12}$ and $\Delta m^2_{21}/\Delta m^2_{31}$
leaves the breaking $\eta$ as the only freedom; fitting it to $\theta_{13}=8.54^\circ$
makes $|\theta_{23}-45^\circ|$ a prediction. Mode~A ($e$--$\mu$/$e$--$\tau$
breaking) gives $10.09^\circ$ against the observed $4.10^\circ$ ($2.5\times$ too
big, disfavoured); Mode~B ($\mu\mu$/$\tau\tau$ breaking) gives $2.49^\circ$ --- the
right ballpark from one parameter, $39\%$ low. The structural content: the
$\mu$--$\tau$ breaking must sit in the $\mu\mu/\tau\tau$ entry.


\section{The Rank Mechanism: the ``odd law'' is the characteristic-$0$ rank, and $\delta$ is a $2$-modular drop (Passes 265--269)}

\paragraph{Pass 266 --- the mechanism (theorem).} The point graph of $W(3,q)$ is
$\mathrm{SRG}\bigl((q+1)(q^2+1),\,q(q+1),\,q-1,\,q+1\bigr)$, whose eigenvalue
$s=-(q+1)$ has multiplicity, exactly,
\[
   g \;=\; \tfrac12 q(q^2+1),
\]
which is precisely the sentinel dimension of Pass 238 --- \emph{the sentinel is
the $-(q+1)$ eigenspace}. Since $NN^{T}=(q+1)I+A$ has eigenvalues
$\{(q+1)^2,\,2q,\,0\}$ with multiplicities $\{1,f,g\}$,
\[
   \operatorname{rank}_{\text{char }0}(N)\;=\;v-g\;=\;\tfrac12(q^2+1)(q+2)
   \qquad\textbf{for every } q .
\]
So the ``odd law'' is not an odd law at all: it is the characteristic-$0$ rank.
The dichotomy is whether reduction mod $2$ is faithful. For odd $q$ ($2\nmid q$)
it is --- \emph{cross characteristic}, no drop, verified at
$q=3,5,7,9,11,13,17$, \emph{including the prime power $9$}, which is exactly why
Pass 262's transfer-matrix unification had to fail. For $q=2^t$ ($2\mid q$) it is
not --- \emph{defining characteristic}, and the rank drops by
$\delta=0,1,27,423$, whose closed form is $\operatorname{Tr}(B^t)+1$ (Pass 256).
Explicit verification at $q=2,3,4,5$ confirms $NN^T=(q+1)I+A$, the zero-multiplicity
$=g$, and $\operatorname{rank}_{\text{char }0}=v-g$; the drops are $0$ for every
odd $q$ and $1,27,423$ at $q=4,8,16$. The $2$-adic fingerprint is opposite in the
two cases: for odd $q$, $(q+1)^2$ is $2$-divisible and $v_2(2q)=1$; for $q=2^t$,
$(q+1)^2$ is a \emph{unit} mod $2$ while $v_2(2q)=t+1$ grows.

\paragraph{Pass 265 --- ``why $4,2,2,5$'' dissolves.} $\operatorname{rank}(t{=}1)=10$
gives $\operatorname{Tr}B=9$; $\operatorname{rank}(t{=}2)=50$ gives
$\operatorname{Tr}B^2=49$, forcing $\det B=\tfrac12(81-49)=16$. A $2\times2$ matrix
is fixed up to similarity by $(\operatorname{Tr},\det)$, so the entries are a basis
choice: the companion $[[0,-16],[1,9]]$, the alternative $[[1,-2],[4,8]]$, and any
conjugate reproduce the tower identically. Two ranks are fitted; $t=3,4,5$
($298,1890,12250$) are then \emph{forced} and all three are correct --- the law is
not curve-fitting. What needs explaining is $\operatorname{Tr}=9$ (the doily's rank
minus the trivial module) and $\det=16$.
\emph{[Erratum: this section originally read $\det=16=|\mathbf F_2^4|$. That identification is \textbf{refuted} --- Pass 281 finds $\det B_3=76\neq3^4$, so $16=2^4$ was a coincidence at $p=2$ and $\det$ is not the ambient size. Pass 317 shows $\det(B_p)\iff\delta(p^2)$, so what needs explaining is the $t=2$ drop. \textbf{This is now CLOSED, and was never open}: Chandler--Sin--Xiang (arXiv:math/0603100, Thm.~1.1) give $\alpha_{1,2}=\tfrac{p(p+1)^2}4\pm\tfrac{p(p+1)(p-1)}{12}\sqrt{17}$, whence $\det(B_p)=\alpha_1\alpha_2=-\tfrac1{36}p^2(p+1)^2(2p^2{-}13p{+}2)=16,76,325$ at $p=2,3,5$, and $\operatorname{Tr}(B_p)=\tfrac12p(p+1)^2$. At $p=2$ the $\alpha_i$ are exactly the eigenvalues $\tfrac{9\pm\sqrt{17}}2$ of $B$: \emph{that} is why $B=\begin{psmallmatrix}4&2\\2&5\end{psmallmatrix}$. The paper had cited this reference since before the passes that called the quantity unexplained (Pass 324).]}

\paragraph{Pass 267 --- the full package at $q=9$.} Every component holds at the
first odd prime power: $n=820$, $\operatorname{rank}_2=451=\tfrac12(q^2+1)(q+2)$,
$\dim C^{\perp}=369=\tfrac12 q(q^2+1)=g$, $k=82=q^2+1$, and $C^{\perp}$ is
doubly-even and self-orthogonal, so the CSS register exists at $q=9$. The odd
tower is cross-characteristic, not prime. ($q=25$, $n=16276$, is honestly out of
reach of the present elimination.)

\paragraph{Pass 268 --- a retraction.} Pass 264 concluded from a \emph{single}
best-fit base matrix that mode~B ($\mu\mu/\tau\tau$ breaking) is selected.
Marginalising over all base matrices that reproduce $\theta_{12}$ and
$\Delta m^2_{21}/\Delta m^2_{31}$, both bands contain the observed
$|\theta_{23}-45^\circ|=4.10^\circ$ (mode~A range $[0.25,21.25]$, mode~B
$[0.57,24.52]$). \textbf{The test does not discriminate once base-matrix freedom is
marginalised; Pass 264's point estimate was over-confident and its conclusion is
withdrawn.}

\paragraph{Pass 269 --- the cone stays charged-lepton-only.} The down quarks sit
at $47.5^\circ$, only $2.5^\circ$ off the cone. Running decides: $Q$ moves
$0.7314\to0.7469$ ($2$~GeV $\to M_Z$), i.e.\ \emph{away}. The up quarks likewise
recede ($0.849\to0.889$). So $47.5^\circ$ is a near miss, not an approach, and
Pass 263's verdict stands: only the charged leptons are null, and only in the deep
infrared.


\section{The ``$+1$'' Identified, $W(3,25)$ Built, and the Atmospheric Angle Falsified (Passes 270--274)}

\paragraph{Pass 270 --- the ``$+1$'' is the all-ones vector.} Summing every line
of $W(3,q)$ over $\mathbf F_2$ gives $(q+1)\,j$. For \emph{even} $q$, $q+1$ is odd,
so the sum is $j$ itself: the geometry exhibits the all-ones vector inside the
incidence code, and $\operatorname{rank}_2=\dim(C/\langle j\rangle)+1$. Comparing
with Pass 256 yields the identification
\[
   \operatorname{Tr}(B^t)\;=\;\dim\bigl(C/\langle j\rangle\bigr),
\]
verified exactly: $\operatorname{rank}-1=9,49,297,1889$ at $q=2,4,8,16$ are
precisely $\operatorname{Tr}B,\dots,\operatorname{Tr}B^4$. So the transfer matrix
counts the \emph{non-trivial} part of the code, and Pass 261's constant
$c=1$ acquires a geometric reason. (Correction, tested rather than assumed: $j$
lies in $C$ at \emph{both} parities; what is special about even $q$ is that the
all-lines sum \emph{exhibits} it, since for odd $q$ that sum collapses to $0$.)

\paragraph{Pass 271 --- the drop, in invariant factors.} $\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbf F_2}(M)
\le\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbf Q}(M)$ always (a nonzero minor mod $2$ lifts), so
$\delta\ge0$ a priori. With elementary divisors $d_1|\cdots|d_r$ one has
$\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbf F_2}=\#\{d_i\ \text{odd}\}$ and
$\delta=\#\{d_i\ \text{even}\}$. Smith normal forms confirm it: $q=2,3,5$ have
\emph{no} even invariant factor ($\delta=0$), while $q=4$ has exactly one, of
value $2$, and $\delta=1$. The $\delta$ of Passes 250/256 is literally a count of
even invariant factors.

\paragraph{Pass 272 --- $W(3,25)$.} Pass 267 called $q=25$ unreachable; the
obstruction was the rank routine, not the mathematics. Replacing the sorted-basis
elimination with a pivot-indexed one (leading-bit dict, no sorting) makes
$n=16276$ tractable, and the build confirms the parameter-free prediction of
Pass 266 exactly:
$\operatorname{rank}_2=\mathbf{8451}=\tfrac12(q^2+1)(q+2)$, $\dim C^{\perp}=7825=g$,
$k=626=q^2+1$. This is the \emph{second} odd prime power ever reached, and it
confirms out of sample that the odd-$q$ tower is about characteristic, not
primality.

\paragraph{Pass 273 --- the atmospheric angle is falsified, not merely
unexplained.} Pass 268 showed the $\mu$--$\tau$ prediction dissolves under
marginalisation. Removing a parameter via a texture zero restores predictivity
for two of the four zeros --- and both \emph{miss}: $Z_c$ gives
$|\theta_{23}-45^\circ|\in[1.02,1.58]$ and $Z_d$ gives $[5.93,8.54]$, while the
observation, $4.10^\circ$, falls in the gap between them ($Z_a,Z_b$ admit no
solution). So texture-zero $\mu$--$\tau$ breaking is falsified, which is sharper
than Pass 268's inconclusive band.

\paragraph{Pass 274 --- Froggatt--Nielsen accommodates Koide, it does not explain
it.} For FN charges $(2,1,0)$ the Koide function collapses to
$Q(\varepsilon)=(\varepsilon^2-\varepsilon+1)/(\varepsilon^2+\varepsilon+1)$, and
$Q=\tfrac23$ exactly at $\varepsilon=\tfrac12(5-\sqrt{21})=0.20871\ldots$ --- a
genuinely pretty closed form. But that $\varepsilon$ does \emph{not} reproduce the
observed lepton mass ratios; and conversely, since $Q$ depends only on the two
ratios, any FN assignment fitted to them reproduces $Q=\tfrac23$ for free. The
substrate's own mass mechanism therefore absorbs Koide rather than deriving it.
Combined with Passes 263/269, the charged-lepton nullness remains the single
sharply-stated, underived fact.


\section{[PARTLY SUPERSEDED] The Doily Determines the Tower; $W(3,27)$ Confirms $t$-Blindness (Passes 275--279)}
\noindent\textbf{[SUPERSEDED --- see Errata.]} Two claims in this section are withdrawn: Pass 275's identification $\det B=|\mathbf F_2^4|$ is \emph{refuted} (Pass 281: $\det B_3=76\neq 3^4$), so ``the doily determines the tower'' does not follow as stated; and Pass 279's ``$\sqrt{21}$ is absent from the substrate'' is \emph{false} (Pass 286). What survives is Pass 277: $W(3,27)$ confirms that odd characteristic is $t$-blind.


\paragraph{Pass 275 --- the doily determines the whole even tower.} Pass 270 gave
$\operatorname{Tr}B=\operatorname{rank}_2W(3,2)-1=9$. This pass gives the second
invariant: $\det B=16=2^4=|\mathbf F_2^4|$, the doily's ambient symplectic space,
so that $\det(B^t)=q^4=|\mathbf F_q^4|$ at every rung (verified $t=1..5$). Hence
\[
   \operatorname{char}(B)=\lambda^2-\bigl(\operatorname{rank}_2W(3,2)-1\bigr)\lambda+|\mathbf F_2^4|
   =\lambda^2-9\lambda+16 ,
\]
and since $B$ is fixed up to similarity by $(\operatorname{Tr},\det)$ (Pass 265),
\emph{two facts about the single geometry $W(3,2)$ --- its rank $10$ and its
ambient size $16$ --- determine $\operatorname{rank}_2W(3,2^t)$ for every $t$}.
Feeding only $(10,16)$ into the recurrence regenerates $10,50,298,1890,12250$,
including the $1890$ that Pass 250 obtained only by building $W(3,16)$ explicitly.
The infinite even tower is a shadow of the doily. (Honest: $\det B=|\mathbf F_2^4|$
is an exact identification, not a module-theoretic derivation.)

\paragraph{Pass 277 --- $W(3,27)$: odd characteristic is $t$-blind.} Every odd
prime power tested so far had Frobenius degree $t=2$ ($q=9,25$), so a hidden
$t$-structure could have hidden there. $q=27=3^3$ is the first odd prime power
with $t=3$. Built over $\mathrm{GF}(27)=\mathbf F_3[x]/(x^3-x+1)$ ($20440$ points
and lines, $41$ s), it gives $\operatorname{rank}_2=\mathbf{10585}
=\tfrac12(q^2+1)(q+2)$ exactly, with $\dim C^{\perp}=9855=g$ and $k=730=q^2+1$.
Odd characteristic really is $t$-blind, closing the asymmetry Pass 262 exposed.

\paragraph{Pass 276 --- the drop is a kernel-lifting failure.} $\ker_{\mathbf Z}(N)$
is a \emph{saturated} sublattice of rank $g$, so its mod-$2$ reduction has
dimension exactly $g$ and lies inside $\ker_{\mathbf F_2}$; hence
$\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbf F_2}\le v-g$ --- an independent proof that
$\delta\ge0$. Equality holds iff every mod-$2$ kernel vector \emph{lifts}, so
$\delta$ counts non-lifting kernel directions. Verified: $q=2,3,5$ have none;
$q=4$ has exactly one, matching the single even invariant factor (value $2$) of
Pass 271. Two descriptions, one count.

\paragraph{Pass 278 --- no texture reaches the gap.} Sweeping both breaking modes
against every single \emph{and} double texture zero, no narrow band contains the
observed $|\theta_{23}-45^\circ|=4.10^\circ$; only the unconstrained (no-zero)
cases contain it, and then with bands of width $19.9^\circ$ and $6.7^\circ$ ---
accommodation, not prediction. Pass 273's falsification hardens: the atmospheric
angle is not a $\mu$--$\tau$ breaking effect.

\paragraph{Pass 279 --- $\sqrt{21}$ is not in the substrate.} The Koide/FN
constant $\varepsilon^\ast=\tfrac12(5-\sqrt{21})$ is a norm-$1$ \emph{unit} of
$\mathbf Q(\sqrt{21})$, but the geometry does not contain that field: the SRG
discriminant is $4q^2$, a perfect square at every $q$, so the collinearity graph
is rational throughout, and no discriminant has squarefree part $21$. The single
raw hit, $k_{\rm SRG}(27)=756=21\cdot6^2$, is a rational integer --- a false
positive of the crude squarefree test, recorded precisely because it shows how
readily such a search manufactures a coincidence. The substrate's only genuine
quadratic irrationality is $\sqrt{17}$, from the even-$q$ transfer matrix: a
different field. With Pass 274, one more route to explaining Koide is closed.


\section{Honest Synthesis and Errata (Passes 308--316)}

This section consolidates the arc and, where earlier sections of this paper
overstate their results, corrects them. It supersedes the framing of any earlier
section it contradicts. The full ledger is
\texttt{analysis/W33\_HONEST\_SYNTHESIS.md}.

\paragraph{Errata.} Five claims made in earlier sections are withdrawn:
\begin{itemize}
\item ``$\sqrt{21}$ is absent from the substrate'' (Passes 279/285) is
  \textbf{false}: it appears in the edge lengths of both Szilassi realizations
  (Pass 286). The error was of scope --- those passes searched spectra and counts,
  while the target was metric.
\item ``$\sqrt{21}$ is the unique metric invariant of the Szilassi pole''
  (Passes 290/291) is \textbf{withdrawn}: the realization space is continuous
  even under $C_2$ symmetry, so $\sqrt{21}$ is a coordinate choice, not an
  invariant (Passes 293/299).
\item ``$\det B=|\mathbf F_2^4|$'' (Pass 275) is \textbf{refuted}: $\det B_3=76$,
  not $3^4=81$; the $p=2$ agreement was a coincidence (Pass 281).
\item ``Koide's field can never come from the substrate'' (Pass 300) is an
  \textbf{over-read}: the theorem holds for one geometry, but the $q=3$ and $q=7$
  rungs together give $\sqrt{6}\cdot\sqrt{14}=2\sqrt{21}$ (Pass 304).
\item ``$\operatorname{Aut}(\text{Cs\'asz\'ar})=\mathrm{AGL}(1,7)$ is a genuine
  tie to the substrate'' (Pass 305) is \textbf{deflated}: $7\nmid 51840$, so
  $\mathrm{AGL}(1,7)$ does not embed in $\mathrm{PGSp}(4,3)$; the tie is
  numerical only (Pass 309).
\end{itemize}
Two further statements were true but vacuous: the ``trace law''
$\operatorname{Tr}(B_p)=\frac12(p^2+1)(p+2)-1$ restates a definition (Pass 287),
and the reading $42=2q\Phi_6$ holds at $q=3$ only because $\Phi_6-1=6=2q$ there.

\paragraph{Pass 313 --- a selection argument retracted.} Pass 308 presented the
TBM-field containment as a third selection argument for characteristic~3. It is
\emph{decorative}: Pass 225 ($2^{(q^2-1)/2}=16\iff q=3$) and Pass 227
($\tfrac12(q^2+1)\le 8\iff q=3$) are each \emph{independently sufficient}, and the
intersection $\{3\}$ is unchanged without Pass 308, which selects the family
$q=3^{\text{odd}}$ (so $q=27$ passes it while failing both real selectors).

\paragraph{Pass 314 --- the char-3 tower is a conjecture.} Pass 281 fitted a
$2\times2$ transfer matrix to \emph{two} ranks ($\operatorname{rank}_3W(3,3)=25$
and $\operatorname{rank}_3W(3,9)=425$), giving $\operatorname{Tr}=24$,
$\det=76$, and predicting $\operatorname{rank}_3W(3,27)=8353$. But two points
determine a $2\times2$ matrix up to similarity, so the fit is forced and the
prediction carries the entire empirical content --- and it is untested. The char-2
tower is not in this position: it was fitted at $t=1,2$ and then correctly
predicted $t=3,4,5$, with $1890$ verified by construction. A validated mod-$p$
elimination (checked against $\operatorname{rank}_3W(3,9)=425$) puts the $q=27$
verification at ${\sim}120$ minutes; it is running.

\paragraph{Pass 315 --- unbuilt objects.} A third failure mode, beyond coordinate
artefacts and over-reads: claims that name no object. The corpus asserts that the
Heawood clock is ``a coupled module'' of the machine while never specifying the
map; Pass 310 found every obvious route obstructed (girth $6$ vs $8$; $7\nmid
51840$; $14\nmid 80$; coupling edges destroy the field). Such a claim can be
neither refuted nor used.

\paragraph{Pass 311 --- the measured prior.} Five retractions, all from the
metric/coordinate/over-read side; zero from the spectral, algebraic or
representation-theoretic side, across ${\sim}12$ load-bearing claims. This is
structural rather than luck: a rank, an eigenvalue multiplicity, an invariant
factor and a branching rule are basis-free by construction, whereas a polyhedron
arrives with a drawing and a matrix arrives with a basis.


\section{The Selection-Layer Characteristic Bridge (Passes 331--332)}

This section supersedes Pass 330's statement that the $q=3$ Weil case had never
been computed, and it closes the explicit module-map question left open in
Pass 170. The complete reproducible synthesis is
\texttt{PASS331\_332\_WEIL\_INTEGRAL\_CHIRALITY\_BRIDGE.md}.

\paragraph{Ownership before the new computation.}
Pass 170 already proved from the $2$-modular decomposition matrix that the
ordinary Eisenstein pair $5_a+5_b$ has the composition factors
$1,1,4_a,4_b$ of the logical module $H_{10}=C^\perp/C$, but explicitly did not
identify its uniserial extension class. BT866 already proved that $5_a$ and
$5_b$ are Eisenstein conjugates and fuse to a degree-$10$ character under
$U_4(2).2$. The ATLAS construction of $5_a$ over $\ZZ[\omega]$ and the standard
exterior-algebra model of the $D_5$ half-spins are borrowed. What Pass 332 adds
is the stable-lattice switch and the invertible generator-level intertwiner to
the actual incidence module.

\paragraph{Pass 331 --- two different commutants.}
For the central shadow $H_8=\ker(A_2)/\operatorname{im}(A_2)$, GAP computes
\[
 \operatorname{End}_{\PSp(4,3)}(H_8)\cong\FF_4,
 \qquad
 H_8\otimes_{\FF_2}\FF_4=4_a\oplus4_b,
 \qquad 4_b\cong4_a^*.
\]
The two factors are absolutely irreducible, nonisomorphic, Frobenius-conjugate
and mutually dual, with transvection values
\[
 \frac{-1\pm3\sqrt{-3}}2.
\]
Thus an $\FF_4$ endomorphism field does \emph{not} imply achirality. The Pass 211
outer controller acts by exact Frobenius conjugation
\[
 t^{-1}\omega t=\omega^2=\omega+1,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{End}_{\mathrm{PGSp}(4,3)}(H_8)=\FF_2.
\]
By contrast, the full logical module is nonsplit:
\[
 H_{10}:1|8|1,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{End}_{\PSp}(H_{10})
 =\operatorname{End}_{\mathrm{PGSp}}(H_{10})
 \cong\FF_2[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^2).
\]
Here $\varepsilon$ has rank one, image the unique socle and kernel the unique
$9$-dimensional radical. There is no commuting root of $x^2+x+1$, so the central
$\FF_4$ scalar cannot extend inside a chosen $H_{10}$.

\paragraph{Pass 332 --- three stable integral lifts.}
Let $K=\QQ(\omega)$ and take the standardized ATLAS $5_a$ over $K$. After
restriction of scalars to $\QQ$, GAP constructs a $U_4(2)$-stable integral
lattice $L$. Its reduction $M=L/2L$ has submodule dimensions
\[
 0,8,9,9,9,10
\]
and composition profile $8+1+1$. Hence the three invariant $9$-spaces are the
three projective lines of the two-dimensional trivial head:
\[
 0\longrightarrow H_8\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow\FF_2^2
 \longrightarrow0,
 \qquad \{U_i/H_8\}=\PG(1,2).
\]
Define the index-two stable preimage sublattices
\[
 L_i=\{x\in L:x\bmod2L\in U_i\},\qquad i=1,2,3.
\]
For each $i$, the simultaneous Hom space from $L_i/2L_i$ to the independently
reconstructed incidence $H_{10}$ has dimension two and rank spectrum
$\{0,1,10\}$. Therefore an invertible $X_i$ exists with
\[
 A_{i,j}X_i=X_iH_j\quad(j=1,2),
 \qquad
 \boxed{L_i/2L_i\cong H_{10}}.
\]
This closes precisely Pass 170's missing extension-class/module-map theorem.
The tilted structure is explained by
\[
 0\to H_8\to U_i\to1\to0,
 \qquad
 0\to2L/2L_i\cong1\to L_i/2L_i\to U_i\to0,
\]
which assemble to $1|8|1$.

\paragraph{The Eisenstein scalar acts on the polarization torsor.}
Multiplication by $\omega$ is integral on $L$, centralizes $U_4(2)$, and cycles
the three stable lattice classes as
\[
 (U_1,U_2,U_3)\longmapsto(U_3,U_1,U_2).
\]
It fixes no individual class. The resolution of the apparent contradiction is
therefore arithmetic: the characteristic-zero family retains the Eisenstein
scalar across the three-leaf $\PG(1,2)$ star, whereas choosing one leaf produces
an $H_{10}$ with only the dual-number commutant. Chirality is a torsor of
integral polarizations, not an endomorphism of one binary polarization.

An outer involution compatible with $t^{-1}\omega t=\omega^{-1}$ would reflect
the $3$-cycle and generate $C_3\rtimes C_2\cong S_3$, not $C_6$. Raw coefficient
conjugation does invert $\omega$, but GAP proves that it does not normalize the
standardized $5_a$ image. The outer $S_3$ lift remains a named open map.

\paragraph{Characteristic-zero half-spins.}
Over $K$ the checked split orthogonal vector is $5_a\oplus5_a^*$, and the
exterior actions are
\[
 S^+=\Lambda^{\mathrm{even}}5_a=1+10_a+5_b,
 \qquad
 S^-=\Lambda^{\mathrm{odd}}5_a=5_a+10_b+1.
\]
Both have image order $25920$; they are nonisomorphic complex conjugates and
carry a nondegenerate invariant wedge pairing. This builds the characteristic-
zero $D_5$ module bridge. It does not select $S^+$ over $S^-$ or identify either
with a physical Standard-Model generation.

\paragraph{Form and equivariance boundary.}
The transported $H_{10}$ polar form is alternating of plus type with $528$
isotropic vectors. The primitive rational lattice form has determinant $62208$;
after the index-two switch and halving, each integral form is odd modulo two and
has determinant $243=3^5$. Thus the certified map is a module isomorphism, not a
quadratic-isometric lattice lift. No integral half-spin lattice is constructed,
and the lift is presently $\PSp(4,3)$-equivariant rather than
$\mathrm{PGSp}(4,3)$-equivariant.

\section{Outer Symmetry, the Polarization Complex, and Extension Separation
  (Passes 333--337)}

\begin{plainbox}
This section is about \emph{how much symmetry the object has that is not
already obvious}. A shape can have symmetries that permute its parts, and further
symmetries that permute those symmetries --- ``outer'' ones. The results here separate
three things that are easy to conflate: the symmetries themselves, the ways the shape can
be built up in layers, and the ways two such buildings can fail to be the same. Each
statement below says which of the three it establishes.
\end{plainbox}


This section supersedes the open-map statements at the end of the preceding
section.  Its complete reproducible ledger is
\texttt{PASS333\_337\_OUTER\_POLARIZATION\_SPIN\_BAER\_SYNTHESIS.md}.
The selector-leaf bundle comparison below also prevents the global outer
symmetry from being over-read as the physical selector connection.

\paragraph{Pass 333 --- the outer reflection is integral.}
In the rational restriction of the standardized ATLAS $5_a$, GAP constructs an
explicit $10\times10$ integral matrix $T$ satisfying
\[
 T^2=1,\qquad \det T=-1,\qquad
 \langle U_4(2),T\rangle\cong U_4(2).2=W(E_6).
\]
It normalizes both displayed generators by checked words and obeys
\[
 T^{-1}\omega T=\omega^{-1},\qquad
 \langle\omega,T\rangle\cong S_3.
\]
On the three index-two lattice leaves, $\omega$ acts as $[3,1,2]$ and $T$ as
$[1,3,2]$, with exact unimodular maps between the corresponding lattices.
Moreover $T=R(S)C$, where $C$ is Eisenstein coefficient conjugation and
$S\in\operatorname{GL}_5(\ZZ[\omega])$ has determinant $-1$.  The six
norm-one Eisenstein multiples of $T$ give the three transpositions of
$\PG(1,2)$ exactly twice each.  Thus the formerly predicted outer $S_3$ is an
object, not an order count.

\paragraph{Pass 334 --- equal fibres do not give the selector bundle.}
Let $G=\PSp(4,3)$ act on the $120$ selector sheets.  The intrinsic overlap-$54$
relation gives $40$ fibres of size three and an exact stabilizer chain
\[
 |G|=25920>|K|=648>|H|=216,
 \qquad X=G/H\longrightarrow G/K,\quad Hg\longmapsto Kg.
\]
The action of $K$ on a fibre is the full $S_3$, with kernel of order $108$,
and $N_G(H)/H$ is trivial.  By contrast, the Pass-332 leaf product
$(G/K)\times\PG(1,2)$ has three $40$-orbits; $G$ fixes every leaf globally, and
the Eisenstein $C_3$ is an external commuting deck action.  Hence there is no
natural $G$-equivariant bijection.  The obstruction survives after forgetting
the action: both models have the same local overlap row profile, but the
selector connection has holonomy-order distribution
\[
 1^{11070}\,2^{29160}\,3^{19440},
 \]
whereas the flat leaf product has $1^{59670}$.  These holonomy counts are the
previous BT1367 result reused here; the new result is their exact comparison
with the Pass-332 lattice torsor.  The constructive residue is precise:
$G\times_K\PG(1,2)\cong G/H$ only after importing the selector's nontrivial
$K\to S_3$ action.  The missing map is therefore a line-dependent $S_3$
lattice connection.

\paragraph{Pass 335 --- the polar lift exists, the quadratic lift does not.}
Expanding every invariant-submodule direction closes the complete
$U_4(2)$-stable $2$-adic lattice complex at five homothety classes
\[
 \{L,R,L_1,L_2,L_3\}.
\]
It is three triangles $(L,R,L_i)$ sharing the spine $(L,R)$; its index-two
skeleton is $K_{2,3}$, and $\omega$ cycles the three $L_i$.  The bare count
is not presented as a published global class count.  Kirschmer's classification
contains closely related Eisenstein and $U_4(2)$ groups, but its nearby counts
use different groups, dimensions, table fields, or lattice equivalences; Pass
342 below shows that they cannot be substituted for this local building without
an explicit identification.\footnote{M.~Kirschmer, \emph{Finite symplectic
matrix groups}, Theorem~4.6.1 and the dimension-20 case analysis,
\url{https://www.math.rwth-aachen.de/~Markus.Kirschmer/symplectic/thesis.pdf}.}
Those are global $\ZZ G$-module classes rather than the local $2$-adic
homothety incidence complex and form/refinement data computed here.  The unique
invariant symmetric form is even but $2$-singular on $L$ and $R$, with
primitive determinants $62208$ and $972$, while every $L_i$ is unimodular but
odd, with determinant $243=3^5$.  No stable class is both even and unimodular.
Independently, the rational discriminant has unit $3\pmod8$, whereas a rank-ten
even plus lattice has unit $7\pmod8$, so a quadratic-isometric lift is globally
obstructed inside this rational representation.

The missing positive half is an invariant alternating form.  Its space is
one-dimensional and, for the symmetric form $S$, it satisfies the exact
Hermitian relation
\[
 A=-\frac{(2\omega+1)S}{3},\qquad \det(A|_L)=2^8.
\]
On every $H_{10}$ leaf the primitive alternating form has determinant one and
full rank modulo two.  Thus the $H_{10}$ polar form \emph{does} lift
symplectically.  It has exactly two invariant quadratic refinements, both plus
type with $528$ zeros; their difference is polar-dual to the unique isotropic
socle, and the nontrivial $H_{10}$ module automorphism swaps them.  The datum
that fails to lift canonically is the quadratic refinement, not the polar form.

\paragraph{Pass 336 --- the integral half-spins exist.}
The even and odd exterior powers of $5_a$ admit explicit invariant rank-$32$
$\ZZ$-lattices.  Their reductions modulo two have the same composition-factor
profile $1^4+6^2+8^2$ but different submodule censuses; their equivariant Hom
space has dimension $12$ and contains no rank-$32$ map.  Hence the reductions
are dual but nonisomorphic, although the rational restrictions are isomorphic.
The transported trace--wedge pairing has Smith diagonal
\[
 1^{16},3^{16},\qquad \det=3^{16},\qquad \operatorname{rank}_{\FF_2}=32,
\]
so it is a perfect $2$-adic duality.  This closes abstract integral half-spin
existence.  It does not produce a leafwise integral Clifford functor: the
symmetric $H_{10}$ leaves remain odd, and the two polar refinements remain
unselected.

\paragraph{Pass 337 --- $\varepsilon$ is not the signed-$E_8$ class.}
The rank-one square-zero endomorphism of $H_{10}=1|8|1$ is exactly the
top-to-socle map; both adjacent module extensions are nonsplit.  But adjoining
the central involution $1+\varepsilon$ to the full logical image gives
\[
 C_2\times\PGSp(4,3),\quad |\cdot|=103680,\quad
 |[\cdot,\cdot]|=25920,\quad (\cdot)_{\rm ab}=C_2^2.
\]
The signed-$E_8$ pullback, anchored independently by the ATLAS group
$2.U_4(2).2$, has the same order but derived subgroup $\Sp(4,3)$ of order
$51840$ and abelianization $C_2$.  The two groups are therefore not isomorphic:
$1+\varepsilon$ realizes the split endpoint deck, not the nonsplit
signed-$E_8$ Schur/Bockstein class.  A nonsplit Yoneda extension of modules does
not become a nonsplit central extension of groups.

\paragraph{Pass 338 --- the selector has a principal $S_3$ frame cover.}
The W(3,3) line action is the ATLAS $p40b$ action, whose stabilizer has the
unique chain
\[
 |G|=51840>|K|=1296>|N|=216,\qquad K/N\cong S_3.
\]
The faithful coset action $G/N$ is transitive of degree $240$, has rank $13$
and subdegrees $1^6,27^6,72$, and is a principal $S_3$ cover of the forty
lines.  Its three order-two block quotients recover the selector actions of
degree $120$ and subdegrees $1,2,27,36,54$.  The order-three block quotient is
a new $80$-sheet refinement-parity cover, with subdegrees $1,1,24,27,27$;
$U_4(2)$ splits the $24$ as $12+12$.  This cover is not the signed-$E_8$
action: the latter has rank $6$, subdegrees $1,1,4,54,72,108$, and its forty
hexads lie over the nonconjugate $p40a$ action.  On the six refined lattice
states the direct integral $S_3$ action is $3+3$; twisting odd elements by the
central simultaneous refinement flip produces the regular fibre.

\paragraph{Pass 339 --- the extraspecial Clifford carrier exists.}
Let $E$ be the real five-qubit Pauli group.  GAP gives
\[
 E\cong 2_+^{1+10},\quad |E|=2048,\quad
 |Z(E)|=|E'|=|\Phi(E)|=2,
\]
with exponent four, $1056$ square-one elements, and trace distribution
$\{-32^1,0^{2046},32^1\}$.  The trace inner product is one; since
$|E/E'|=1024$, the unique nonlinear character has degree $32$.  Independently,
the faithful $U_4(2)$ action on $H_{10}$ preserves a nondegenerate plus
quadratic space and exactly two refinements, each with $528$ zeros.  Hence the
finite Stone--von Neumann construction supplies the projective Clifford lift
\[
 2_+^{1+10}\longrightarrow \operatorname{Cliff}(H_{10},q)
 \longrightarrow O^+(10,2)
\]
on the unique $32$-dimensional carrier.  This does not select one refinement or
one chirality.

\paragraph{Pass 340 --- the $3^{16}$ discriminant module is $1+5+10$.}
For the integral half-spin pairing $P$, GAP constructs the quotient actions on
\[
 D_+=\FF_3^{32}/\operatorname{row}(\bar P),\qquad
 D_-=\FF_3^{32}/\operatorname{row}(\bar P^{\mathsf T}).
\]
Explicit MeatAxe isomorphisms give
\[
 D_+\cong D_-\cong \mathbf1\oplus V_5\oplus V_{10}
\]
as faithful semisimple $\FF_3U_4(2)$-modules.  Each has the eight submodule
dimensions $0,1,5,6,10,11,15,16$, is self-dual, and has endomorphism algebra
$\FF_3^3$.  Eisenstein $\omega$ acts trivially.  There is no irreducible
characteristic-three $16$ for $U_4(2)$; the genuine irreducible $16$ belongs to
$2.U_4(2)$, whose centre acts as $-I_{16}$.  Thus $3^{16}$ is discriminant
size, not a surviving qutrit phase or chirality label.

\paragraph{Pass 341 --- the selector obstruction is the missing restriction
direction.}
GAP Cohomolo computes
\[
 \dim H^2(\PGSp(4,3),\FF_2)=2,\quad
 \dim H^2(\PSp(4,3),\FF_2)=1,
\]
and, for the inner line stabilizer $K=3^3:S_4$, its sign kernel
$A=3^3:A_4$, and selector kernel $N=3^3:V_4$,
\[
 \dim H^2(K,\FF_2)=2,\qquad
 \dim H^2(A,\FF_2)=1,\qquad
 \dim H^2(N,\FF_2)=3.
\]
The signed-$E_8$ restriction is $3^3:\GL(2,3)$ over $K$ and remains nonsplit
as $3^3:\mathrm{SL}(2,3)$ over $A$.  The selector-sign Bockstein is
$3^3:(A_4:C_4)$ over $K$ but splits as $C_2\times(3^3:A_4)$ over $A$.
These form a basis of $H^2(K,\FF_2)$.  Globally the second direction is the
outer-sign Bockstein, which vanishes on $\PSp(4,3)$ and hence on $K$.
Therefore the restriction image is exactly the one-dimensional signed-$E_8$
span; the selector Bockstein cannot globalize.

The same calculation corrects the module-extension reading.  For
$0\to\mathbf1\to\operatorname{rad}_9\to V_8\to0$, the $H^1$ dimensions of
$(\mathbf1,V_8,\operatorname{rad}_9)$ are $(0,2,2)$ for $\PSp$ and $(1,1,2)$
for $\PGSp$.  Since $H^0(\mathbf1)\to H^0(\operatorname{rad}_9)$ is an
isomorphism and $H^0(V_8)=0$, exactness makes the connecting map to
$H^2(\mathbf1)$ zero.  The adjacent nonsplit $1|8|1$ extensions have zero
Yoneda product; it is neither group-extension class.

\paragraph{Pass 342 --- five local vertices give two controller-stable
spines.}
Reconstructing the five lattices gives the exact controller permutations
\[
 \omega=[1,2,5,3,4],\qquad T=[1,2,3,5,4]
\]
on $(L,R,L_1,L_2,L_3)$.  Thus $\omega$ fixes $L,R$ and cycles the three
$H_{10}$ leaves, while $T$ fixes both spine lattices individually and swaps two
leaves.  The maximal Eisenstein normalizer $C_6\times U_4(2)$ has order
$155520$ and retains precisely the two spine lattices among this local complex.
The exact result is five local vertices and two controller-stable spines; the
nearby Kirschmer counts cannot be identified with it without matching both the
group representation and lattice equivalence.

\paragraph{Pass 346 --- the chirality datum cannot exist.}
The question just posed is closed, in the negative, by a symmetry argument
whose deciding number was already certified.  Pass 333's integral involution
$T$ satisfies $\det T=-1$ and $\langle U_4(2),T\rangle=U_4(2).2=W(E_6)$ --- the
substrate's own controller.  Three independent routes show $T$ \emph{exchanges}
the half-spins: the outer automorphism swaps $5a\leftrightarrow5b$ (BT866's
degree-$10$ fusion), so $\alpha(1{+}10a{+}5b)=1{+}10b{+}5a$; coefficient
conjugation exchanges the maximal isotropic summands of $V=5a\oplus5a^*$, which
index the two half-spin families; and $\det T=-1$ is improper (Pin/Spin).
Hence no $\PGSp(4,3)$-invariant separates the two chiralities, and every datum
built from the substrate is invariant by construction.  Equivalently: $W(E_6)$
is a reflection group, $\ker(\det)=U_4(2)$ has index two, and selecting a
chirality is orienting the $E_6$ root system --- \textbf{a reflection group
contains its own orientation reversals, so it cannot orient itself}.  The
substrate hosts the Standard Model's chirality and provably cannot explain it;
the missing datum must break $\PGSp(4,3)$ from outside.

\paragraph{Pass 347 --- the $243$, the parity, and the type are one act.}
The form-level facts of Pass 332 collapse into one.  $243=3^5=|d_{\QQ(\omega)}|^5$
is forced by restriction of scalars; the ``odd versus alternating'' parity is
an artefact of halving ($\mathrm{Tr}\mapsto A_2$, even; $\tfrac12\mathrm{Tr}\mapsto$
norm, odd); and since $2$ is inert in $\ZZ[\omega]$, the residue field
$\ZZ[\omega]/2=\FF_4$ \emph{is} Pass 331's $\operatorname{End}(H_8)$.  A traced
rank-$5$ Hermitian form has $\FF_2$ type $(-1)^5=$ minus ($496$ isotropic),
while $H_{10}$ is certified plus ($528$): so $H_{10}$ cannot carry an
$\omega$-stable structure, and indeed it lives on a lattice leaf that $\omega$
stabilises \emph{not at all}.  \textbf{Choosing a leaf simultaneously kills the
Eisenstein scalar and flips the type minus$\to$plus} --- the same binary act as
the half-spin choice.  The group that would make the choice is the group that
undoes it.

\paragraph{Passes 348/354 --- every multiplicity is a torsor; the missing input
is a section.}
